Then the Venerable Ānanda said this to those elders: "Venerable ones, it will be the irreversible bodhisattvas, the great beings, or persons whose view is perfected, or the arhats in whom all defilements are exhausted — it is they who will come to properly hold this teaching of the Perfection of Wisdom as it has been set forth here."

Then the Venerable Subhūti said this to those elders: "Venerable ones, no one whatsoever will come to properly hold this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been thus expounded. Why is that? Because in this teaching not a single phenomenon has been spoken of, not a single phenomenon has been made to appear, and not a single phenomenon has been posited. Therefore, just as not a single phenomenon has been spoken of, not a single phenomenon has been made to appear, and not a single phenomenon has been posited, so in exactly that way no one whatsoever will come to properly hold this teaching of the Perfection of Wisdom as it has been set forth here."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra), thought to himself: "As the noble Subhūti has thus taught this category of Dharma, I too shall, for the sake of making offering, miraculously conjure flowers and scatter them before the noble Subhūti." And so Śakra, lord of the gods, at that moment miraculously conjured flowers and scattered them before the Venerable Subhūti.

Then the Elder Venerable Subhūti, wishing to commend Śakra, lord of the gods, thought to himself: "These flowers that Śakra, lord of the gods, has scattered — such as these I have not seen even in the realm of the Thirty-Three Gods. These flowers are conjured."

"These flowers that have been miraculously scattered by Śakra, lord of the gods, are not born from trees, creepers, or climbing plants — they are flowers born from the mind." With that thought in his heart, Śakra, lord of the gods, read the intention within the mind of the Venerable Subhūti, and spoke to the Venerable Subhūti these words: "Noble Subhūti, these flowers are unborn. And why? Because there are no flowers born from the mind, nor are they born from trees, creepers, or climbing plants."

Then the Venerable Subhūti spoke to Śakra, lord of the gods, these words: "Kauśika, you have said that these flowers are unborn — that they are born neither from the mind, nor from trees, creepers, nor climbing plants. Kauśika, that which is unborn is not a flower."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, thought to himself: "How wonderful! The noble Subhūti's wisdom is profound. He teaches even what is merely designated by name and word, yet he does not contradict it either. He illuminates suchness itself and directly reveals suchness itself."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, spoke to the Venerable Subhūti these words: "Noble Subhūti, it is so, it is indeed so. Just as the noble Subhūti has here taught, so too should the bodhisattvas, the great beings, train in this."

When this was said, the Venerable Subhūti spoke to Śakra, lord of the gods, these words: "Kauśika, it is so, it is indeed so. In this way should the bodhisattvas, the great beings, train. Kauśika, when a bodhisattva, a great being, trains in this way, he does not train toward the fruit of stream-entry. He does not train toward the fruit of once-returning. He does not train toward the fruit of non-returning. He does not train toward arhathood. He does not train toward pratyekabuddhahood. He does not train toward buddhahood — for whoever does not train in these grounds, that one trains in buddhahood and omniscience itself. And whoever trains in buddhahood and omniscience itself trains in the immeasurable and incalculable qualities of a buddha. And whoever trains in the immeasurable and incalculable qualities of a buddha does not train for the sake of increasing or diminishing form. Likewise, that one does not train for the sake of increasing or diminishing feeling, perception, formations, or consciousness. Whoever does not train for the sake of increasing or diminishing form, and whoever does not train for the sake of increasing or diminishing feeling, perception, formations, or consciousness — such a one does not train for the sake of fully grasping or abandoning form. Likewise, that one does not train for the sake of fully grasping or abandoning feeling, perception, formations, or consciousness. One does not train for the sake of fully grasping any phenomenon whatsoever. One does not train for the sake of arising, and one does not train for the sake of ceasing. Whoever does not train for the sake of fully grasping any phenomenon, and does not train for the sake of arising or ceasing — that one also does not train for the sake of fully grasping omniscience itself, and does not train for the sake of its arising or ceasing. A bodhisattva, a great being, training in this way, trains in omniscience itself and shall assuredly emerge into omniscience itself."

Then the venerable Śāriputra said this to the venerable Subhūti: "Venerable Subhūti, if a bodhisattva, a great being, does not train for the sake of fully grasping any phenomenon whatsoever, and does not train for the sake of arising, and does not train for the sake of ceasing — then does that one train in omniscience itself, and will that one assuredly emerge into omniscience itself?"

The venerable Subhūti replied: "Venerable Śāriputra, it is exactly so, it is exactly so. Venerable Śāriputra, a bodhisattva, a great being, who does not train for the sake of fully grasping any phenomenon whatsoever, and does not train for the sake of arising, and does not train for the sake of ceasing — that one also does not train for the sake of fully grasping omniscience itself, and does not train for the sake of its arising or ceasing; and that one likewise does not train for the sake of fully grasping the qualities of a buddha, and does not train for the sake of their arising or ceasing. Venerable Śāriputra, a bodhisattva, a great being, who trains in this way, trains in omniscience itself and shall assuredly emerge into omniscience itself."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra, king of the devas), said this to the venerable Śāriputra: "Noble Śāriputra, from where should a bodhisattva, a great being, seek the Perfection of Wisdom?"

Śāriputra replied: "Kauśika, a bodhisattva, a great being, should seek the Perfection of Wisdom from the chapter of the venerable Subhūti."

When he had said this, Śakra, lord of the gods, said again to the venerable Śāriputra: "Noble Śāriputra, this teaching of the Perfection of Wisdom that the noble Subhūti is expounding — whose power should it be understood as, and whose blessing should it be recognized as?"

The venerable Śāriputra replied: "Kauśika, this teaching of the Perfection of Wisdom that the venerable Subhūti is expounding should be understood as the power of the Tathāgata. It should be recognized as the blessing of the Tathāgata."

Then the venerable Subhūti said this to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, you ask: 'This teaching of the Perfection of Wisdom that the noble Subhūti is expounding — whose power should it be recognized as, and whose blessing should it be understood as?' Kauśika, I say that this teaching of the Perfection of Wisdom that I am expounding should be understood as the power of the Tathāgata, and should be recognized as the blessing of the Tathāgata.

"And Kauśika, you also ask: 'From where should a bodhisattva, a great being, seek the Perfection of Wisdom?' Kauśika, a bodhisattva, a great being, should not seek the Perfection of Wisdom from form, nor should one seek it apart from form. Likewise, one should not seek it from feeling, nor from perception, nor from formations, nor from consciousness, nor should one seek it apart from consciousness.

"Why is that? Kauśika, it is because form itself is not the Perfection of Wisdom, and yet apart from form the Perfection of Wisdom does not exist."

"Likewise, feeling is not the Perfection of Wisdom, perception is not the Perfection of Wisdom, formations are not the Perfection of Wisdom, consciousness is not the Perfection of Wisdom, and yet apart from consciousness the Perfection of Wisdom does not exist."

When this was said, Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra, king of the divine realm), spoke these words to the venerable Subhūti: "Noble Subhūti, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a great perfection. Noble Subhūti, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a perfection beyond measure. Noble Subhūti, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a perfection without limit. Noble Subhūti, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a perfection of infinite extent."

The elder Subhūti replied: "So it is, Kauśika. So it is indeed. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a great perfection. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection beyond measure. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection without limit. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent.

"And why is that? Because form is vast, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a great perfection. Likewise, because feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are vast, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a great perfection.

"Because form is beyond measure, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection beyond measure. Likewise, because feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are beyond measure, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection beyond measure.

"Because form is without limit, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection without limit. Likewise, because feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are without limit, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection without limit.

"Because form is of infinite extent, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent. Likewise, because feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are of infinite extent, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent.

"Kauśika, one does not cling to it as being called 'a great perfection,' nor does one cling to it as being called 'a perfection beyond measure,' nor as 'a perfection without limit,' nor as 'a perfection of infinite extent.' Kauśika, therefore this Perfection of Wisdom is a great perfection. It is a perfection beyond measure. It is a perfection without limit. Kauśika, it is a perfection of infinite extent.

"Kauśika, because its objects are of infinite extent, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent. Kauśika, because sentient beings are of infinite extent, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent.

"And Kauśika, in what way is the Perfection of Wisdom a perfection of infinite extent because its objects are of infinite extent?"

"Kauśika, since the beginning, middle, and end of all phenomena are not apprehended, therefore, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent. Kauśika, by this category as well, because its objects are of infinite extent, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent.

"Furthermore, Kauśika, because all phenomena are infinite and boundless, and their beginning, middle, or end is likewise not apprehended, therefore, Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent. Why is that? Kauśika, the beginning, middle, or end of form is not apprehended. And likewise, Kauśika, the beginning, middle, or end of feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness is not apprehended. Kauśika, by this category as well, because its objects are of infinite extent, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent.

"Furthermore, Kauśika, sentient beings are infinite and boundless. Why is that? Because the beginning, middle, or end of sentient beings is not apprehended. Kauśika, therefore, because sentient beings are of infinite extent, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent."

Then Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, spoke these words to the venerable Subhūti: "Noble Subhūti, in what way is this Perfection of Wisdom a perfection of infinite extent because sentient beings are of infinite extent?"

The elder Subhūti replied: "Kauśika, because sentient beings are of infinite extent in a way that surpasses all counting or manifold counting, this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent."

Śakra said: "Noble Subhūti, but then in what way should one speak of this Perfection of Wisdom as a perfection of infinite extent because sentient beings are of infinite extent?"

The elder Subhūti replied: "Kauśika, what do you think? This expression 'sentient being, sentient being' — of which phenomenon is it a word-designation?"

Śakra said: "Noble Subhūti, this expression 'sentient being, sentient being' is neither a word-designation of a phenomenon nor a word-designation of a non-phenomenon. This designation has arisen adventitiously. This designation is without any real thing. This designation has no basis in a self. This designation 'sentient being, sentient being' is without any apprehensible referent."

The elder Subhūti said: "Kauśika, what do you think? Is there any thing here that has been firmly established as a sentient being?"

Śakra replied: "Noble Subhūti, there is not."

Subhūti said: "Kauśika, when there is nothing that has been established as a sentient being, can there be sentient beings of infinite extent? Kauśika, even if a Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and fully awakened buddha were to dwell for as many eons as there are grains of sand in the Ganges River, and were to proclaim the word 'sentient being, sentient being' in a voice of boundless reach, deep and resonant — would there in that time be any sentient being that had been born, will be born, or is being born, or that had ceased, will cease, or is ceasing?"

Śakra replied: "Noble Subhūti, there would not."

"Why is that?"

"Because sentient beings are pure from the very beginning, and perfectly pure from the very beginning."

Subhūti said: "Kauśika, by this category as well, it is because sentient beings are of infinite extent that this Perfection of Wisdom is a perfection of infinite extent."

"Kauśika, in this way as well, it is because sentient beings are of infinite extent that the Perfection of Wisdom is to be understood as a perfection of infinite extent."

Then the gods together with Indra (Śakra), together with Brahmā, together with Prajāpati (the Lord of Creatures), together with the seers, and together with the assemblies of men and women, three times made this solemn proclamation: "How wonderful are phenomena! How wonderful are phenomena! How wonderful are phenomena! The nature of reality of all phenomena — that which is the arising of Tathāgatas — has been well spoken, declared, taught, and properly established by the noble elder Subhūti."

"Blessed One, from this day forward we shall regard whichever bodhisattvas, great bodhisattvas, are inseparable from this Perfection of Wisdom, and whichever bodhisattvas, great bodhisattvas, abide in this abiding of the Perfection of Wisdom — we shall regard these as Tathāgatas."

Then the Blessed One declared to those gods together with Indra, together with Brahmā, together with Prajāpati, together with the seers, and together with the assemblies of men and women: "Sons of the gods, so it is, so it is. Sons of the gods, when in the past, in the marketplace called Dīpaṃkara within the royal palace's surrounding city, I was in the presence of the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and completely awakened Buddha Dīpaṃkara, and was inseparable from this Perfection of Wisdom — at that time the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and completely awakened Buddha Dīpaṃkara, gave me, a brahmin youth, the prophecy of unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening, saying: 'In the future, after innumerable aeons, you shall become the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and completely awakened Buddha named Śākyamuni — one endowed with knowledge and conduct, a Sugata, knower of the world, unsurpassed guide of persons to be tamed, teacher of gods and humans, a buddha, a Blessed One.'"

Then those sons of the gods addressed the Blessed One thus: "Blessed One, it is wonderful that this Perfection of Wisdom of the bodhisattvas, the great bodhisattvas, accomplishes and thoroughly sustains them all the way to omniscience. Sugata, it is most wonderful."

This concludes the second chapter, called "The Chapter of Śakra," from the noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines.

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines. The Third Fascicle.

Then the Blessed One, knowing that in that assembly there had gathered and seated themselves sons of the gods, and knowing that the assembly of monks, nuns, laymen, and laywomen had gathered and seated themselves, and knowing that those sons of the gods were gathered and seated there, and knowing also that the entire fourfold assembly had gathered and was seated — with the sons of the gods who move in the desire realm and the sons of the gods who move in the form realm, together with the sons of the gods of the Brahma class, and the sons of the gods of Ābhāsvara, Śubhakṛtsna, and Akaniṣṭha as witnesses, he declared to the sons of the gods of the desire realm such as Śakra, lord of the gods, and to the sons of the gods of the Brahma class such as Great Brahmā, and to the sons of the gods of Ābhāsvara: "Sons of the gods, if some son of good family or daughter of good family holds, bears, recites, masters, or proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom, no māra or god of the māra class who is watching and seeking an opportunity shall find any opportunity against that person. Nor shall those humans or non-humans who are watching and seeking an opportunity find any opportunity against that son of good family or daughter of good family."

That son of good family or daughter of good family shall not die an untimely death while still having left things undone.

Sons of the gods, furthermore, those sons of the gods who have genuinely entered into the path toward unsurpassable, perfectly complete awakening, but who have not yet held, borne, recited, mastered, or proclaimed aloud this Perfection of Wisdom — they shall wish to come into the presence of that son of good family. They shall come to listen to that son of good family who holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom. And having listened, they shall hold it, bear it, recite it, master it, and proclaim it aloud.

Sons of the gods, furthermore, whatever son of good family or daughter of good family holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom — whether they go to a forest hermitage, or to the foot of a tree, or to an empty house, or to an unsheltered place, or along a road, or down a side path, or to a remote wilderness, or to the great ocean — wherever they go, whether walking, standing, sitting, or lying down: no fear, no terror shall arise or come to be.

Then the four great kings addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, it is wonderful that any son of good family or daughter of good family who holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom thereby disciplines sentient beings by the three vehicles, and yet does not give rise to the perception of them as sentient beings. Blessed One, we shall guard, protect, and shelter whatever son of good family or daughter of good family holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra, king of the divine beings), addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, I too shall guard, protect, and shelter whatever son of good family or daughter of good family holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom."

Then Great Brahmā, lord of the Sahā world, together with the sons of the gods of the Brahma class, also addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, we too shall guard, protect, and shelter whatever son of good family or daughter of good family holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One once more with these words: "Blessed One, it is wonderful that any son of good family or daughter of good family who holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom shall certainly attain and fully retain these qualities in this very life. Blessed One, and further — if one holds this Perfection of Wisdom, shall one thereby come to hold all six perfections?"

When this was asked, the Blessed One spoke to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, it is exactly so, exactly so. Kauśika, by holding the Perfection of Wisdom one shall come to hold all six perfections."

"Kauśika, furthermore, whoever holds, bears, recites, masters, and proclaims aloud this Perfection of Wisdom — all the qualities that such a son of good family or daughter of good family shall come to hold in this very life: listen well and take this to heart, for I shall explain it to you."

Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra), replied to the Blessed One, "Excellent!" and then turned attentively toward the Blessed One. And the Blessed One declared as follows:

"Kauśika, as for anyone who wishes to dispute, challenge, or contradict this Dharma — whatever disputes, challenges, and contradictions they have raised or may raise shall in turn come to nothing and shall not persist; the intentions of those who wish to dispute, challenge, or contradict it shall not be fulfilled. Why is that? Kauśika, it is because of this: wherever a son of good family or daughter of good family holds, bears, recites, masters, proclaims aloud, teaches, extensively teaches, recites by transmission, and chants this Perfection of Wisdom — whatever grounds for dispute have arisen or are raised in that very setting shall in turn come to nothing and shall not persist; the intentions of those who wish to dispute, challenge, or contradict it shall not be fulfilled.

Kauśika, a son of good family or daughter of good family who holds, bears, recites, masters, proclaims aloud, teaches, extensively teaches, recites by transmission, and chants this Perfection of Wisdom shall in this very life come to hold all these qualities fully.

Kauśika, consider this example. There is a plant called Mā-ghi which pacifies every poison entirely. Now, a venomous serpent, hungry and seeking food and wishing to eat, comes upon some living creature and pursues it, following the scent of that creature. But then that creature happens to be where the Mā-ghi plant is and goes there and settles. Thereupon the serpent turns back from the mere fragrance of that plant. Why? Because, just as the venom of the serpent is overpowered in that way, this is to be understood as the medicinal power of that plant — so great is its potency.

Kauśika, in the same way, for a son of good family or daughter of good family who holds, bears, recites, masters, proclaims aloud, teaches, extensively teaches, recites by transmission, and chants this Perfection of Wisdom — whatever grounds for dispute, challenge, and contradiction have arisen or are raised shall swiftly vanish from that very place by the radiance, power, strength, and force of the Perfection of Wisdom, subsiding and ceasing to grow; wherever they arose, from that very place they shall be checked, subside, and not persist. Why? Because the Perfection of Wisdom pacifies all that is grasped at — from attachment all the way to that which has not yet passed beyond sorrow (nirvāṇa) — and does not cause it to proliferate.

Kauśika, the four great kings, Śakra lord of the gods, Brahmā lord of the Sahā world, the buddhas who are Blessed Ones, and all the bodhisattvas —"

any son or daughter of good family who upholds, bears, reads, thoroughly masters, recites aloud, teaches, explains in detail, chants by rote, and proclaims this Perfection of Wisdom — it shall protect, shelter, and guard them, and in this very life they shall fully come to possess these qualities. Kauśika, furthermore, any son or daughter of good family who upholds, bears, reads, thoroughly masters, recites aloud, teaches, explains in detail, chants by rote, and proclaims this Perfection of Wisdom — their speech shall become worthy of being heeded, their speech shall become gentle, their speech shall become fitting, and their speech shall be free from error. They shall not be overcome by anger, and they shall not be overcome by pride. Why? Because the Perfection of Wisdom thoroughly tames and thoroughly subdues: it does not allow anger to increase, does not allow pride to increase, does not allow resentment to be harbored, does not allow malice to be harbored, and does not allow underlying tendencies to take hold. In the son or daughter of good family who practices in this way, mindfulness and loving-kindness shall arise, and they shall think to themselves: "If I were to give rise to malice, my sense faculties would be disturbed and the color of my face would change — and for one who has genuinely entered upon the path toward unsurpassed, perfectly complete awakening and wishes to train in it, to fall under the power of anger would be improper." Thinking thus, they shall swiftly recover their mindfulness. Kauśika, any son or daughter of good family who upholds, bears, reads, thoroughly masters, recites aloud, teaches, explains in detail, chants by rote, and proclaims this Perfection of Wisdom — they too shall fully come to possess these qualities in this very life.

When the Blessed One had spoken these words, Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One thus: "Blessed One, it is wonderful that the Perfection of Wisdom dwells in this way so as to thoroughly tame the great bodhisattvas and free them from arrogance."

The Blessed One then declared: "Kauśika, furthermore, any son or daughter of good family who shall uphold, bear, read, thoroughly master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by rote, and proclaim this Perfection of Wisdom — if that son or daughter of good family, engaging in this way with the Perfection of Wisdom — upholding it, bearing it, reading it, thoroughly mastering it, reciting it aloud, teaching it, explaining it in detail, chanting it by rote, and proclaiming it — were to enter first into battle at the time of going to war, whether they enter battle, have entered battle, have passed through battle, or are going, standing, or sitting within the battle — Kauśika, for any son or daughter of good family who holds this Perfection of Wisdom in mind, bears it, reads it, thoroughly masters it, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, chants it by rote, and proclaims it, the possibility of their life being cut short does not exist and the opportunity for that does not arise. Nor does the possibility exist that harm from another could cut their life short.

Kauśika, furthermore, if anyone were to hurl a club, a weapon, a stone, or anything else whatsoever at such a person, it will not strike their body. Why is that? Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a great knowledge-mantra (vidyā). Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a knowledge-mantra without measure. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a knowledge-mantra beyond reckoning. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is an unsurpassed knowledge-mantra. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is an unequalled knowledge-mantra. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is a knowledge-mantra equal to the unequalled.

Why is that? Kauśika, a son or daughter of good family who trains in this knowledge-mantra does not think of doing harm to themselves, does not think of doing harm to others, and does not think of doing harm to both.

Kauśika, a bodhisattva, a great being, who trains in this knowledge-mantra will awaken to the unsurpassed, perfectly complete awakening, will attain the gnosis of omniscience, and having fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfectly complete awakening, will behold the minds of all sentient beings. Why is that? Kauśika, for the bodhisattva, the great being, who has trained in this knowledge-mantra, there is nothing whatsoever that has not been attained, not been known, or not been made directly apparent. Therefore it is called the gnosis of omniscience.

Kauśika, whatever son or daughter of good family holds this Perfection of Wisdom in mind, bears it, reads it, thoroughly masters it, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, chants it by rote, and proclaims it — that person will fully embrace this very quality in the present life.

Kauśika, furthermore, wherever this Perfection of Wisdom is written out in a small form, placed within a book, set down with offerings made before it, and reverenced — even if one neither holds it in mind, nor bears it, nor reads it, nor thoroughly masters it, nor recites it aloud, nor teaches it, nor explains it in detail, nor chants it by rote, nor proclaims it — still, Kauśika, apart from the ripening of former karma, no human or non-human being seeking an opportunity against sentient beings will find that opportunity, for there is no such possibility. Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family too will fully embrace this very quality in the present life.

Kauśika, consider this example: those humans, non-humans, and even animals who are seated at the seat of awakening (Vajrāsana), or who are in the vicinity of the seat of awakening, or who are within the seat of awakening, or who are at the root of the awakening tree — apart from the ripening of former karma, no human or non-human being is able to harm them, injure them, or bring them low. Why is that? Because it is there that the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas of the past, future, and present have perfectly and completely awakened, are perfectly and completely awakening, and will perfectly and completely awaken — they who cultivate and proclaim fearlessness, harmlessness, and freedom from terror toward all sentient beings.

Likewise, Kauśika, wherever a son or daughter of good family holds, bears, reads, thoroughly masters, recites aloud, teaches, explains in detail, proclaims by rote, and chants this very Perfection of Wisdom — there too, Kauśika, apart from the ripening of former karma, no human or non-human being is able to harm them, injure them, or bring them low. Why is that? Because this very Perfection of Wisdom has caused that place to become a shrine for sentient beings — a place to be venerated, revered, honored, decorated, served with devotion, and esteemed as supreme — and has caused it to become a protector, refuge, shelter, and ally for sentient beings who approach it.

Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family thereby fully grasps all these benefits in this very lifetime.

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra, king of the divine realm), addressed the Blessed One with these words: Blessed One, if a son or daughter of good family were to write down this Perfection of Wisdom, place it within a book, and keep it there — honoring it with divine flowers, divine incense, divine perfume, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine garments, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, divine pennants, and all around it a garland of divine lamps, along with many other divine offerings — venerating it, esteeming it as supreme, revering it, honoring it, decorating it, and serving it with devotion — and if another were to honor with veneration and to esteem as supreme, revere, worship, decorate, and serve with devotion the bodily relics of the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas who have passed beyond sorrow (nirvāṇa), placed and kept within stūpas — honoring those stūpas likewise with divine flowers, divine incense, divine perfume, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine garments, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, divine pennants, and all around them a garland of divine lamps, along with many other divine offerings — venerating them, esteeming them as supreme, revering them, honoring them, decorating them, and serving them with devotion — then of those two, the son or daughter of good family, which one generates the greater merit?

When he had thus inquired, the Blessed One said to Śakra, lord of the gods: Kauśika, it is precisely for this that I put the question to you yourself — prophesy it as best you are able. Kauśika, what do you think? That through which the omniscience that is the very essence of a Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly complete buddha is fully accomplished — by training in which path did the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly complete buddha attain and fully, perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening and omniscience?

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, Śakra, lord of the gods, said to the Blessed One: Blessed One, it is by training in this very Perfection of Wisdom that the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly complete buddha attained and fully, perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening and omniscience.

The Blessed One then declared:

Kauśika, that being so, it is not by obtaining this physical body of mine that one comes to be counted among those called Tathāgata. It is by obtaining omniscience that one comes to be counted among those called Tathāgata. Kauśika, this omniscience of the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly complete buddha arises from the Perfection of Wisdom. And Kauśika, this obtaining of the Tathāgata's physical body also arises from the Perfection of Wisdom and from skillful means, and it serves as the support for the wisdom of omniscience. Relying upon this support, the wisdom of omniscience is fully revealed. The body of a buddha is fully revealed. The body of Dharma is fully revealed. The body of the Saṅgha is fully revealed. Thus, since this obtaining of the physical body — which is the cause of the wisdom of omniscience — serves as the support for the wisdom of omniscience, it becomes a stūpa worthy of veneration, homage, honor, reverence, offerings, praise, and service by all sentient beings. In just the same way, even after I have passed into nirvāṇa, those bodies will continue to be venerated with offerings. Therefore, Kauśika, whatever son or daughter of good family writes out this Perfection of Wisdom, has it compiled into a book, and then venerates it with divine flowers, divine incense, divine fragrances, divine garlands, divine unguents, divine powders, divine garments, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, and divine pennants, and surrounds it with rows of divine lamps, and honors it with many kinds of divine offerings — paying it homage, honoring it, revering it, making offerings to it, praising it, and serving it — Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family will generate very great merit. Why is that? Kauśika, because that son or daughter of good family will have made offerings to the wisdom of omniscience. And whatever son or daughter of good family, when seeking to learn this Perfection of Wisdom, honors, reveres, and venerates this very book, makes offerings to it, praises it, and serves it with many kinds of offerings — that person generates even more merit than the former. Why is that? Kauśika, because whoever makes offerings to the Perfection of Wisdom will have made offerings to the wisdom of omniscience.

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, Śakra, lord of the gods, said this to the Blessed One: Blessed One, those people of this Jambudvīpa who do not seek out this Perfection of Wisdom, do not take hold of it, do not retain it, do not read it, do not master it thoroughly, do not recite it aloud, do not teach it, do not explain it in detail, do not transmit its scriptural reading, do not commit it to recitation by heart — and who do not venerate this Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, fragrances, garlands, unguents, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, with rows of lamps surrounding it, and with many kinds of offerings — who do not pay it homage, honor it, revere it, make offerings to it, praise it, or serve it —

"Blessed One, was not the offering made to the Perfection of Wisdom as proclaimed by the Blessed One thereby of great meaning? Blessed One, was it not understood that the offering made to this Perfection of Wisdom as proclaimed by the Blessed One is attended with great benefit? Was it not known by that person that it brings great fruit and great karmic maturation? Yet even so, they came to hold it, understand it, and know it — and then turned back from it and did not come to have faith."

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke thus to Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods: "Kauśika, what do you think? Among the people of Jambudvīpa, how many are there who are endowed with faith through understanding the Buddha, who are endowed with faith through understanding the Dharma, and who are endowed with faith through understanding the Saṅgha?"

When this was said, Śakra, lord of the gods, replied to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, among the people of Jambudvīpa, those who are endowed with faith through understanding the Buddha, endowed with faith through understanding the Dharma, and endowed with faith through understanding the Saṅgha — these are few."

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke thus to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, it is so, it is so. Among the people of Jambudvīpa, those who are endowed with faith through understanding the Buddha, endowed with faith through understanding the Dharma, and endowed with faith through understanding the Saṅgha — such persons are indeed few. Kauśika, those who have attained the fruit of stream-entry are fewer still than these. Those who have attained the fruit of once-returning and the fruit of non-returning are fewer still than these. Those who have attained arhatship are fewer still than these. Those who actualize solitary awakening are fewer still than these. Those who have aroused the mind set upon unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening are fewer still than these. Those who have aroused the mind set upon unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening and cause that mind of awakening to flourish and expand are fewer still than these. Those who have aroused the mind set upon unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, caused it to flourish, and then taken up diligent effort and abide in that are fewer still than these. Those who practice the Perfection of Wisdom as a discipline of yoga are fewer still than these. Those who engage in the Perfection of Wisdom are fewer still than these. Those who engage in and strive in the Perfection of Wisdom and abide upon the bodhisattva ground of non-retrogression are fewer still than these. Those who engage in and strive in the Perfection of Wisdom and will come to perfectly and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening are fewer still than these. Those who engage in and strive in the Perfection of Wisdom and do perfectly and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — these are fewer still than all of these.

"Kauśika, as for those great bodhisattvas who, abiding on the bodhisattva ground of non-retrogression, have perfectly and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and who thereafter, with excellent and complete superior intention, themselves train in and strive in the Perfection of Wisdom, and at the same time introduce others to this Perfection of Wisdom, presenting it close at hand, transmitting its reading aloud to them, and drawing those others as well into taking it up —"

— leading others to carry it, to read it, to master it fully, to recite it aloud, to teach it, to teach it in detail, to transmit its oral reading, and to chant it — those persons worship this very Perfection of Wisdom with divine flowers, divine incense, divine fragrance, divine garlands, divine unguents, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, and divine pennants, and all around it with strings of divine lamps, and with many kinds of divine offerings. They pay it honor, revere it as supreme, hold it in esteem, worship it, adorn it with ornament, and pay it homage.

Kauśika, as for those sentient beings who have generated the awakening mind, and who, having generated the awakening mind, cause that awakening mind to expand and cause it to fully flourish, and who then practice in accordance with awakening — such sentient beings are immeasurable, beyond counting. Yet, Kauśika, from among those immeasurable, innumerable sentient beings practicing for the sake of awakening, perhaps only one or two will come to abide on the bodhisattva ground of non-retrogression. Why is that? Kauśika, unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening is difficult to accomplish for those of weak diligence, those who are lazy, those of inferior aspiration, those of inferior mind, those of inferior perception, those of inferior devotion, and those of inferior wisdom.

Kauśika, therefore, any son or daughter of good family who wishes to swiftly and easily realize, manifest, and awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening should, without interruption, listen to this very Perfection of Wisdom, take hold of it, retain it, read it, master it fully, recite it aloud, teach it, teach it in detail, transmit its oral reading, chant it, and inquire into it thoroughly. Why is that? Kauśika, such sons and daughters of good family understand thus: the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and completely awakened buddhas — when formerly they were practicing the conduct of bodhisattvas, they trained in this Perfection of Wisdom. Therefore we too shall train in it, for this Perfection of Wisdom is our Teacher. Kauśika, whether the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and completely awakened Buddha is present or whether he has fully passed into nirvāṇa, the bodhisattva, the great being, should rely on this very Perfection of Wisdom.

Kauśika, therefore, if a son or daughter of good family, for the purpose of venerating a Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and completely awakened Buddha who has fully passed into nirvāṇa, were to have many tens of millions of stūpas made of the seven precious materials, containing within them relics of the Tathāgata as their essence, and having had them built were then, for as long as they lived, to worship those stūpas with divine flowers, divine incense, divine fragrance, divine garlands, divine unguents, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, and divine pennants, and all around with strings of divine lamps, and with many kinds of divine offerings — paying them honor, revering them as supreme, holding them in esteem, worshiping them, adorning them with ornament, and paying them homage — then, Kauśika, what do you think? Does that son or daughter of good family generate much merit on that basis?" Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods) replied:

"Very many, O Blessed One! Very many, O Sugata!" The Blessed One then declared: "Kauśika, even more merit than that is generated by that son or daughter of good family who, with sincere faith in this Perfection of Wisdom, reflects upon it, takes delight in it, and, with a devoted mind, arouses the awakening mind toward full awakening — and then, with elevated intention, listens to it, takes it up, retains it, reads it, masters it thoroughly, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, chants it from scripture, commits it to memory, and expounds it fully and correctly to others; who also elucidates the meaning of this teaching, reflects upon it in the mind, and, with wisdom that accords with how things are established, thoroughly investigates this teaching; who, thinking 'May the lineage of the buddhas not be severed, may the sublime Dharma not decline,' acts to establish the sublime Dharma so that it abides for a long time; and who, without departing from proper conduct, brings about benefit for the great bodhisattvas — having, even in a small way when young, caused this to be entered into a volume, whether carrying or placing it, then worshiping that very Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, fragrance, garlands, unguents, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, and pennants, and all around with strings of lamps, and with many kinds of offerings — paying it honor, revering it as supreme, holding it in esteem, worshiping it, adorning it with ornament, and paying it homage. Kauśika, that very son or daughter of good family generates an even greater accumulation of merit than the other.

"Now, Kauśika, let alone stūpas of the nature of the seven precious substances containing relics of Tathāgatas in their multitudes of tens of millions — Kauśika, if a son or daughter of good family were to cause this entire Jambudvīpa to be filled throughout with stūpas of the nature of the seven precious substances containing the essential relics of Tathāgatas, and having done so were to worship those stūpas with divine flowers, divine incense, divine fragrance, divine garlands, divine unguents, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, and divine pennants, and all around with strings of divine lamps, and with many kinds of divine offerings — paying them honor, revering them as supreme, holding them in esteem, worshiping them, adorning them with ornament, and paying them homage — for as long as they live, then, Kauśika, what do you think? Does that son or daughter of good family generate much merit on that basis?" Śakra replied: "Very many, O Blessed One! Very many, O Sugata!" The Blessed One then declared: "Kauśika, even more merit than that is generated by that son or daughter of good family who, with sincere faith in this Perfection of Wisdom, reflects upon it, takes delight in it, and, with a devoted mind, arouses the awakening mind toward full awakening — and then, with elevated intention, listens to it, takes it up, retains it, reads it, masters it thoroughly, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, chants it from scripture, commits it to memory, and expounds it fully and correctly to others; who also elucidates the meaning of this teaching, reflects upon it in the mind, and, with wisdom that accords with how things are established, thoroughly investigates this teaching; and who, thinking 'May the lineage of the buddhas not be severed, may the sublime Dharma not decline,' acts to establish the sublime Dharma so that it abides for a long time;

and in that way, without diverging from proper conduct, accomplishes the task of benefiting the great bodhisattvas — even if only in the smallest measure, by placing this teaching within a volume and keeping or preserving it, and then honoring that Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, fragrant substances, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, and garlands of lamps encircling it all around, and with many other kinds of offerings; paying it homage, exalting it, revering it, venerating it, making prostrations before it, and attending upon it —

Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates even more merit than that.

Kauśika, let us suppose that this continent of Jambudvīpa were entirely filled with stūpas made of the seven precious substances, each containing relics of the Tathāgatas as their heart and essence. Kauśika, and now suppose further that in the world-realm of the four great continents, every single sentient being that exists — each one of them — were to have one such stūpa made of the seven precious substances, containing Tathāgata relics at its heart, and that for the duration of their lives they were to honor those stūpas with divine flowers, divine incense, divine fragrant substances, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, divine pennants, divine garlands of lamps encircling them all around, and many other kinds of divine offerings; paying them homage, exalting them, revering them, venerating them, making prostrations before them, and attending upon them —

Kauśika, what do you think? Would all those sentient beings thereby generate a great amount of merit from that basis?

Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods) replied: They would, Blessed One. They would, Sugata.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, a son or daughter of good family who generates merit in this way generates even greater merit still — that is, one who, with deep faith in this Perfection of Wisdom, who contemplates and inclines toward it, who arouses the mind set on awakening with wholeheartedness, and with elevated intention listens to it, takes it up, retains it, reads it, masters it thoroughly, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, chants it from scripture, commits it to memory, expounds it fully and correctly to others, elucidates the meaning of this teaching, reflects upon it in the mind, and with wisdom that accords with how things are established thoroughly investigates it; and who, thinking "May the lineage of the buddhas not be severed, may the sublime Dharma not decline," acts to establish the sublime Dharma so that it abides for a long time; and who, without diverging from proper conduct, accomplishes the task of benefiting the great bodhisattvas — even if only in the smallest measure, by placing this teaching within a volume and keeping or preserving it, and then honoring that Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, fragrant substances, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, and garlands of lamps encircling it all around, and with many other kinds of offerings; paying it homage, exalting it, revering it, venerating it, making prostrations before it, and attending upon it —

Kauśika, this son or daughter of good family thereby generates far greater merit from that basis.

Kauśika, let all the sentient beings of the world-realm of the four great continents be set aside. Kauśika, now suppose further that all the sentient beings that exist in a thousand world-systems — each one of them — were to have one stūpa made of the seven precious substances, containing Tathāgata relics at its heart,

and who, for as long as they live, were to make offerings to them with divine flowers, divine incense, divine fragrances, divine garlands, divine unguents, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, and divine pennants, and who, encircling them all around, were to offer divine strings of lamps and many other kinds of divine offerings; paying them homage, exalting them, revering them, venerating them, making prostrations before them, and attending upon them —

Kauśika, what do you think? Do all those sentient beings thereby generate great merit from that basis?

Śakra (Indra, king of the gods) replied: Greatly so, Blessed One. Greatly so, Sugata.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, even so, this son or daughter of good family generates far greater merit than that — one who, with faith and devotion, with thought and aspiration, having aroused the awakening mind toward awakening, with elevated intention listens to this Perfection of Wisdom, retains it, bears it, reads it, masters it fully, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it, transmits the scriptural text, chants it, and teaches it to others in full and correctly; who expounds the meaning of this text, follows it with discerning thought, and with wisdom established just as it truly is thoroughly investigates it here; who, with the intention that the stream of the Buddha's way not be cut off and the sacred Dharma not decline, strives to establish the sacred Dharma so that it may long endure; and who, not departing from proper conduct, works to accomplish benefit for the great bodhisattvas — even if only in the smallest measure, having compiled it into a volume, and either bearing it or setting it down, were to make offerings to that Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, fragrances, garlands, unguents, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, and pennants, and, encircling it all around, were to offer strings of lamps and many other kinds of offerings; paying it homage, exalting it, revering it, venerating it, making prostrations before it, and attending upon it —

Kauśika, this son or daughter of good family thereby generates far greater merit from that basis.

Kauśika, let all the sentient beings of a thousand world-systems be set aside. Kauśika, now suppose further that all the sentient beings that exist in a two-thousand middling world-system — each one of them — were to have one stūpa made of the seven precious substances, containing Tathāgata relics at its heart, and for as long as they live were to make offerings to them with divine flowers, divine incense, divine fragrances, divine garlands, divine unguents, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, and divine pennants, and who, encircling them all around, were to offer divine strings of lamps and many other kinds of divine offerings; paying them homage, exalting them, revering them, venerating them, making prostrations before them, and attending upon them —

Kauśika, what do you think? Do all those sentient beings thereby generate great merit from that basis?

Śakra replied: Greatly so, Blessed One. Greatly so, Sugata.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, even so, this son or daughter of good family generates far greater merit — one who, with faith and devotion, with thought and aspiration, having aroused the awakening mind toward awakening,

listens with earnest aspiration, retains it, upholds it, reads it, masters it completely, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, intones the scriptural transmission, chants it by heart, and also teaches others extensively and correctly; who explains the meaning of this text, reflects on it mentally, and thoroughly examines it here with wisdom that is established just as it is; who, thinking "may the way of the buddhas never be cut off, may the sacred Dharma never decline," strives to make the sacred Dharma long endure; and who, without departing from proper conduct, undertakes to bring benefit to the bodhisattvas, the great beings — even if only a small portion of this has been compiled into a volume and is held or preserved; and who offers to that Perfection of Wisdom flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, and encircling strings of lamps, together with many other kinds of offerings; paying homage to it, exalting it, revering it, venerating it, making artistic representations of it, and attending upon it —

Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family thereby generates far greater merit from that basis.

Kauśika, let alone all the sentient beings of a two-thousandfold intermediate world system. Kauśika, imagine further that all the sentient beings there are in a great three-thousandfold world system — that each and every one of those sentient beings causes to be made, one each, a stūpa of seven precious substances containing relics of a Tathāgata at its heart; and that for as long as they live, they offer to those stūpas divine flowers, divine incense, divine perfume, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, divine pennants, and encircling divine strings of lamps, together with many other kinds of divine offerings; paying them homage, exalting them, revering them, venerating them, making artistic representations of them, and attending upon them —

Kauśika, what do you think? Do all those sentient beings thereby generate great merit from that basis?

Śakra replied: Greatly so, Blessed One. Greatly so, Sugata.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, even so, this son or daughter of good family generates far greater merit — one who, with faith and devotion, with thought and aspiration, having aroused the awakening mind toward awakening, listens with earnest aspiration, retains it, upholds it, reads it, masters it completely, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, intones the scriptural transmission, chants it by heart, and also teaches others extensively and correctly; who explains the meaning of this text, reflects on it mentally, and thoroughly examines it here with wisdom that is established just as it is; who, thinking "may the way of the buddhas never be cut off, may the sacred Dharma never decline," strives to make the sacred Dharma long endure; and who, without departing from proper conduct, undertakes to bring benefit to the bodhisattvas, the great beings — even if only a small portion of this has been compiled into a volume and is held or preserved; and who offers to that Perfection of Wisdom flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, and encircling strings of lamps, together with many other kinds of offerings; paying homage to it, exalting it, revering it, venerating it, making artistic representations of it, and attending upon it —

Kauśika, this son or daughter of good family generates far greater merit from that basis alone. Kauśika, consider: every single sentient being throughout the great trichiliocosm has each caused to be made one stūpa of the seven precious substances, containing the relics of the Tathāgata as its essential core, and each of them makes offerings to those stūpas for as long as they live — with divine flowers, divine incense, divine perfume, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, divine pennants, and encircling strings of divine lamps, together with many other kinds of divine offerings; paying homage to them, exalting them, revering them, venerating them, making artistic representations of them, and attending upon them. Let that stand as it is.

Kauśika, now suppose further that every single sentient being there may be throughout the great trichiliocosm — all of them, surveyed and counted without exception, having in succession obtained a human body — and from among them, too, each sentient being causes to be made one stūpa of the seven precious substances, containing the relics of the Tathāgata as its essential core; and having caused them to be made, each of those sentient beings then takes their position before all those stūpas and, for a kalpa or more than a kalpa, makes offerings with every kind of music, every kind of song, every kind of dance, all manner of instruments and all manner of percussion, divine flowers, divine incense, divine perfume, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, all divine pennants, encircling strings of divine lamps, and all the many kinds of offerings of both gods and humans; paying homage to them, exalting them, revering them, venerating them, making artistic representations of them, and attending upon them; and all those sentient beings, by such meritorious deeds as these, cause to be made immeasurable and countless stūpas and cause such offerings as these to be made as well — Kauśika, what do you think? Would all those sentient beings generate a great deal of merit from that basis?

Śakra replied: Greatly so, Blessed One. Greatly so, Sugata.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, even surpassing that, this son or daughter of good family generates far greater merit still — namely, one who, with faith directed toward this Perfection of Wisdom, with reflection and devoted attention, with a heart full of sincere feeling, having aroused the mind of awakening, and with heightened intention, listens to it, holds it, expounds it, reads it, fully masters it, recites it aloud, teaches it, demonstrates it in detail, proclaims its transmission, chants it by heart, teaches it extensively and correctly to others, explains its meaning, follows it through in reflective thought, and investigates it thoroughly here with whatever level of wisdom one has attained; who aspires that the stream of the Buddha's way not be cut off and that the sacred Dharma not decline, thus working to make the sacred Dharma long endure; and who, without departing from proper conduct, undertakes to bring benefit to the bodhisattvas, the great beings — even if only a small portion of this has been compiled into a volume and is held or preserved; and who offers to that Perfection of Wisdom flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, and encircling strings of lamps, together with many other kinds of offerings;

honoring it, holding it supreme, revering it, venerating it, making symbolic representations of it, and showing it devoted respect — such a son or daughter of good family, O Kauśika, generates thereby far more merit than from that former basis.

When the Blessed One had spoken these words, Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One with these words: "It is so, Blessed One; it is so, Sugata. Blessed One, the son or daughter of good family who honors this Perfection of Wisdom, holds it supreme, reveres it, venerates it, makes symbolic representations of it, and shows it devoted respect — that person thereby greatly honors, holds supreme, reveres, venerates, makes symbolic representations of, and shows devoted respect to all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones — past, future, and present — dwelling in all the world-systems, whom all the buddhas fully know by their buddha-gnosis. Blessed One, to say nothing of all the sentient beings of this great trichiliocosm — even if all the sentient beings of as many great trichiliocosmic world-systems as there are grains of sand in the Gaṅgā River were each to be granted permission to build a stūpa of the seven precious substances enshrining the relics of a Tathāgata, and having built them, every one of those sentient beings were to establish all those stūpas and venerate them for a kalpa or more with every kind of music and melody, every song and dance, every instrument and rhythmic clapping, and all divine flowers, divine incense, divine perfumes, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, divine pennants, encircling strings of divine lamps, and many other varieties of divine offerings — honoring them, holding them supreme, revering them, venerating them, making symbolic representations of them, and showing devoted respect — yet, Blessed One, this son or daughter of good family who, with genuine faith in this Perfection of Wisdom, who reflects upon it, who is devoted and earnest in mind, who has aroused the awakening mind toward full enlightenment, and who with heightened intention listens to it, grasps it, retains it, reads it, brings about thorough understanding, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, transmits its wording, chants it, teaches it fully and accurately to others, comments upon its meaning, mentally investigates it as an object of contemplation, and with the wisdom of what has been truly established examines it thoroughly here; and who, aspiring that the stream of the Buddha's way not be cut off and the sacred Dharma not decline, thus works to make the sacred Dharma long endure; and who, without departing from proper conduct, undertakes to bring benefit to the bodhisattvas, the great beings — even if only a small portion has been compiled into a volume and is held or preserved; and who offers to that Perfection of Wisdom flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, encircling strings of lamps, and many other varieties of offerings, honoring it, holding it supreme, revering it, venerating it, making symbolic representations of it, and showing it devoted respect — Blessed One, this son or daughter of good family thereby generates far more merit than all of that."

Then the Blessed One spoke these words to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, it is so, it is so.

Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than from that basis. Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates immeasurable merit from that basis. Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates incalculable merit from that basis. Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates inconceivable merit from that basis. Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates incomparable merit from that basis. Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates boundless merit from that basis.

Why is that? Kauśika, the omniscience of the Tathāgatas, arhats, and perfectly awakened buddhas is born from the Perfection of Wisdom, and the veneration of the Tathāgatas' bodies is likewise born from omniscience. Therefore, Kauśika, when a son or daughter of good family has faith in this Perfection of Wisdom and reflects upon it with delight, with a mind of deep engagement arouses the awakening mind oriented toward awakening, and then with elevated aspiration listens to it, grasps it, retains it, reads it, fully masters it, proclaims it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, recites it by transmission, chants it, and broadly and correctly teaches it to others — and further elucidates its meaning, reflects upon it with the mind, investigates it thoroughly here with wisdom commensurate with what has been established, acts to ensure that the stream of the buddhas' way is not severed and that the sacred Dharma does not decline, striving to make the sacred Dharma long endure, and, without parting from proper conduct, works to bring about benefit for bodhisattvas, those great beings — and who, even if they did all this only in a small way, compiled it into a volume and holds or preserves it, and offers to that Perfection of Wisdom flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, encircling strings of lamps, and many other varieties of offerings, honoring it, holding it supreme, revering it, venerating it, making symbolic representations of it, and showing it devoted respect — Kauśika, the merit accumulated by this person does not approach even a hundredth part of the merit previously accumulated by honoring that reliquary of the seven precious materials enshrining the Tathāgata's relics; not a thousandth part, not a hundred-thousandth part, not a ten-millionth part, not a billion-part, not a trillion-part, not a hundred-trillion-part, not a ten-quadrillion-part — it does not come close even by a hundred thousand trillions, it does not come close numerically, it cannot be reckoned, illustrated by example, demonstrated by analogy, or borne as a basis or cause.

Then forty thousand divine sons who had gathered and assembled in that retinue together with Śakra, lord of the gods, said this to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Friend, seize the Perfection of Wisdom! Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be grasped. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be retained. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be read. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be fully mastered. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be proclaimed aloud. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be taught. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be explained in detail. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be recited by transmission. Friend, the Perfection of Wisdom is to be chanted."

Then the Blessed One spoke these words to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, seize the Perfection of Wisdom! Kauśika, carry the Perfection of Wisdom! Kauśika, retain the Perfection of Wisdom! Kauśika, master the Perfection of Wisdom fully! Kauśika, proclaim the Perfection of Wisdom aloud! Kauśika, teach the Perfection of Wisdom! Kauśika, explain the Perfection of Wisdom in detail! Kauśika, recite the Perfection of Wisdom by transmission! Kauśika, chant the Perfection of Wisdom!

And why is this? Kauśika, whenever thoughts of this kind arise in the minds of the asuras — 'We shall go to battle against the gods of the Thirty-Three! We shall wage war together against the gods of the Thirty-Three!' — at that very moment, Kauśika, you should call this Perfection of Wisdom to mind and chant it, and those very thoughts arising in the minds of those asuras will be extinguished."

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, Śakra, lord of the gods, said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is a great vidyā-mantra. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is a vidyā-mantra without measure. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is a vidyā-mantra without limit. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is a vidyā-mantra without superior. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is an unsurpassed vidyā-mantra. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is a vidyā-mantra without equal. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is a vidyā-mantra equal to the unequalled."

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One said this to Śakra, lord of the gods: "So it is, Kauśika, so it is. This Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a great vidyā-mantra. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a vidyā-mantra without measure. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a vidyā-mantra without limit. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a vidyā-mantra without superior. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed an unsurpassed vidyā-mantra. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a vidyā-mantra without equal. Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed a vidyā-mantra equal to the unequalled.

And why is this? Kauśika, it is in reliance upon this vidyā-mantra of the Perfection of Wisdom that the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas of former times fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening."

Kauśika, however many Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas shall arise in future times and fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening — all of those as well, Kauśika, shall fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening in reliance upon this very vidyā-mantra of the Perfection of Wisdom. Kauśika, the buddhas, Blessed Ones, who at this very moment fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening in the immeasurable and innumerable world-systems of the great trichiliocosm — all of those as well, Kauśika, fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening in reliance upon this very vidyā-mantra of the Perfection of Wisdom. Kauśika, I too have fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening in reliance upon this very vidyā-mantra of the Perfection of Wisdom.

Kauśika, in reliance upon this very vidyā-mantra, the paths of the ten virtuous actions are fully revealed in the world. The four meditative concentrations endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world. The four immeasurables endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world. The four formless absorptions endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world. The six higher knowledges endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world. The thirty-seven factors concordant with awakening are fully revealed in the world. In brief, the eighty-four thousand aggregates of Dharma are fully revealed in the world. The wisdom of the buddhas, self-arisen wisdom, and inconceivable wisdom are fully revealed in the world.

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines. The Fourth Fascicle.

Kauśika, at whatever time Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas do not arise in the world, at that time too, Kauśika, in reliance upon this very vidyā-mantra of the Perfection of Wisdom, the bodhisattvas, great beings, having previously heard what is concordant with the Perfection of Wisdom as its cause, will come to be endowed with skillful means. Those who are endowed with loving care for sentient beings, out of that very loving care, relying upon this world, fully reveal the paths of the ten virtuous actions in the world. The four meditative concentrations not endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world. The four immeasurables not endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world. The four formless absorptions not endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world. The five higher knowledges not endowed with the branches of awakening are fully revealed in the world.

Kauśika, consider this example: in dependence upon the orb of the moon, all medicinal herbs and stars manifest whatever power and might they possess, and the constellations too appear with whatever power and might they have. Kauśika, in just the same way, when a Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and fully awakened buddha has passed away and the sacred Dharma has fallen into decline, and when Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas have not yet arisen in the world —

— in that time, whatever practices of Dharma-conformity, of peaceful conduct, and of virtuous conduct are established and cultivated in the world — all of these are born from the bodhisattva and are fully set forth by the bodhisattva. They issue from the bodhisattva's skillful means, and it should be understood that this skillful means of the bodhisattvas is itself born from the Perfection of Wisdom.

Kauśika, furthermore, those sons of good family and daughters of good family who hold, bear, recite, make thoroughly complete, proclaim, teach, explain, transmit by oral tradition, chant, commit to writing, attend to with the mind, and keep in memory this Perfection of Wisdom — from that basis they may hope for many benefits in this very life.

When this had been declared, Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods), lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One thus: Blessed One, with what benefits of this life will those sons of good family and daughters of good family be endowed?

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, those sons of good family and daughters of good family will not meet their end through hunger and thirst. They will not meet their end through poison. They will not meet their end through weapons. They will not meet their end through fire. They will not meet their end through water. They will not meet their end through clubs. They will not meet their end through harm inflicted by others. Even should harm arise and befall them at the hands of a king, a king's son, a king's great minister, or a king's ministers, those who recollect and recite this Perfection of Wisdom will not be made to appear before them. And if a king, a king's son, a king's great minister, or a king's ministers who have come to seek an opening against them and search for a vulnerability should draw near, those ministers and kings will not find any opening — and this is because this Perfection of Wisdom has been thoroughly held. Even should a king, a king's son, a king's great minister, or a king's ministers draw near, those persons will find themselves wishing to speak and desiring to converse, thinking: "May we truly gladden them."

Why is this? Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom abides in proximity to all sentient beings, cultivating loving-kindness through a mind of loving-kindness and cultivating compassion through a mind of compassion toward them all.

Kauśika, for this very reason, even if sons of good family or daughters of good family have entered into a wilderness thronged with venomous creatures and many poisonous serpents, those humans or non-humans who seek an opening against them and look for a vulnerability will, Kauśika, find no opening against them — except through the maturation of previous karma.

Now at that time, certain wandering tīrthikas (non-Buddhists), their minds set on seeking a pretext for fault-finding, came to where the Blessed One was.

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, seeing those wandering tīrthikas approaching from afar, examined their minds and thought: "These wandering tīrthikas have come to where the Blessed One is with the intention of seeking a pretext for fault-finding. By all means I must prevent these wandering tīrthikas from drawing near into the presence of the Blessed One."

"Whatever portion of this Perfection of Wisdom I have retained from the Blessed One's presence — that much I shall call to mind, reflect upon, recite, and proclaim aloud. Having done so, there will be no obstacles to the teaching of this Perfection of Wisdom." With that thought, Śakra, lord of the gods, called to mind however much of this Perfection of Wisdom he had retained from the Blessed One's presence, reflected upon it, recited it, and proclaimed it aloud.

Then those wandering tīrthikas, from a distance even greater than before, circumambulated the Blessed One and departed by that same gate and that same road.

Then the venerable Śāriputra thought to himself: "Why is it that those wandering tīrthikas, from a distance even greater than before, circumambulated the Blessed One and then departed by that same gate and that same road?"

The Blessed One, knowing with his mind the nature of the venerable Śāriputra's thought, spoke to the venerable Śāriputra as follows: "Śāriputra, Śakra, lord of the gods, saw the minds of those wandering tīrthikas who had come with the intention of seeking a pretext for fault-finding, and thought: 'It would not be right for them to create obstacles to the teaching of this Perfection of Wisdom; by all means let those wandering tīrthikas not come while the Perfection of Wisdom is being taught.' In order to turn back those wandering tīrthikas who wished to argue, who wished to dispute, and who wished to contradict, he called to mind this Perfection of Wisdom, reflected upon it, recited it, and proclaimed it aloud. And just as Śakra, lord of the gods, did, so too have I permitted it. Why is that? Śāriputra, I do not see even one wholesome quality in any of those wandering tīrthikas. Śāriputra, every one of them came wishing to do so with minds bent on fault-finding and filled with hostility."

Then Māra the Evil One thought to himself: "These four assemblies of the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly and fully awakened Buddha, are gathered and assembled in the presence of the Tathāgata, and the sons of the gods dwelling in the desire realm and the form realm are also present here. Surely bodhisattvas, great beings, will here be prophesied to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. I shall draw near to disrupt this with my eyes."

Then Māra the Evil One conjured forth a fourfold army host and drew near to where the Blessed One was.

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, thought: "Alas! These are Māra the Evil One, who has conjured forth a fourfold army host and come to where the Blessed One is. Yet this array of the fourfold army host is not the array of the fourfold army host of King Prasenarasāra, nor the array of the fourfold army host of King Prasenajit, nor the array of the fourfold army host of the Śākyas, nor the array of the fourfold army host of the Licchavīs. This has been conjured forth by Māra the Evil One.

Māra the Evil One has, from time without beginning, been watching for an opportunity against the Blessed One, seeking out a vulnerability, and has been bound up with the intention to cause harm to sentient beings. Now, by calling this Perfection of Wisdom to mind, I shall contemplate it, recite it, and proclaim it aloud."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, called this very Perfection of Wisdom to mind, contemplated it, recited it, and proclaimed it aloud.
