And in exact proportion as Śakra, lord of the gods, called the Perfection of Wisdom to mind, contemplated it, recited it, and proclaimed it aloud, so in that same proportion Māra the Evil One turned back through that very gate and by that very road and departed.

Then the sons of the gods of the Heaven of the Thirty-Three manifested divine flowers, the māndārava blossoms, and remaining in the sky amid the air, scattered and strewed those divine māndārava flowers upon the Blessed One. And they proclaimed aloud: "How wonderful! This Perfection of Wisdom has come among the people of Jambudvīpa after such a long interval of time!" Having thus made that solemn declaration, they again took up the divine flowers and scattered and strewed them upon the Blessed One, and spoke these words:

"Blessed One, those sentient beings who expound the Perfection of Wisdom, who meditate upon it, and who practise the Perfection of Wisdom — these cannot be found vulnerable by Māra or by the gods of Māra's host. Blessed One, those sentient beings who listen to this Perfection of Wisdom, who having heard it retain it, uphold it, read it, master it thoroughly, proclaim it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, recite its scriptural transmission, and make it a recitation — those sentient beings, Blessed One, are not without roots of virtue accumulated from former times. And those sentient beings in whose ear-passages this Perfection of Wisdom has come to resound — they too have surely served the Victors of the past. How much more, then, those who retain it, uphold it, read it, master it thoroughly, proclaim it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, recite its scriptural transmission, make it a recitation, train in it as suchness, realize it as suchness, and practise it in yoga as suchness! Blessed One, those sentient beings have paid homage and service to the Tathāgatas. Why? Because it is from this Perfection of Wisdom that omniscience is fully revealed, and it is from this Perfection of Wisdom that omniscience is sought. Blessed One, consider this: just as every jewel and great jewel whatsoever is revealed by the great ocean, and all of them are sought from the great ocean — so too, Blessed One, the great jewel of omniscience is sought from this great ocean of the Perfection of Wisdom of the Tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly and completely awakened buddhas."

When this had been said, the Blessed One spoke thus to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, it is so, it is indeed so. The great jewel of omniscience of the Tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly and completely awakened buddhas arises from none other than this great ocean of the Perfection of Wisdom."

Then the venerable Ānanda spoke to the Blessed One and said:

"Blessed One, you do not praise the perfection of generosity nor proclaim its name in full. Nor do you praise the perfection of moral discipline, nor the perfection of patience, nor the perfection of diligence. Blessed One, you do not praise the perfection of meditative concentration nor proclaim its name in full. And yet, Blessed One, you praise the Perfection of Wisdom alone in full and proclaim its name in full."

The Blessed One declared: "It is so, Ānanda, it is indeed so. Ānanda, I praise and proclaim in full the name of the Perfection of Wisdom alone, and not the other perfections. And why is that? Ānanda, the Perfection of Wisdom goes before the five perfections, and it is for that reason that they obtain the name 'perfection.'

"Ānanda, what do you think — would giving that has not been dedicated to omniscience obtain the name 'perfection of generosity'?"

Ānanda replied: "Blessed One, it would not."

The Blessed One declared: "Ānanda, what do you think — would moral discipline that has not been so dedicated, patience that has not been so dedicated, diligence that has not been so dedicated, and meditative concentration that has not been so dedicated obtain that name? And Ānanda, what do you think — would wisdom that has not been dedicated to omniscience obtain the name 'Perfection of Wisdom'?"

Ānanda replied: "Blessed One, it would not."

The Blessed One declared: "Ānanda, what do you think — is the wisdom by which all roots of virtue are dedicated, dedicating them fully to omniscience, inconceivable?"

Ānanda replied: "Blessed One, it is so. Blessed One, that wisdom by which all roots of virtue are dedicated, dedicating them fully to omniscience, is indeed inconceivable. Blessed One, it is supremely inconceivable."

The Blessed One declared: "Ānanda, therefore, because it is supreme in that way, it obtains the name 'Perfection of Wisdom.' Those who have dedicated all roots of virtue to omniscience obtain the name 'perfection.' Ānanda, therefore, because the roots of virtue are dedicated fully to omniscience, the Perfection of Wisdom goes before the five perfections, leads them, and leads them completely. In this way, the five perfections are all subsumed within the Perfection of Wisdom alone. Ānanda, the very name 'Perfection of Wisdom' is the supreme expression for the complete fulfillment of all six perfections. Ānanda, therefore, when the Perfection of Wisdom is proclaimed in full, all six perfections are proclaimed in full.

"Ānanda, consider this analogy: seeds planted in the great earth will sprout when they meet with the right conditions, for the great earth is the ground of those seeds — it is in dependence upon the great earth that those seeds sprout. Ānanda, in just the same way, the five perfections, when properly taken up by the Perfection of Wisdom, are grounded in omniscience, and it is in dependence upon the Perfection of Wisdom that the five perfections arise. It is because they are fully taken up by the Perfection of Wisdom that they obtain the name 'perfection.'"

"Ānanda, therefore this very Perfection of Wisdom goes before the five perfections, leading and guiding them completely."

Then Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, all the qualities that a son of good family or a daughter of good family would fully acquire through holding, retaining, reading, mastering, reciting aloud, teaching, expounding, receiving scriptural transmission of, and memorizing this Perfection of Wisdom — all of these qualities of the Perfection of Wisdom have not been proclaimed in full by the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly and completely awakened buddha. Rather, what I myself have done is to take up and recite aloud just one portion of this Perfection of Wisdom as it comes from the Blessed One."

The Blessed One declared: "Excellent, excellent, Kauśika! And yet the qualities described are not limited to arising only in those who hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, expound, receive scriptural transmission of, and memorize this Perfection of Wisdom. Kauśika, any son of good family or daughter of good family who, wishing that the line of the buddhas may not be broken and that the sacred Dharma may not decline — wishing that the sacred Dharma may long endure — and who, without departing from proper conduct, acts to accomplish benefit for great bodhisattvas, writes out this Perfection of Wisdom, places it in a volume, and keeps or enshrines it; who then makes offerings to that Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, unguents, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, garlands of lamps encircling it all about, and manifold other offerings; who pays it homage, honors it, venerates it, worships it, makes painted images of it, and renders it service — Kauśika, it is the qualities accruing to such a son of good family or daughter of good family in this very life that I have described."

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, Śakra, lord of the gods, addressed him with these words: "Blessed One, I too shall write out this Perfection of Wisdom, place it in a volume, keep or enshrine it, wishing that the line of the buddhas may not be broken and that the sacred Dharma may not decline — wishing that the sacred Dharma may long endure — and without departing from proper conduct, to accomplish benefit for great bodhisattvas. And I shall make offerings to that Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, unguents, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, garlands of lamps encircling it all about, and manifold other offerings; I shall pay it homage, honor it, venerate it, worship it, make painted images of it, and render it service. And if I shall protect, guard, and shelter any son of good family or daughter of good family who does the same — who asks about, holds, retains, reads, masters, recites aloud, teaches, expounds, receives scriptural transmission of, and memorizes this Perfection of Wisdom, and who likewise makes offerings with flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, unguents, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, garlands of lamps encircling it all about, and manifold other offerings, paying homage, honoring, venerating, worshipping, making painted images, and rendering service — then how much more need be said?"

The Blessed One declared: "Excellent, excellent, Kauśika! Moreover, Kauśika, when any son of good family or daughter of good family recites this Perfection of Wisdom aloud, many hundreds of divine sons will come to that very place. Many thousands of divine sons, and many hundreds of thousands of divine sons, will draw near in order to hear the Dharma. As those divine sons listen to the Dharma, they will think to themselves: 'We shall lend our support to the confidence of this Dharma-speaker.' And even at a moment when the Dharma-speaker wishes to stop speaking, those divine sons, moved by that very reverence for the Dharma, will think to lend their support to the confidence of that son of good family or daughter of good family, just as they aspire that he or she continue speaking.

Kauśika, any son of good family or daughter of good family who holds, retains, reads, masters, recites aloud, teaches, expounds, receives scriptural transmission of, and memorizes this Perfection of Wisdom — they too will fully possess these virtues in this very life.

Kauśika, furthermore, when that son of good family or daughter of good family is expounding this Perfection of Wisdom, even if someone approaches with the intention of seeking out a fault in them, that person will not become faint-hearted before the assembly, thinking: 'This is not right.' Why is that? Because the Perfection of Wisdom guards, shelters, and conceals them. One who abides in the Perfection of Wisdom cannot truly be found to have any fault. One who abides in the Perfection of Wisdom cannot truly be found to be seeking out faults. The one in whom a fault is to be sought cannot truly be found. The Perfection of Wisdom itself cannot truly be found. Thus, through this category of reasoning, no refutation whatsoever will arise against a son of good family or daughter of good family who is fully upheld by the Perfection of Wisdom. And this will not cause fear and trembling — neither in people nor in me.

Kauśika, any son of good family or daughter of good family who holds, retains, reads, masters, recites aloud, teaches, expounds, receives scriptural transmission of, and memorizes this Perfection of Wisdom will fully possess these virtues in this very life.

Kauśika, furthermore, such sons of good family or daughters of good family will become pleasing and agreeable to their parents, friends, ministers, relatives, kinsmen by birth, as well as to renunciants (śramaṇas) and brahmins. When opponents arise and men present challenges, they will have the power and ability to refute those opponents through the Dharma; and when questioned by others, they will be able to respond with scriptural authority.

Kauśika, any son of good family or daughter of good family who holds, retains, reads, masters, recites aloud, teaches, expounds, receives scriptural transmission of, and memorizes this Perfection of Wisdom will fully possess these virtues in this very life.

Kauśika, moreover, in whatever direction that son of good family or daughter of good family has written out this Perfection of Wisdom and placed it as a volume, preceded by offerings — in that very place, Kauśika, whichever divine sons among the gods of the realm of the Four Great Kings have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to come there.

They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it. Having gazed upon it, paid homage, bowed down, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, moreover, whichever divine sons among the gods of the Thirty-Three have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to come there. They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it. Having gazed upon it, paid homage, bowed down, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, moreover, whichever divine sons among the gods of the Heaven Free from Strife have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to draw near and come there. They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it. Having gazed upon it, paid homage, bowed down, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, moreover, whichever divine sons among the gods of the Joyous Heaven have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to come there. They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it. Having gazed upon it, paid homage, bowed down, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, moreover, whichever divine sons among the gods of the Heaven of Delight in Creation have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to come there. They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it.

gaze upon it, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, moreover, whichever divine sons among the gods of the Heaven of Wielding Others' Creations have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to come there. They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it, and having gazed upon it, paid homage to it, bowed down before it, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, likewise, among all the divine sons dwelling in the form realm gods, from whatever number there may be up to those in the Brahma world — whichever divine sons of the Brahmā class have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to come there. They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it, and having gazed upon it, paid homage to it, bowed down before it, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, you should not think of this place merely in terms of the Brahmā class alone. Just as with the Brahmā class, so too apply the same in full, as before, for whichever divine sons there may be among the gods called Brahmaḥpurohita. And likewise for Great Brahmā, the gods of Minor Radiance, of Immeasurable Radiance, of Resplendent Light, of Minor Virtue, of Immeasurable Virtue, of Abundant Virtue, of Cloudless Heaven, of Increasing Merit, of Great Fruit, of the Beings Without Perception, of Non-Turbulent, of Unafflicted, of Superb Appearance, of Clarity of Vision — and, Kauśika, among the gods of Akaniṣṭha, whichever divine sons have genuinely entered upon unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening will think to come there. They too will come there and will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it has been committed to a volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it, and having gazed upon it, paid homage to it, bowed down before it, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, a son of good family or a daughter of good family should generate the following thought: toward all those gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas (dri za), asuras, garuḍas (nam mkha' lding), kinnaras (mi 'am ci), mahoragas, and non-human beings dwelling in the immeasurable and innumerable world-systems in the ten directions —

"Look upon the Perfection of Wisdom in this volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it." Having gazed upon it, paid homage to it, bowed down before it, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to return to their own abodes. One should think: "May this very gift of Dharma be given to all of them."

Kauśika, you should not think that only those sons of the gods dwelling in the desire realm and the form realm within this very world-system of the four great continents who have genuinely entered into unsurpassable, perfect, complete awakening will come there. Kauśika, one should not regard it in that way.

Kauśika, rather, however many sons of the gods dwelling in the desire realm and the form realm throughout the great trichiliocosm who have genuinely entered into unsurpassable, perfect, complete awakening — all of them too will think to come there. And having come there, they will gaze upon this Perfection of Wisdom as it abides in the volume, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it. Having gazed upon it, paid homage to it, bowed down before it, held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, expounded it, received scriptural transmission of it, and memorized it, they will then think to depart.

Kauśika, the home, dwelling place, and mansion of that son of good family or daughter of good family will be most excellently protected, and no one will cause harm to that person — save for the maturation of previous karma. Kauśika, this is because gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings come there, drawn by the thought that there is great splendor, great splendor — called "Great Splendor" — in that place. Kauśika, that son of good family or daughter of good family will fully possess these qualities in this very life as well.

When this was said, Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods) addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, how may a son of good family or a daughter of good family arrange for gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings to come there in order to listen to this Perfection of Wisdom, to gaze upon it, pay homage to it, bow down before it, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, receive scriptural transmission of it, and memorize it?"

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke to Śakra, lord of the gods, as follows: "Kauśika, if a son of good family or a daughter of good family sees great illuminations there, that son of good family or daughter of good family should understand: 'Gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings have surely come and arrived here.' Kauśika, furthermore, if that son of good family or daughter of good family smells a fragrance of non-human beings never smelled before,

having smelled that fragrance, that son of good family or daughter of good family should understand: "Gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings have surely come and arrived here."

Kauśika, furthermore, if that son of good family or daughter of good family should find their conduct becoming pure and their conduct becoming clean, then by reason of that pure and clean conduct, gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings will wish to come there. And those gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings who have come there will be pleased, delighted, and gladdened by that pure and clean conduct, and joy and happiness will arise in them. Then those gods of lesser splendor who dwell there will feel moved to depart. Why is that? Because those gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings of great splendor — those very ones of great splendor — with their glory, majesty, and dignity being unbearable, those gods of lesser splendor will accordingly feel moved to depart.

Kauśika, furthermore, just as those gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-human beings of great splendor — those of great splendor — always wish to come, so too that son of good family or daughter of good family will come to have many devoted followers. Kauśika, that son of good family or daughter of good family will also fully possess these qualities in this very life.

Kauśika, furthermore, that son of good family or daughter of good family, in order to honour, revere, and make complete offerings in that place of the Dharma, should not engage in conduct that is impure or unclean in the environs of that place of the Dharma.

Kauśika, furthermore, that son of good family or daughter of good family will not become fatigued in body or weary in mind. They will sleep comfortably and move about comfortably. Even when they lie down to sleep, they will not see evil dreams. And if they do see something, they will see none other than Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas. They will see none other than stūpas. They will see none other than bodhisattvas. They will see none other than the śrāvakas of the Tathāgata. When they hear sounds, they will hear none other than the sounds of the perfections. They will hear the Dharma teachings of the factors of awakening. They will see none other than trees of awakening, and at those very trees they will see Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas, achieving complete and perfect awakening. Likewise they will see those who have become completely and perfectly awakened turning the wheel of Dharma. They will see great bodhisattvas, great beings, many in number, who delight in discoursing on the Perfection of Wisdom, themselves discoursing on this very Perfection of Wisdom and demonstrating with skillful means how omniscience is to be fully taken up and how buddha-fields are to be completely purified. They will also hear the great sound of the Blessed One buddhas achieving manifest awakening.

They will also hear the great sound of the buddhas — the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and fully awakened buddha named "The Very Great One" — who, in a certain direction, in a certain quarter of that direction, in a certain world-system, is teaching the Dharma surrounded by and attended upon by many śrāvakas and bodhisattvas, by many hundreds of śrāvakas and bodhisattvas, by many thousands, by many hundreds of thousands, by many tens of millions, by many hundreds of tens of millions, by many thousands of tens of millions, by many hundreds of thousands of tens of millions, and by many hundreds of thousands of hundreds of millions of śrāvakas and bodhisattvas.

O Kauśika, that son of good family or daughter of good family will see dreams of this kind and nature. They will sleep comfortably and wake comfortably. Radiance will suffuse their body from within. They will experience very pleasant food, and they will experience a sense of lightness and spaciousness. No stream of craving thoughts toward food arising at an unsuitable time will arise in them, and their perception of food will grow slight. O Kauśika, just as a monk who is a practitioner of Yogācāra, whose mind is thoroughly saturated with the intention that arises from meditative absorption, does not experience fierce craving for food and his perception of food grows slight — so too, O Kauśika, in that son of good family or daughter of good family, fierce craving for food will not arise, and their perception of food will grow slight. Why is that? O Kauśika, it is because, however that son of good family or daughter of good family strives in the yoga of cultivating the Perfection of Wisdom, to that same degree non-human beings will set their minds on replenishing the radiance of that person's body.

O Kauśika, that son of good family or daughter of good family will also come to fully possess these qualities in this very lifetime.

O Kauśika, furthermore, a son of good family or daughter of good family who writes out this Perfection of Wisdom, has it put into a volume, places it having preceded it with offerings, and venerates it — yet does not take it up, does not bear it, does not read it, does not master it thoroughly, does not recite it aloud, does not teach it, does not explain it in detail, does not chant it from scripture, and does not commit it to recitation — even so, O Kauśika, that son of good family or daughter of good family generates abundant merit. But the son of good family or daughter of good family who has faith and confidence in this Perfection of Wisdom, who reflects on it and is devoted to it with a wholehearted mind, who has generated the awakening mind oriented toward awakening, and who with elevated aspiration listens to it, takes it up, bears it, reads it, masters it thoroughly, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, chants it from scripture, commits it to recitation, teaches it to others as well in its full extent and correctly, elucidates its meaning, reflects on it with the mind, and thoroughly investigates it here with the wisdom of understanding just how things are established — who, with the thought "May the stream of the buddhas' tradition not be cut off! May the noble Dharma not decline!" acts to make the noble Dharma abide for a long time, and, without being separated from the proper conduct, accomplishes the work of bringing benefit to bodhisattvas, great beings — even if that person has the text put into a volume while still young and merely bears or places it —

that person also makes offerings to the Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, and surrounding garlands of lamps in all directions, together with many other kinds of offering. One who pays honor, homage, reverence, veneration, and worship to the Perfection of Wisdom, and shows devotion to it — that very son or daughter of good family will on that basis generate merit far more abundantly. Therefore, Kauśika, a son or daughter of good family who wishes to fully possess in this very life these remarkable qualities should arouse faith in this Perfection of Wisdom itself, thoroughly examine it, cultivate deep aspiration for it, and generate the awakening mind wholeheartedly. With elevated intention one should listen to it, take it up, retain it, read it, master it thoroughly, recite it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, transmit it through scriptural authority, recite it by heart, and expound it fully and correctly to others; one should elucidate its meaning, reflect on it with the mind, and thoroughly investigate it here with the wisdom of understanding just how things are established. With the thought "May the stream of the buddhas' tradition not be cut off! May the noble Dharma not decline!" one should act to make the noble Dharma abide for a long time, and without being separated from proper conduct, accomplish the work of bringing benefit to bodhisattvas, great beings — even if as a young person one has the text put into a volume and merely bears or places it. It should also be properly distributed to those sons and daughters of good family who are earnestly seeking its meaning. For oneself and for other virtuous sentient beings, it will bring the result of not being separated from the great eye of the buddhas' tradition. Therefore one should at all times make offerings to it with divine flowers, divine incense, divine perfume, divine garlands, divine ointments, divine powders, divine robes, divine parasols, divine victory banners, divine bells, divine pennants, surrounding garlands of divine lamps, and many other kinds of offering. One should honor it, pay homage to it, revere it, venerate it, worship it, and show it devotion.

This concludes the third chapter, called "Holding the Immeasurable Qualities of Honoring the Perfection of Wisdom and Stūpas," from the Noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines.

Furthermore, the Blessed One declared to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, suppose this entire Jambudvīpa were filled to overflowing with the physical forms of Tathāgatas and someone gave them to you; and suppose this Perfection of Wisdom were also written out and entrusted to you, and then someone were to take away either portion while leaving the other — Kauśika, which of the two portions would you take?"

Śakra replied: "Blessed One, if this entire Jambudvīpa were filled to overflowing with the physical forms of Tathāgatas and bestowed upon me, and this Perfection of Wisdom were also written out and entrusted to me, and then either portion were taken away while the other remained — Blessed One, I would take this very Perfection of Wisdom itself. And why? Because it is for the sake of depicting the Tathāgata's tradition in image. This is the true body of meaning of the Tathāgata. And why so? Because the Blessed One has declared that buddhas, Blessed Ones, are the dharmakāya — the truth body. He has said, 'Monks, do not cling to this as the supreme body; monks, look upon me as the dharmakāya, perfectly complete.' Therefore this physical form of the Tathāgata too should be regarded as fully analyzed by true reality — that is to say, as the Perfection of Wisdom. Blessed One, nor do I fail to show reverence to those physical forms of the Tathāgata."

"Blessed One, it is indeed I who show reverence to those physical forms of the Tathāgata. Blessed One, yet it is from this Perfection of Wisdom that the physical forms of the Tathāgata have arisen and have thereby come to receive honor. Blessed One, therefore, by paying reverence to this Perfection of Wisdom itself, one will also have completely fulfilled reverence to those physical forms of the Tathāgata. And why so? Because the physical forms of the Tathāgata have arisen from the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, consider this analogy. When I, Dharmasubha the god, am present on my own divine throne in my own abode, the sons of the gods come to render service to me. But when I am absent, those sons of the gods, out of reverence for me, bow to that throne of mine, circumambulate it, and then withdraw. And why so? Because they think, 'Śakra, lord of the gods, sits upon this throne and teaches the Dharma to the gods of the Thirty-Three.'

Blessed One, in precisely the same way, the Perfection of Wisdom — which has come to be renowned for its great power and which serves as the cause and condition — brings about omniscience for the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and completely awakened buddhas. The physical forms of the Tathāgata have become the abode of omniscience; yet those forms are not themselves the cause that gives rise to wisdom, nor are they its condition. Blessed One, thus it is that reverence paid to the physical forms of the Tathāgata arises from the cause of omniscient wisdom.

Blessed One, therefore, between those two portions, I choose this Perfection of Wisdom itself. Blessed One, I am not without reverence for those physical forms of the Tathāgata — Blessed One, it is indeed I who show reverence to those physical forms of the Tathāgata. Yet those physical forms of the Tathāgata have come to receive honor through being completely nourished by the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, let alone Jambudvīpa filled brimful with physical forms of the Tathāgata — let alone the world-system of the four great continents filled brimful with physical forms of the Tathāgata, let alone a thousand world-systems filled brimful with physical forms of the Tathāgata, let alone the two-thousand middling world-systems filled brimful with physical forms of the Tathāgata. Blessed One, suppose this very great trichiliocosm — the three-thousand-great-thousand world-system — were filled brimful with physical forms of the Tathāgata, gathered together and set on one side, and this Perfection of Wisdom were written out and placed as the second portion, and one were then told, 'Of these two portions, take whichever you wish.' Blessed One, in that case I would choose this Perfection of Wisdom itself. Blessed One, I am not without reverence for those physical forms of the Tathāgata — Blessed One, it is indeed I who show reverence to those physical forms of the Tathāgata. Yet those physical forms of the Tathāgata too have come to receive honor through being completely nourished by the Perfection of Wisdom. The physical forms of the Tathāgata have become the abode of omniscient wisdom, and that omniscient wisdom itself issues forth definitively from the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, therefore, between those two portions, I choose this Perfection of Wisdom itself."

Blessed One, I am not without reverence for those physical forms of the Tathāgata — Blessed One, it is indeed I who show reverence to those physical forms of the Tathāgata. Yet, Blessed One, those physical forms of the Tathāgata have also arisen from this Perfection of Wisdom itself, and the honor those physical forms of the Tathāgata have come to receive is precisely this: they have been completely nourished by the Perfection of Wisdom.

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines. The Fifth Fascicle.

Blessed One, consider, for example, a precious jewel of priceless value, possessed of qualities such as these: wherever it is placed, neither humans nor non-humans can find an opening to cause harm. In whatever place a man or woman has been seized by a non-human, the moment that great precious jewel is given there, the non-human departs at once from that place. When placed upon a body afflicted and swollen with bile-fever, it cuts off that bile and prevents it from spreading, and brings it to peace. When placed upon a body that has become hot with bile, it cuts off that bile too and prevents it from spreading, and brings it to peace. When placed upon one swollen and seized throughout with phlegm, it cuts off that phlegm as well and prevents it from spreading, and brings it to peace. When placed upon a body suffering from a wind-disorder, it cuts off that wind-disorder too and prevents it from spreading, and brings it to peace. In the darkness of a dark night, it causes light to shine. In time of heat, whatever region it is placed in becomes cool. In time of cold, whatever region it is placed in becomes warm. In whatever region there are venomous snakes and likewise other small creatures, if it is held and placed there, those venomous snakes and those small creatures will depart. Blessed One, if a man or a woman has been seized by a venomous snake and that precious jewel is shown to them, the moment they see the precious jewel their poison is overcome and ceases to exist. Blessed One, that precious jewel is also endowed with those qualities and other such natures as well.

Blessed One, for those whose eyes are afflicted with cataracts, or dimness of sight, or eye-disease, or film over the eye, if that precious jewel is placed upon their eyes, the moment it is placed there those eye-defects are cleared away and become pacified. Blessed One, that precious jewel is endowed with those qualities and with others like them as well.

Whatever water it is placed into takes on a single color, its own color. If it is wrapped in white cloth and placed in water, that water also becomes white. Likewise, if it is wrapped in blue cloth, or yellow cloth, or red cloth, or crimson cloth, or any other of the many various kinds of cloth — whatever cloth it may be wrapped or bound in and placed in water — that water also takes on the respective natural color of those cloths, and whatever turbidity there is in that water also becomes clear. Blessed One, that precious jewel is endowed with qualities of just such a nature.

Then the venerable Ānanda spoke these words to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, are those precious jewels found only in the world of the gods, or are those precious jewels also found among the humans of Jambudvīpa?"

Śakra replied:

"Noble Ānanda, those precious jewels are indeed found among the gods. The humans of Jambudvīpa also have precious jewels, yet those jewels are heavy, small, and coarse, and their qualities are incomplete — they do not possess qualities of that kind of nature. They do not come even close to a hundredth part of those precious jewels of the gods, not to a thousandth, not to a hundred-thousandth, not to a ten-millionth, not to a billion, not to a hundred billion, not to a trillion, not to a hundred trillion. They cannot be reached by counting, by analogy, by illustration through example, by serving as a basis of comparison, or by serving as a proximate cause. Those precious jewels of the gods are perfectly and completely endowed with every quality and dimension.

When that precious jewel is placed into a casket or taken out of it, even after it has been removed, that casket becomes exceedingly dear and beloved — the qualities of that precious jewel cause the casket to become supremely cherished.

Blessed One, in just the same way, one who venerates the physical relics of a Tathāgata, an arhat, a perfectly and completely awakened buddha who has passed into nirvāṇa — that veneration is a quality of the Perfection of Wisdom and of the wisdom of omniscience. These physical relics of the Tathāgata have become vessels of the wisdom of omniscience.

Blessed One, just as the teaching of Dharma by all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones, in every world-realm is venerated because it arises from the Perfection of Wisdom, so too the teaching of Dharma by a Dharma-preacher is to be venerated because it arises from the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, consider how a king's man, by the power of the king, is paid reverence without fear by great multitudes of people. In just the same way, a Dharma-preacher, by the power of the Dharma body, is paid reverence without fear by great multitudes of beings.

In the manner that a Dharma-preacher who teaches the Dharma obtains veneration, in that same manner veneration is obtained through those physical relics of the Tathāgata.

Blessed One, therefore, let alone a great trichiliocosm filled to the brim with the physical relics of the Tathāgata — Blessed One, even if world-realms as numerous as the grains of sand of the Ganges River, all filled to the brim with physical relics of the Tathāgata, were placed on one side, and this Perfection of Wisdom, written out, were placed on the other side, and if someone were to say to me, 'Of these two portions set before you, take whichever one you wish' — of those two portions, I myself would take this very Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, it is not that I am disrespectful toward those physical relics of the Tathāgata — Blessed One, I do indeed hold reverence for those physical relics of the Tathāgata. However, Blessed One, omniscience itself is clearly derived from the Perfection of Wisdom, and the veneration of the physical relics of the Tathāgata arises from the wisdom of omniscience.

Blessed One, therefore, when one venerates this Perfection of Wisdom, one thereby venerates all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones — those of the past, those yet to come, and those of the present."

"Blessed One, furthermore, sons of good family or daughters of good family who wish to behold the buddhas, the Blessed Ones, who at this very moment dwell, live, and abide in immeasurable, incalculable world-systems — those persons should practice this Perfection of Wisdom, should devote themselves to yoga in this Perfection of Wisdom, should meditate upon this Perfection of Wisdom."

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke to Śakra (Indra), Lord of the Gods, as follows:

"Kauśika, it is exactly so, it is exactly so. Kauśika, all the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas who arose in times past — they too, Kauśika, relying upon this very Perfection of Wisdom, manifestly and fully awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. Kauśika, all the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas who shall arise in times to come — they too, Kauśika, relying upon this very Perfection of Wisdom, shall manifestly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. Kauśika, all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones, who at this very present time dwell, live, and abide in immeasurable, incalculable world-systems — they too, Kauśika, relying upon this very Perfection of Wisdom, manifestly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. Kauśika, I myself, the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and fully awakened buddha of the present, have also, relying upon this very Perfection of Wisdom, manifestly and fully awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening."

When this was declared, Śakra, Lord of the Gods, said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is indeed the great perfection. Blessed One, the Tathāgata, arhat, perfectly and fully awakened buddha thoroughly knows and rightly beholds the mental conduct of all sentient beings."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, it is exactly so, it is exactly so. Kauśika, when a bodhisattva, a great being, practices the Perfection of Wisdom for a long time, that one also rightly knows and rightly beholds the mental conduct of all sentient beings through the Perfection of Wisdom."

Then Śakra, Lord of the Gods, said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, does a bodhisattva, a great being, practice only the Perfection of Wisdom alone, and not the other perfections?"

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, a bodhisattva, a great being, practices all six perfections. However, Kauśika, it is none other than this Perfection of Wisdom of the bodhisattva, the great being, that leads the way before all of them. It is this very Perfection of Wisdom of the bodhisattva, the great being — the one who practices generosity, upholds moral discipline, cultivates patience, undertakes diligent effort, enters into meditative concentration, and views phenomena rightly — that goes ahead of all these."

Kauśika, among these six perfections, when they are gathered up by skillful means and dedicated through the Perfection of Wisdom, and dedicated toward omniscience, no distinction whatsoever is perceived among them, nor is any separateness to be found.

Kauśika, here is an analogy: the many and various trees of Jambudvīpa differ in color, differ in shape, differ in their leaves, differ in their flowers, differ in their fruits, and differ in the fullness of their canopies — yet the shadows of those trees show no distinction among themselves, nor is any separateness apparent. In other respects they go by the single term "shadow, shadow." In just the same way, Kauśika, among these six perfections, when they are gathered up by skillful means and dedicated through the Perfection of Wisdom, and dedicated toward omniscience, no distinction whatsoever is perceived among them, nor is any separateness to be found.

When this had been declared, Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra), addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is endowed with great qualities. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is endowed with immeasurable qualities. Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is endowed with qualities without limit."

This has been the fourth chapter, entitled "A Full Account of the Qualities," from the noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines.

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, whatever son of good family or daughter of good family, with genuine faith in this Perfection of Wisdom, reflects upon it, aspires to it, arouses the mind toward awakening with wholehearted engagement, and then, with elevated intention, listens to it, retains it, upholds it, reads it, makes it fully comprehensible, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it, transmits it by scriptural reading, chants it, and teaches it in full and correctly to others — and who also explicates its meaning, considers it with the mind, and investigates it here with the wisdom of established understanding as it truly is — and who, thinking 'may the lineage of the buddhas not be cut off, may the holy Dharma not decline,' works to establish the holy Dharma's long abiding, and, without departing from proper conduct, strives to accomplish benefit for bodhisattvas, great beings — even if, while still young, they have had it written into a book and carry it or preserve it, and having heard this teaching so set forth, think: 'How great is the meaning of this Perfection of Wisdom! How great are its benefits! How great is its fruit! How great is its karmic ripening! How great are its many qualities! I will not abandon this Perfection of Wisdom. I will guard this Perfection of Wisdom. I will cherish this Perfection of Wisdom. This Perfection of Wisdom is exceedingly difficult to find' — and who, with such intensified aspiration, themselves venerate this Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, fragrance, garlands, ointments, powders, robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, pennants, and rings of butter-lamp garlands surrounding it, along with offerings of many kinds — and who honor it, exalt it, revere it, venerate it, worship it, and serve it —

And if another were to have it copied into a book and offer veneration to it, and if a son or daughter of good family, aspiring and seeking for the benefit of others, were to give it away, present it, and make a complete gift of it, then, Blessed One, which of these two — a son or daughter of good family who possesses the resolve to give, and one who does not possess the resolve to give — which of the two generates the greater merit?

When this was asked, the Blessed One spoke these words to Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods: "Kauśika, on this very point I put the question to you — declare it as best you can. Kauśika, what do you think? There is a son or daughter of good family who honors and venerates, holds and reveres a Tathāgata's reliquary that they themselves have treated with honor — who honors it, exalts it, reveres it, worships it, has it depicted, and venerates it. And there is another son or daughter of good family who likewise honors, exalts, reveres, worships, has depicted, and venerates a Tathāgata's reliquary, thinking 'May the offerings become vast' — and out of loving affection for sentient beings, broadly and correctly teaches others as well, gives it to them, and distributes it fully. Of these two sons or daughters of good family, Kauśika, which generates the greater merit? Is it the one who makes offerings themselves and also broadly and correctly teaches others, gives it to them, and distributes it fully? Or is it the one who alone, on their own, makes offerings only for themselves?"

Śakra replied: "Blessed One, a son or daughter of good family who themselves honors, exalts, reveres, worships, has depicted, and venerates the Tathāgata's reliquary, thinking 'May the offerings become vast,' and who out of loving affection for sentient beings also broadly and correctly teaches others, gives it to them, and distributes it fully — of these two, this very one generates far greater merit."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, it is so — it is indeed so. Kauśika, a son or daughter of good family who writes out this Perfection of Wisdom, has it put into a book, retains it, bears it, reads it, thoroughly masters it, recites it aloud, teaches it, explains it in detail, transmits it through scriptural recitation, and chants it — and who, aspiring and seeking for the benefit of other sons and daughters of good family, even has it put into a small book and gives it, presents it, and makes a complete gift of it — Kauśika, of these two sons or daughters of good family, the one who cares for other sons and daughters of good family and who possesses the resolve of complete giving generates far greater merit thereby than the former basis.

Furthermore, Kauśika, a son or daughter of good family who goes to wherever any son or daughter of good family who has become a vessel for this Perfection of Wisdom may be staying, and there gives this Perfection of Wisdom to them and distributes it correctly — this very one, Kauśika, generates far greater merit than that."

"Furthermore, Kauśika, suppose some son or daughter of good family were to establish and settle all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa — however many there may be — perfectly on the path of the ten virtuous actions of karma. Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family thereby generate a great deal of merit?"

Śakra (Indra, king of the gods) replied: "A great deal, Blessed One. A great deal, Sugata."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, yet this son or daughter of good family generates even more merit than that — namely, whoever takes even a small portion of this Perfection of Wisdom and commits it to a volume, and then, to one who has genuine faith, gives with genuine faith; to one who has clear investigation, gives with clear investigation; to one who has deep aspiration, gives with deep aspiration; to one who has a pure mind, gives with a pure mind; to one who has excellent superior intention, gives with excellent superior intention — and who, with superior intention, gives it to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind toward awakening; and who, even in a small amount, works without laziness at questioning and reading it; and who diligently leads that person to uphold it, correctly teaches it, correctly sets it forth, correctly praises it, correctly causes delight in it, expounds it in words, illustrates it fully, and points it out again and again; and who correctly teaches the meaning of this to that person and speaks to them thus: 'Come here, son of good family. Learn this path of the bodhisattva in the following way. If you train in this path, practice it, and apply yourself, you will swiftly come to fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed and complete awakening. And having fully and perfectly become a buddha, you will guide the immeasurable realm of sentient beings — in this supreme manner, which is the complete exhaustion of the aggregates — to taming through the complete analysis of the true limit'; and who in this way purifies that person's mind and makes them free from doubt — Kauśika, this son or daughter of good family who possesses the resolve of complete giving generates far greater merit than the former.

Kauśika, now set aside all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa. Kauśika, in this same way, consider all the sentient beings — however many there may be — in the world-system of the four great continents: some son or daughter of good family who perfectly establishes and settles all of them on the path of the ten virtuous actions of karma — and Kauśika, now set aside all the sentient beings of the world-system of the four great continents.

Kauśika, in that same way, consider all the sentient beings — however many there may be — in the world-system of a thousand worlds, the first of a thousand: some son or daughter of good family who perfectly establishes and settles all of them on the path of the ten virtuous actions of karma — and Kauśika, now set aside all the sentient beings of the world-system of a thousand worlds.

Kauśika, in that same way, consider all the sentient beings — however many there may be — in the world-system of the two-thousand middling worlds: some son or daughter of good family who perfectly establishes and settles all of them on the path of the ten virtuous actions of karma — and Kauśika, now set aside all the sentient beings of the world-system of the two-thousand middling worlds.

Kauśika, in that same way, consider all the sentient beings — however many there may be — in the world-system of the great trichiliocosm of three-thousand worlds: some son or daughter of good family who perfectly establishes and settles all of them on the path of the ten virtuous actions of karma — and Kauśika, now set aside all the sentient beings of the world-system of the great trichiliocosm of three-thousand worlds."

"Kauśika, in that same way, consider however many sentient beings there may be in as many world-systems of the great trichiliocosm of three-thousand worlds as there are grains of sand in the river Ganges: if some son or daughter of good family were to perfectly establish and settle each and every one of them on the path of the ten virtuous actions of karma — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family thereby generate great merit?"

Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods) replied: "Greatly so, Blessed One. Greatly so, Sugata."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, yet even greater merit is generated by a son or daughter of good family who — having committed even a small portion of this Perfection of Wisdom to a volume — gives it with an excellent intention to a bodhisattva who has given rise to the awakening mind and who approaches it with conviction, who examines it with thorough reflection, who is drawn to it with heightened aspiration, who is clear of heart with a clear heart, and who is endowed with an excellent and heightened resolve; or who engages without fatigue in even a little copying and reciting of it; who with diligence leads another to take it up; who properly teaches it, properly sets it forth, properly extols it, and properly causes delight in it; who illuminates it in word, thoroughly illuminates it, and illuminates it in all its aspects; who properly expounds its meaning; and who speaks thus to another: 'Come here, son of good family — train in this path of the bodhisattva! If you train and practise and strive on this path, you will swiftly come to full and perfect awakening of unsurpassed, complete enlightenment. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will lead immeasurable realms of sentient beings — bringing their aggregates to complete exhaustion — to tame them in this unsurpassed way: the thorough discernment of the genuine limit' — and who in this way purifies that person's mind and removes all doubt. Kauśika, this son or daughter of good family who is endowed with a mind of complete relinquishment generates far greater merit than the earlier son or daughter of good family.

Kauśika, furthermore, if there are however many sentient beings in Jambudvīpa and some son or daughter of good family were to establish each and every one of them in the four meditative concentrations (dhyāna) — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family thereby generate great merit?"

Śakra replied: "Greatly so, Blessed One. Greatly so, Sugata."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, yet even greater merit is generated by a son or daughter of good family who — having committed even a small portion of this Perfection of Wisdom to a volume — gives it with an excellent intention to a bodhisattva who has given rise to the awakening mind, who approaches it with conviction, who examines it with thorough reflection, who is drawn to it with heightened aspiration, who is clear of heart, who is endowed with an excellent and heightened resolve; or who engages without fatigue in even a little copying and reciting of it; who with diligence leads another to take it up; who properly teaches it, properly sets it forth, properly extols it, and properly causes delight in it; who illuminates it in word, thoroughly illuminates it, and illuminates it in all its aspects; who properly expounds its meaning; and who speaks thus to another: 'Come here, son of good family — train in this path of the bodhisattva!'"

"If you train and practice and strive upon this path, you will swiftly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. And once you have fully awakened, you will discipline an immeasurable realm of sentient beings — those whose aggregates have completely exhausted — in this supreme opening of the true limit, the unsurpassed."

Speaking thus, one purifies that other's mind and makes it free of doubt.

"Kauśika, set aside all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa. Kauśika, if in the world-realm of the four great continents, however many sentient beings there are — all of them were to be established in the four meditative concentrations by some son or daughter of good family; and, Kauśika, set aside all the sentient beings of the world-realm of the four great continents — Kauśika, if in the world-realm of a thousand-fold universe, however many sentient beings there are — all of them were to be established in the four meditative concentrations by some son or daughter of good family; and, Kauśika, set aside all the sentient beings of the world-realm of the thousand-fold universe — Kauśika, if in the world-realm of a twice-thousand middling universe, however many sentient beings there are — all of them were to be established in the four meditative concentrations by some son or daughter of good family; and, Kauśika, set aside all the sentient beings of the world-realm of the twice-thousand middling universe — Kauśika, if in the world-realm of a great trichiliocosm, however many sentient beings there are — all of them were to be established in the four meditative concentrations by some son or daughter of good family; and, Kauśika, set aside all the sentient beings of the world-realm of the great trichiliocosm — Kauśika, if in world-realms as numerous as the sands of the Ganges River, however many great trichiliocosms there are, and however many sentient beings dwell within them — all of them were to be established in the four meditative concentrations by some son or daughter of good family: Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family thereby generate a great amount of merit?"

Śakra replied: "A great amount, Blessed One. A great amount, Sugata."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, even more merit than that will be generated by a son or daughter of good family who — even with only a small portion of this Perfection of Wisdom committed to a book — approaches with clear faith the one who has clear faith, approaches with heightened reflection the one who engages in heightened reflection, approaches with heightened aspiration the one who has heightened aspiration, approaches with clarity of heart the one who is clear of heart, approaches with excellent and heightened resolve the one who is endowed with excellent and heightened resolve, and with the awakening mind that has been aroused for awakening, approaches with heightened resolve the bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind; who engages without fatigue in even a little copying and reciting of it; who with diligence leads another to take it up; who properly teaches it, properly sets it forth, properly extols it, and properly causes delight in it; who illuminates it in word, thoroughly illuminates it, and illuminates it in all its aspects; who properly expounds its meaning; and who speaks thus to another: 'Come here, son of good family — train in this path of the bodhisattva! If you train and practice and strive upon this path, you will swiftly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening.'"

— and who, after fully awakening, then tames the immeasurable realm of sentient beings, leading them to that unsurpassed complete exhaustion of the aggregates, namely the thorough discernment of the true end of reality — who speaks thus and in that way purifies that person's mind and frees it from all doubt.

Kauśika, there is yet another point. However many sentient beings there are in Jambudvīpa, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all of them in the four immeasurables, and likewise, as with the immeasurables, were to establish them in the four formless absorptions and the five higher knowledges — from meditative concentration through the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and all the higher knowledges without exception — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family generate great merit from that basis?

Śakra replied: "A great amount, Blessed One. A great amount, Sugata."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, far greater still is the merit generated by that son or daughter of good family who, having committed even so much as a small portion of this Perfection of Wisdom to a book, then, with earnest faith, engages one of earnest faith; with thorough examination, engages one of thorough examination; with heightened aspiration, engages one of heightened aspiration; with a gladdened mind, engages one of gladdened mind; with perfect superior intention, engages one of perfect superior intention; and who, by arousing the mind of awakening, gives even a little to a bodhisattva whose mind of awakening has been aroused — who engages without fatigue in even a little copying and reciting of it; who with diligence leads another to take it up; who properly teaches it, properly sets it forth, properly extols it, and properly causes delight in it; who illuminates it in word, thoroughly illuminates it, and illuminates it in all its aspects; who properly expounds its meaning; and who speaks thus to another: 'Come here, son of good family — train in this path of the bodhisattva! If you train and practice and strive upon this path, you will swiftly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening.' And who, after fully awakening, then tames the immeasurable realm of sentient beings, leading them to that unsurpassed complete exhaustion of the aggregates, namely the thorough discernment of the true end of reality — who speaks thus and in that way purifies that person's mind and frees it from all doubt.

Kauśika, never mind the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa in meditative concentration, the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and the higher knowledges. Kauśika, in the same way, never mind the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all the sentient beings — however many there are — throughout a world-system of four great continents in meditative concentration, the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and the higher knowledges. Kauśika, again, never mind the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all the sentient beings — however many there are — throughout a world-system of four great continents in meditative concentration, the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and the higher knowledges. Kauśika, never mind the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all the sentient beings — however many there are — in a world-system of a thousand worlds

Kauśika, never mind the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all the sentient beings — however many there are — throughout a world-system of a thousand worlds, the chief among thousands, in meditative concentration, the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and the higher knowledges. Kauśika, never mind the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all the sentient beings — however many there are — in a world-system of two thousand middle worlds in meditative concentration, the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and the higher knowledges. Kauśika, never mind the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all the sentient beings — however many there are — in a great trichiliocosm in meditative concentration, the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and the higher knowledges. Kauśika, if, in the same way and by that same measure, some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings — however many there are — throughout world-systems as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River, each being a great trichiliocosm, in meditative concentration, the immeasurables, the formless absorptions, and the higher knowledges — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family thereby accumulate much merit?

Śakra replied: "Greatly, O Blessed One! Greatly, O Sugata!"

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, even more merit than that is accumulated by the son or daughter of good family who, having committed even a brief portion of this Perfection of Wisdom to a book, gives it — moved by sincere faith to one who has sincere faith, with thorough reflection to one who reflects thoroughly, with keen devotion to one who is keenly devoted, with a clear and joyful mind to one who has a clear and joyful mind, with a perfectly excellent superior intention to one who has a perfectly excellent superior intention, and to a bodhisattva who has generated the awakening mind with an excellent superior intention; and who, even in brief, applies themselves with tireless effort to the writing and reading of it; and who causes others to take it up, who teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, extols it correctly, and occasions correct delight in it; who illustrates it with words, makes it clear, and clarifies it further, and teaches correctly the meaning of this text — saying to someone: 'Come here, son of good family, train on this path of the bodhisattva! If you train on this path, practice it, and apply yourself to it, you will swiftly achieve manifest and perfect awakening of unsurpassed, complete enlightenment. And having become fully and perfectly awakened, you will then tame immeasurable numbers of sentient beings by perfectly exhausting the aggregates — the uttermost perfection, the utter laying bare of the true limit' — and thus purifies that person's mind and frees them of doubt.

Kauśika, furthermore, whatever son or daughter of good family writes out this Perfection of Wisdom and both reads it themselves and gives it to others as written, just as before —

Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the one before. Kauśika, furthermore, whatever son or daughter of good family, with skill in meaning, reads this Perfection of Wisdom, and just as before writes it out and gives it to others, and then teaches it with full clarity — explaining both the meaning and the words — Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates far more merit still.

Then Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods), the ruler of the gods, addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, shall this Perfection of Wisdom also be taught nearby and made accessible?"

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One replied to Śakra, ruler of the gods, with these words: "Kauśika, this Perfection of Wisdom should indeed be taught nearby and made accessible to those who have not yet understood it. And why? Because in the time to come, a counterfeit image of the Perfection of Wisdom will appear. If those sons or daughters of good family who have not yet understood it, who wish to achieve manifest and perfect awakening of unsurpassed, complete enlightenment, were to hear a counterfeit image of the Perfection of Wisdom and be misled by it, that would be most improper."

Then Śakra, ruler of the gods, addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, how shall one recognize in the time to come what counts as the teaching of a counterfeit image of the Perfection of Wisdom? How is one to know that it is a counterfeit image of the Perfection of Wisdom?"

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One replied to Śakra, ruler of the gods, with these words: "Kauśika, in the time to come, certain monks will appear who have not cultivated the body, have not cultivated moral discipline, have not cultivated mind, have not cultivated wisdom — monks whose wisdom is corrupt, dull and sheeplike by nature, their wisdom utterly deteriorated. These monks, claiming to teach the Perfection of Wisdom, will instead present a counterfeit image of it.

Kauśika, how will they present a counterfeit image of the Perfection of Wisdom? They will teach: 'The disintegration of form is the very impermanence of form,' and likewise: 'The disintegration of feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness is the very impermanence of consciousness.' They will further teach: 'Whoever seeks in that way is engaging in the Perfection of Wisdom.' Kauśika, this is to be recognized as a counterfeit image of the Perfection of Wisdom.

Kauśika, in contrast, the disintegration of form is not to be viewed as the impermanence of form. Likewise, the disintegration of feeling, perception, and formations — and Kauśika, the disintegration of consciousness is not to be viewed as the impermanence of consciousness. If one views things in that latter way, that is engaging in a counterfeit image of the Perfection of Wisdom.

Therefore, Kauśika, a son or daughter of good family should teach the meaning of this Perfection of Wisdom accessibly and clearly. And when they do teach the meaning of the Perfection of Wisdom, that son or daughter of good family generates extremely abundant merit.

Kauśika, furthermore, consider a son or daughter of good family who establishes all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa, however many there may be, in the fruit of stream-entry. Kauśika, what do you think? Does that son or daughter of good family generate much merit on that account?"

Śakra (Indra, king of the gods) replied: "Blessed One, that is a great deal of merit. Sugata, that is a great deal of merit."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, even more merit than that is generated by a son or daughter of good family who, even in a small way, puts this Perfection of Wisdom into a volume of scripture, and then, with faith, gives it to one with faith; with thorough discernment, gives it to one with thorough discernment; with keen aspiration, gives it to one with keen aspiration; with a clear and open mind, gives it to one with a clear and open mind; with excellent and elevated intention, gives it to one with excellent and elevated intention; and with elevated intention gives it to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind in order to attain awakening. And even in a small way, that son or daughter of good family applies themselves without rest to writing it and reading it, and with diligence leads others to take it up, teaches it correctly, presents it correctly, praises it correctly, causes others to rejoice in it correctly, illustrates it with words, explains it fully, and illuminates it again and again. They teach the meaning of it correctly, and they speak these very words: 'Come here, son of good family. Train in this path of the bodhisattva. If you train in this path and practice it and strive, you will swiftly attain full and perfect awakening, unsurpassed and completely perfect.' And once you are fully and perfectly awakened, you will bring the immeasurable realm of sentient beings to complete mastery in the unsurpassed thorough exhaustion of the aggregates — that is, to the clear discernment of the true limit.' Speaking in this way, they purify that person's mind, free them from doubt, and say these words: 'Son of good family, you — just so — should attain those very qualities endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom.' Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the former one.

And why is that? Kauśika, because from this the fruit of stream-entry is clearly distinguished.

Kauśika, never mind the merit of establishing all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa in the fruit of stream-entry. Kauśika, never mind the merit of establishing all the sentient beings in the world-system of the four great continents in the fruit of stream-entry — let that much merit be set aside. Kauśika, never mind the merit of establishing all the sentient beings in the world-system of a thousand worlds headed by their single supreme in the fruit of stream-entry — let that much merit be set aside. Kauśika, never mind the merit of establishing all the sentient beings in the world-system of the two-thousand middling worlds in the fruit of stream-entry — let that much merit be set aside. Kauśika, as for all the sentient beings there may be in the world-system of the great trichiliocosm of three thousand worlds — however many there are — and a son or daughter of good family who establishes every one of them in the fruit of stream-entry —

Kauśika, let the merit of establishing all the sentient beings in the world-system of the great trichiliocosm of three thousand worlds in the fruit of stream-entry be set aside. Kauśika, suppose that in a series of world-systems of the great trichiliocosm of three thousand worlds as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River there are sentient beings — however many there may be — and some son or daughter of good family establishes every single one of them in the fruit of stream-entry. Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family generate much merit on that basis?" Śakra replied: "Much, O Blessed One; much, O Sugata." The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, even more merit than that does this son or daughter of good family generate — namely, whoever has committed this Perfection of Wisdom, even in brief, to a book, and then with faith gives to one who has faith, with earnest reflection gives to one who reflects earnestly, with keen aspiration gives to one who has keen aspiration, with a serene mind gives to one whose mind is serene, with an excellent and exalted intention gives to one who has an excellent and exalted intention, and with the awakening mind thus aroused gives with an exalted intention to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind toward awakening; or who, even in brief, strives without laziness and with diligence in the writing and reading of it, causes another to take it up, teaches it correctly, arranges it correctly, extols it correctly, causes delight in it correctly, indicates it through words, clarifies its meaning through exposition, and traces it further; and who teaches the correct meaning of this to another; and who says these words: 'Come here, son of good family — train yourself on this path of the bodhisattva. Having trained and practised on this path and made the effort, you will swiftly come to fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, complete, perfect awakening. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will bring the immeasurable multitude of sentient beings to perfect exhaustion of the aggregates — that is to say, you will lead them to the full discernment of the true ultimate end'; and who thus purifies that person's mind and dispels all doubt; and who says these words: 'Son of good family, attain for yourself these very qualities that belong to the Perfection of Wisdom' — Kauśika, this son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the former. And why is that? Kauśika, because from this the fruit of stream-entry is fully discerned.

Kauśika, furthermore, suppose some son or daughter of good family establishes all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa — however many there are — in the fruit of once-returning at a single stroke. Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family generate much merit on that basis?" Śakra replied: "Much, O Blessed One; much, O Sugata." The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, even more merit than that does this son or daughter of good family generate — namely, whoever has committed this Perfection of Wisdom, even in brief, to a book, and then with faith gives to one who has faith, with earnest reflection gives to one who reflects earnestly, with keen aspiration gives to one who has keen aspiration, with a serene mind gives to one whose mind is serene, with an excellent and exalted intention gives to one who has an excellent and exalted intention, and with the awakening mind thus aroused gives with an exalted intention to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind toward awakening; or who, even in brief, strives without laziness

to engage another in holding it, to teach it correctly, to expound it correctly, to extol it correctly, to cause another to rejoice in it correctly, to illuminate it with words, to illustrate it with examples, to point it out in accordance with the text, and to teach another its meaning correctly; and who speaks thus: "Come here, son of good family! Train yourself in this path of the bodhisattva. If you train in this path, practice it, and strive at it, you will swiftly attain the manifest, complete, and perfect awakening of unsurpassed, truly perfected buddhahood.
