And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will bring to complete mastery the immeasurable realm of sentient beings in the utterly supreme cessation of the aggregates — that is to say, the unsurpassed, which is the perfect disclosure of the ultimate limit of reality" — speaking thus, purifying that person's mind, and making them free of doubt; and who further says these words: "Son of good family, obtain for yourself these very qualities endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom" — Kauśika, such a son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the former. And why is that? Kauśika, this disclosure leads to the fruit of once-returning.

Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by establishing all sentient beings of Jambudvīpa in the fruit of once-returning. Kauśika, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings there are in the world-system of the four great continents in the fruit of once-returning — Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of the world-system of the four great continents in the fruit of once-returning. Kauśika, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings there are in the world-system of one thousand worlds in the fruit of once-returning — Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of the world-system of one thousand worlds in the fruit of once-returning. Kauśika, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings there are in the world-system of two thousand middling worlds in the fruit of once-returning — Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of the world-system of two thousand middling worlds in the fruit of once-returning. Kauśika, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings there are in the great trichiliocosm, the world-system of three thousand great thousands, in the fruit of once-returning — Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of the great trichiliocosm in the fruit of once-returning. Kauśika, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings there are in as many great trichiliocosms as there are grains of sand in the river Gaṅgā in the fruit of once-returning — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family generate great merit on that basis?

Śakra replied: Greatly so, Blessed One! Greatly so, Sugata!

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, far more than even that does this son or daughter of good family generate merit.

Whoever, having even so much as committed this Perfection of Wisdom to a volume in abbreviated form, then with faith directs that faith, with thorough examination directs that thorough examination, with keen devotion directs that keen devotion, with a clear mind directs that clear mind, with perfect superior intention directs that perfect superior intention, and with the awakening mind aroused toward awakening gives with superior intention to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind toward awakening — and who, even in abbreviated form, diligently applies themselves without laziness to writing and reading it, and with earnestness causes others to take it up, teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, praises it correctly, causes delight in it correctly, elucidates it with words, explains it thoroughly, demonstrates it in full, and teaches its meaning properly — and says to that person: "Come here, son of good family! Train on this path of the bodhisattva! If you train and practice on this path and apply yourself, you will swiftly come to fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will lead the immeasurable realm of sentient beings — in this supreme manner, through the complete exhaustion of their aggregates — to taming them in the thorough discernment of the true limit." Speaking thus, and in that way purifying that person's mind and freeing it from doubt, and saying these words: "Son of good family, may you attain precisely those qualities of the Perfection of Wisdom" — Kauśika, this son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the former. And why is that? Kauśika, it is because this very text discerns the fruit of once-returning.

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines, the sixth fascicle.

Kauśika, furthermore, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa in the fruit of non-returning — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family generate great merit on that basis?

Śakra replied: Greatly so, Blessed One! Greatly so, Sugata!

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, far more than even that does this son or daughter of good family generate merit — whoever, having even so much as committed this Perfection of Wisdom to a volume in abbreviated form, then with faith directs that faith, with thorough examination directs that thorough examination, with keen devotion directs that keen devotion, with a clear mind directs that clear mind, with perfect superior intention directs that perfect superior intention, and with the awakening mind aroused toward awakening gives with superior intention to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind toward awakening — and who, even in abbreviated form, diligently applies themselves without laziness to writing and reading it, and with earnestness causes others to take it up, teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, praises it correctly, causes delight in it correctly, elucidates it with words, explains it thoroughly, demonstrates it in full, and teaches its meaning properly — and says to that person: "Come here, son of good family! Train on this path of the bodhisattva! If you train and practice on this path and apply yourself, you will swiftly come to fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening."

"And having fully and perfectly awakened, one will guide the immeasurable realm of sentient beings to thoroughly realize this unsurpassed complete exhaustion of the aggregates — that is, the perfect opening of the true and real limit." Speaking thus, one who in that way purifies another's mind, frees them from doubt, and says the words: "Son of good family, you should attain those very teachings endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom" — Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the first. And why is that? Kauśika, because this opens up the fruit of non-return.

Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa in the fruit of non-return. Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by any son or daughter of good family who establishes in the fruit of non-return all the sentient beings that exist throughout the world-system of the four great continents. Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of the world-system of the four great continents in the fruit of non-return.

Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by any son or daughter of good family who establishes in the fruit of non-return all the sentient beings that exist throughout the world-system of one thousand worlds. Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of that world-system of one thousand worlds in the fruit of non-return.

Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by any son or daughter of good family who establishes in the fruit of non-return all the sentient beings that exist throughout the intermediate world-system of two thousand worlds. Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of that intermediate world-system of two thousand worlds in the fruit of non-return.

Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by any son or daughter of good family who establishes in the fruit of non-return all the sentient beings that exist throughout the great thousandfold world-system of three thousand worlds. Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of that great thousandfold world-system of three thousand worlds in the fruit of non-return.

Kauśika, if any son or daughter of good family were to establish in the fruit of non-return all the sentient beings that exist in as many great thousandfold world-systems as there are grains of sand in the river Ganges — Kauśika, what do you think: would that son or daughter of good family generate great merit from that basis?

Śakra replied: "Greatly, Blessed One! Greatly, O Sugata!"

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, yet more merit than even that is generated by the son or daughter of good family who, even in a small way, has this Perfection of Wisdom put into a book, and who then, with fervent faith, gives to one of fervent faith; with discerning reflection, gives to one of discerning reflection; with keen aspiration, gives to one of keen aspiration; with a joyful mind, gives to one of joyful mind; with excellent and exalted intention, gives to one of excellent and exalted intention; with an awakening mind aroused toward awakening, gives with exalted intention to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind — even in a small way diligently applies themselves without laziness to writing and reading it, and with earnestness causes others to take it up, teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, praises it correctly, and causes delight in it correctly.

illustrates it with words, illustrates it in detail, and illustrates it repeatedly — and correctly teaches the meaning of this to another person — and furthermore says to that person these very words: "Come here, son of good family! Train in this path of the bodhisattva. If you train, practice, and apply yourself to this path, you will quickly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will tame the immeasurable realm of sentient beings — those whose aggregates are utterly exhausted in the unsurpassed way — leading them to the complete discernment of the true end." And speaking thus, one purifies that person's mind and frees them from doubt. And if one says to them these words: "Son of good family, obtain for yourself precisely those qualities endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom" — Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the former. Why is that? Because, Kauśika, from this very teaching the fruit of non-return is fully discerned.

Furthermore, Kauśika, if some son or daughter of good family were to establish all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa — however many there may be — in the state of arhatship, Kauśika, what do you think: would that son or daughter of good family thereby generate much merit? Śakra replied: "A great deal, Blessed One. A great deal, Sugata." The Blessed One declared: "Yet more merit than that, Kauśika, does that son or daughter of good family generate — the one who has committed this Perfection of Wisdom, even in brief, to a book; who, with keen aspiration, gives to one of keen aspiration; with discerning reflection, to one of discerning reflection; with keen aspiration, to one of keen aspiration; with a joyful mind, to one of joyful mind; with excellent and exalted intention, to one of excellent and exalted intention; with an awakening mind aroused toward awakening, gives with exalted intention to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind — and who diligently applies themselves without laziness even in a small way to writing and reading it, with earnestness causes others to take it up, teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, praises it correctly, causes delight in it correctly, illustrates it with words, illustrates it in detail, and illustrates it repeatedly, and correctly teaches the meaning of this to another — and furthermore says these very words: 'Come here, son of good family! Train in this path of the bodhisattva. If you train, practice, and apply yourself to this path, you will quickly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will tame the immeasurable realm of sentient beings — those whose aggregates are utterly exhausted in the unsurpassed way — leading them to the complete discernment of the true end.' And speaking thus, one purifies that person's mind and frees them from doubt, and says to them these words: 'Son of good family, obtain for yourself precisely those qualities endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom.'"

Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the former. Why is that? Because, Kauśika, from this very teaching even arhatship itself is fully discerned. And in this regard it is as follows: son of good family, however much and in whatever way you train in the Perfection of Wisdom, in exactly that measure and in that very way you will ultimately come to attain the qualities of a buddha and draw ever closer to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

If you train in this training, practice it, and apply yourself to it, you will fully discern the fruit of stream-entry. You will fully discern the fruit of once-returning. You will fully discern the fruit of non-returning. You will fully discern arhatship itself. You will fully discern the state of a pratyekabuddha. And you will fully discern perfect, complete buddhahood — thus one should stir up and increase your enthusiasm.

Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by establishing all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa in arhatship. Kauśika, set aside even the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all sentient beings — however many there are — in the world-system of the four great continents in arhatship, and the merit accumulated by establishing all sentient beings of that world-system of the four great continents in arhatship. Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all sentient beings — however many there are — in the world-system of a thousand worlds in arhatship, and the merit accumulated by establishing all sentient beings of that thousand-world world-system in arhatship. Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all sentient beings — however many there are — in the world-system of the two-thousand middle worlds in arhatship, and the merit accumulated by establishing all sentient beings of that two-thousand middle world-system in arhatship. Kauśika, set aside the merit accumulated by some son or daughter of good family who establishes all sentient beings — however many there are — in the world-system of the great three-thousand-fold world in arhatship, and the merit accumulated by establishing all sentient beings of that great three-thousand-fold world-system in arhatship. Kauśika, if a son or daughter of good family were to establish in arhatship all sentient beings — however many there are — in world-systems as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River multiplied by the number of great three-thousand-fold world-systems, Kauśika, what do you think — would that son or daughter of good family generate great merit from that basis?

Śakra replied: Blessed One, it would be great indeed. Sugata, it would be great indeed. Blessed One, it would not be easy to count that mass of merit. Blessed One, that mass of merit cannot be counted, cannot be illustrated by example, cannot be demonstrated by analogy, cannot serve as a basis or a proximate cause for comparison.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, even so, this son or daughter of good family generates far more merit — whoever, having committed this Perfection of Wisdom, even in condensed form, to a written volume, then gives it to a bodhisattva who has aroused the mind of awakening, giving it with faith to one of faith, with discernment to one of discernment, with keen aspiration to one of keen aspiration, with a clear mind to one of clear mind, with excellent superior intention to one of excellent superior intention; or whoever diligently pursues its copying and reading without intermission, even in condensed form; or whoever applies diligence to retaining it; or whoever teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, extols it correctly, causes others to rejoice in it correctly; or whoever expounds it in words, elucidates it fully, explains it by way of its meaning, and correctly teaches its meaning —

And to that person one says the following: "Come here, son of good family. Train on this path of the bodhisattva. If you train on this path, practise it, and apply yourself with diligence, you will swiftly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will then guide the immeasurable realm of sentient beings — through the supreme exhaustion of the aggregates, namely the thorough discernment of the true limit — to complete and perfect taming." Speaking thus, and thereby purifying that person's mind and freeing it from doubt, and saying these words: "Son of good family, you yourself — obtain these very qualities endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom" — this generates far more merit than all the foregoing. And why is that? Kauśika, from this very teaching the state of arhatship is thoroughly discerned.

In this regard, son of good family, however and in whatever manner you train in the Perfection of Wisdom, in just that manner and to just that extent you will come to obtain the qualities of a buddha, and you will draw near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. If you train on this path, practise it, and apply yourself with diligence, you will thoroughly discern the fruit of stream-entry. You will thoroughly discern the fruit of once-returning. You will thoroughly discern the fruit of non-returning. You will thoroughly discern the state of arhatship. You will thoroughly discern the state of pratyekabuddhahood. You will thoroughly discern the state of perfect, complete buddhahood. Saying this, one will increase the other's enthusiasm.

Moreover, Kauśika, consider all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa — every single one of them. If some son of good family or daughter of good family were to establish them all in the state of pratyekabuddhahood — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son of good family or daughter of good family generate much merit on account of that basis?" Śakra (Indra, ruler of the gods) replied: "Much, Blessed One. Much, O Sugata." The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, more than that does a son of good family or daughter of good family generate merit — one who, even in condensed form, once the Perfection of Wisdom has been committed to a volume, gives it with faith to one of faith, with discernment to one of discernment, with keen aspiration to one of keen aspiration, with a clear mind to one of clear mind, with excellent superior intention to one of excellent superior intention, or to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind with superior intention; or whoever diligently pursues its copying and reading without intermission, even in condensed form; or whoever applies diligence to retaining it; or whoever teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, extols it correctly, causes others to rejoice in it correctly; or whoever expounds it in words, elucidates it fully, explains it by way of its meaning, and correctly teaches its meaning to it — and to that person says the following: 'Come here, son of good family. Train on this path of the bodhisattva. If you train on this path, practise it, and apply yourself with diligence, you will swiftly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will then guide the immeasurable realm of sentient beings — through the supreme exhaustion of the aggregates, namely the thorough discernment of the true limit — to complete and perfect taming.' Speaking thus, and thereby engaging that person's mind and freeing it from doubt, and saying these words: 'Son of good family, you yourself — obtain these very qualities endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom.' This generates far more merit than all the foregoing. And why is that? Kauśika, from this very teaching the state of pratyekabuddhahood is thoroughly discerned.

In this way, the instruction is given: "Son of good family, to whatever degree and in whatever manner you train in the Perfection of Wisdom, to that very degree and in that very manner you will gradually attain the qualities of a buddha. You will draw ever closer to unsurpassed, perfectly complete awakening. Training and practicing in this discipline, striving in it, you will thoroughly discern the fruit of stream-entry. You will thoroughly discern the fruit of once-returning. You will thoroughly discern the fruit of non-returning. You will thoroughly discern arhathood itself. You will thoroughly discern pratyekabuddhahood itself. You will thoroughly discern perfectly complete buddhahood itself." Saying thus, one generates and increases enthusiasm in that person.

Kauśika, set aside the merit of establishing all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa in pratyekabuddhahood. Kauśika, set aside even the merit of one son or daughter of good family establishing all the sentient beings of the world-system of four great continents in pratyekabuddhahood, and then accumulating the merit of establishing all the sentient beings of that very world-system of four great continents in pratyekabuddhahood. Kauśika, set aside the merit of one son or daughter of good family establishing all the sentient beings of a thousandfold world-system in pratyekabuddhahood, and then accumulating the merit of establishing all the sentient beings of that very thousandfold world-system in pratyekabuddhahood. Kauśika, set aside the merit of one son or daughter of good family establishing all the sentient beings of a two-thousandfold intermediate world-system in pratyekabuddhahood, and then accumulating the merit of establishing all the sentient beings of that very two-thousandfold intermediate world-system in pratyekabuddhahood. Kauśika, set aside the merit of one son or daughter of good family establishing all the sentient beings of a three-thousandfold great world-system in pratyekabuddhahood, and then accumulating the merit of establishing all the sentient beings of that very three-thousandfold great world-system in pratyekabuddhahood. Kauśika, suppose that some son or daughter of good family were to establish in pratyekabuddhahood all the sentient beings found in as many three-thousandfold great world-systems as there are grains of sand in the river Ganges — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family generate much merit on that basis?

Śakra — Indra, lord of the gods — replied: "Greatly, Blessed One. Greatly, Sugata."

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, far more merit than that is generated by this son or daughter of good family — whoever, with even so little as a single volume of this Perfection of Wisdom written down, gives it out of genuine faith to one of genuine faith, out of thorough discernment to one of thorough discernment, out of keen aspiration to one of keen aspiration, out of a clear and open mind to one of a clear and open mind, out of excellent elevated intention to one of excellent elevated intention, or gives it to a bodhisattva who has aroused the awakening mind toward awakening with elevated intention — and who, even in so small a matter as copying it out and reading it, strives at this without distraction, and with energy takes it up and holds it, teaches it correctly, sets it forth correctly, praises it correctly, causes delight in it correctly, points to it with words, demonstrates it through analysis, elucidates it in detail, and correctly teaches its meaning —

And to that person one would speak thus: "Come here, son of good family — train yourself on this path of the bodhisattva. If you train on this path, practice it, and strive at it, you will swiftly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And having fully and perfectly awakened, you will then lead the immeasurable realm of sentient beings to the thorough cutting through of this highest goal — the complete exhaustion of the aggregates — to perfect discipline in the utter end of reality." Speaking thus, one purifies that person's mind and frees them from doubt. And if one says the words: "Son of good family, obtain for yourself these very Dharmas of the Perfection of Wisdom" — this generates far more merit than what came before.

Why is that? Kauśika, from this very Perfection of Wisdom the state of a pratyekabuddha is thoroughly distinguished. And it is thus: son of good family, in whatever measure and manner you come to train in the Perfection of Wisdom, in that same measure and manner you will in due course attain the Dharmas of a buddha. You will draw near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. If you train in this training, practice it, and strive at it, you will thoroughly distinguish the fruit of stream-entry. You will thoroughly distinguish the fruit of once-returning. You will thoroughly distinguish the fruit of non-returning. You will thoroughly distinguish the state of arhat. You will thoroughly distinguish the state of pratyekabuddha. You will thoroughly distinguish the state of a perfectly complete buddha — thus one increases enthusiasm and joy.

Kauśika, consider further: however many sentient beings there are in Jambudvīpa, and some son or daughter of good family causes them all to arouse the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — and Kauśika, another son or daughter of good family causes all of those same beings to arouse the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening and then gives them this Perfection of Wisdom written out — and Kauśika, some son or daughter of good family, thinking "I will train in this Perfection of Wisdom and practice it as yoga, and through this very Perfection of Wisdom I will meditate upon, increase, extend, and expand the Perfection of Wisdom, and thereby perfectly complete all the Dharmas of a buddha" — if they then entrust this Perfection of Wisdom to a non-retreating bodhisattva, a great being, this son or daughter of good family generates far more merit than the former.

Why is that? Because this one will certainly, having fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, bring the suffering of sentient beings to its final end.

Kauśika, let alone the merit that is accumulated by all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — Kauśika, let alone the merit accumulated by all the sentient beings of a world-system of four great continents arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening through the efforts of some son or daughter of good family — and Kauśika, let alone further the merit accumulated by all sentient beings of that same world-system of four great continents arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening —

Kauśika, let alone the merit accumulated by all the sentient beings — however many there may be — in a world-system of one thousand worlds (a minor chiliocosm) arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening through the efforts of some son or daughter of good family — and Kauśika, let alone further the merit accumulated by all the sentient beings of that same world-system of one thousand worlds arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — Kauśika, let alone the merit accumulated by all the sentient beings — however many there may be — in a world-system of two thousand worlds (a middling chiliocosm) arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening through the efforts of some son or daughter of good family — and Kauśika, let alone further the merit accumulated by all the sentient beings of that same world-system of two thousand worlds arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — Kauśika, let alone the merit accumulated by all the sentient beings — however many there may be — in a great trichiliocosm world-system arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening through the efforts of some son or daughter of good family — and Kauśika, let alone further the merit accumulated by all the sentient beings of that same great trichiliocosm world-system arousing the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Kauśika, suppose that in world-systems as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River, however many sentient beings there may be, some son or daughter of good family were to cause all of them to arouse the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And then another son or daughter of good family, having caused all of those same beings to arouse the awakening mind toward unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, were to write out this Perfection of Wisdom and give it to them as a gift — and thinking, "By training and practicing in this Perfection of Wisdom in this way, this Perfection of Wisdom will be greatly cultivated, will grow, will expand, and will become vast, and will thereby be brought to complete fulfillment," were to entrust this Perfection of Wisdom to an irreversible great bodhisattva — Kauśika, that son or daughter of good family would generate far more merit than the former. Why is that? Because it is through this that one will assuredly attain unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and having done so will bring the suffering of sentient beings to its final end.

Kauśika, furthermore, suppose all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa — however many there may be — were to become irreversible from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and then some son or daughter of good family were to write out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume and give it as a gift and entrust it to those very beings, teaching them both its meaning and its letters. Kauśika, what do you think — would that son or daughter of good family generate abundant merit from that basis?

Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods) replied: Blessed One, it would be vast. Sugata, it would be vast. Blessed One, that mass of merit is not easy to count. Blessed One, that mass of merit is not easy to enumerate, not easy to illustrate with analogies, not easy to exemplify, not easy to use as a basis for comparison, not easy to trace to its causes.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, all the more so — that son or daughter of good family would generate far greater merit still.

For those non-retrogressing bodhisattvas, great beings, who wish to swiftly manifest perfect, unsurpassed, complete awakening, one who writes out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume, presents it, entrusts it, and teaches it nearby with its meaning and its letters — and who recites and expounds this Perfection of Wisdom — that person generates merit beyond compare. Set aside, Kauśika, the merit of entrusting this Perfection of Wisdom to all the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa who have become non-retrogressing. Kauśika, suppose all the sentient beings in the world system of the four great continents were likewise to become non-retrogressing from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and then some son or daughter of good family were to write out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume, present it, entrust it, and teach it nearby with its meaning and its letters — set aside, Kauśika, even the merit of entrusting this Perfection of Wisdom to all the sentient beings of that world system of the four great continents who have become non-retrogressing.

Kauśika, suppose all the sentient beings in a world system of one thousand worlds were likewise to become non-retrogressing from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and then some son or daughter of good family were to write out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume, present it, entrust it, and teach it nearby with its meaning and its letters — set aside, Kauśika, even the merit of entrusting this Perfection of Wisdom to all the sentient beings of that world system of one thousand worlds who have become non-retrogressing.

Kauśika, suppose all the sentient beings in a world system of two thousand middle worlds were likewise to become non-retrogressing from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and then some son or daughter of good family were to write out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume, present it, entrust it, and teach it nearby with its meaning and its letters — set aside, Kauśika, even the merit of entrusting this Perfection of Wisdom to all the sentient beings of that world system of two thousand middle worlds who have become non-retrogressing.

Kauśika, suppose all the sentient beings in a great trichiliocosm were likewise to become non-retrogressing from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and then some son or daughter of good family were to write out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume, present it, entrust it, and teach it nearby with its meaning and its letters — set aside, Kauśika, even the merit of entrusting this Perfection of Wisdom to all the sentient beings of that great trichiliocosm who have become non-retrogressing.

Kauśika, suppose there were as many sentient beings in a great trichiliocosm as there are grains of sand in the river Ganges, and all of them were to become non-retrogressing from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and then some son or daughter of good family were to write out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume, present it, entrust it, and teach it nearby with its meaning and its letters — Kauśika, what do you think? Would that son or daughter of good family generate much merit from that basis? Śakra (Indra, lord of the gods) replied: Blessed One, very much indeed. Sugata, very much indeed. Blessed One, it would not be easy to count that mass of merit. Blessed One, that mass of merit could not easily be counted, illustrated by example, shown by analogy, or made the basis of a calculation.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, a son or daughter of good family generates even more merit than that — one who writes out this Perfection of Wisdom in a volume, presents it, entrusts it, and teaches it nearby with its meaning and its letters to those non-retrogressing bodhisattvas, great beings, who wish to swiftly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and who recites and expounds this Perfection of Wisdom. And then, Kauśika, suppose another bodhisattva, a great being, should arise and say: "I will fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening even more swiftly than these." Kauśika, whatever son or daughter of good family recites and expounds this Perfection of Wisdom to that bodhisattva, that great being of very swift higher knowledge — that person generates even more merit than the former son or daughter of good family.

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One with these words: Blessed One, however closely a bodhisattva, a great being, has drawn near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, to just that degree one should instruct and teach them in the Perfection of Wisdom. For by instructing and teaching in the Perfection of Wisdom in just that way, they will draw close to suchness. And when they have drawn close to suchness, those who have fully made use of their robes, alms food, bedding, seats, medicines for curing illness, and requisites — the acts performed for them will yield great fruit and great reward. That is why one generates exceedingly much merit. And why is that? Blessed One, it is because this is just what it means for a bodhisattva, a great being, to draw near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Then the venerable Subhūti said to Śakra, lord of the gods: Kauśika, it is excellent, truly excellent, that you inspire, fully embrace, and follow close upon those persons who belong to the bodhisattva vehicle. Kauśika, this is indeed your task: that any noble śrāvaka who wishes to fully embrace all sentient beings should inspire, fully embrace, and follow close upon the bodhisattvas, those great beings, in their enthusiasm for unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — that is what such a one should do. And why is that?

It is from this very source that unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening arises for the bodhisattvas, those great beings. For if those bodhisattvas, those great beings, were to leave this awakening mind unraised, they would not train in unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, nor would they train in the six perfections; and without training, they would not fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. It is because bodhisattvas, those great beings, train in these very trainings of a bodhisattva — the six perfections — that they raise the mind set on awakening, and it is for this reason that they fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

This concludes the fifth chapter, entitled "The Enumeration of Merit," from the noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines.

Then the bodhisattva, that great being, Maitreya spoke thus to the elder, the venerable Subhūti: Noble Subhūti, furthermore, among all the bases of merit — those accompanied by rejoicing in the bodhisattvas, those great beings, together with the dedication of that rejoicing — and among all the bases of merit arising from the generosity of all sentient beings, and the bases of merit arising from moral discipline, and the bases of merit arising from meditation — of all these, this very basis of merit accompanied by rejoicing in the bodhisattvas, those great beings, together with its dedication, is to be called supreme, excellent, foremost, finest, best of all, distinguished, highest, unsurpassed, unexcelled, incomparable, and equal to the incomparable.

When he had spoken these words, the elder, the venerable Subhūti, said this to the bodhisattva, that great being, Maitreya: Maitreya, consider the bodhisattva, that great being, who rejoices and dedicates in this way. In the world-systems of the great trichiliocosm pervading all ten directions — those that are immeasurably immeasurable, incalculably incalculable, immeasurably vast, inconceivably inconceivable, boundlessly infinite — in each and every one of those great trichiliocosmic world-systems, in the time that has passed, there existed Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas who were immeasurably immeasurable, incalculably incalculable, immeasurably vast, inconceivably inconceivable, and boundlessly infinite in number: those whose path was cut off and whose entering upon it was cut off; whose conceptual elaboration and their drawing onward into existence were cut off; whose tears were dried and whose thorns were broken; who had utterly cast down the heavy burden; who had achieved their own goal; whose binding to existence was wholly exhausted; who, through perfect and complete knowledge, were utterly liberated in mind; who had gone to the far shore of the supreme mastery over all minds; who had passed into final nirvāṇa in the realm of nirvāṇa without remainder of aggregates. From the very first moment those Tathāgatas raised the awakening mind, all the way up through their fully and perfectly awakening to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, through their passing into final nirvāṇa in the realm of nirvāṇa without remainder of aggregates, and up to the point where the sacred Dharma had not yet declined —

during that entire interval — the aggregate of moral discipline, the aggregate of meditative absorption, the aggregate of wisdom, the aggregate of liberation, and the aggregate of the wisdom that sees liberation belonging to those blessed buddhas, the Tathāgatas; the roots of virtue endowed with the six perfections; the roots of virtue endowed with the magnificent qualities of buddhahood; the roots of virtue endowed with the perfection of the powers and fearlessnesses; likewise the roots of virtue endowed with the perfection of direct knowledge and the perfection of thorough knowledge; the roots of virtue endowed with the perfection of aspiration; the roots of virtue endowed with the magnificent gnosis of omniscience; whatever was their intention to benefit beings; whatever was their great loving-kindness; whatever was their great compassion; whatever were the immeasurable, countless, magnificent qualities of buddhahood; whatever was unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening; whatever was the bliss of unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening; whatever was the perfection of sovereignty over all phenomena; whatever was the supreme magical display of inexhaustibly subjugating what is immeasurable and subduing all things; whatever was the power of the Tathāgata's genuine, exact gnosis — unobscured, unattached, unobstructed, without equal, equal to the unequal, incomparable, immeasurable; whatever was the power of the buddha's gnosis among all powers; whatever was the buddha's gnosis and vision; whatever was the perfection of the ten powers; whatever was the realization of the supreme and perfect fulfillment of the four fearlessnesses; whatever was the knowledge of Dharma arising from the ultimate establishment of all phenomena; whatever was the supreme turning of the wheel of Dharma; the holding aloft of the lamp of Dharma, the beating of the drum of Dharma, the blowing of the conch of Dharma, the sounding of the conch of Dharma, the wielding of the sword of Dharma, the raining down of the rain of Dharma, the offering of the burnt offering of Dharma, and the thoroughly satisfying and nourishing of all sentient beings through the gift of Dharma —

and whatever roots of virtue belong to all those who, through such teaching of Dharma, were tamed, trained, inspired, made certain, and turned toward perfect awakening, whether through the Dharma of buddhas, or the Dharma of solitary buddhas, or the Dharma of hearers; and whatever roots of virtue belong to those bodhisattvas, great bodhisattvas, whom those blessed buddhas, the Tathāgatas, prophesied to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, endowed as they were with the six perfections; and whatever roots of virtue belong to all those persons of the pratyekabuddha vehicle whom they prophesied to the awakening of a solitary buddha; and whatever is the merit-producing basis arising from generosity, the merit-producing basis arising from moral discipline, and the merit-producing basis arising from meditation, belonging to persons of the śrāvaka vehicle; and whatever are the roots of virtue that are undefiled and belong to those in training; and whatever are the roots of virtue that are undefiled and belong to those beyond training; and whatever roots of virtue were generated in relation to those teachings by ordinary persons —

the four-fold retinue of those Blessed One buddhas — the monks, the nuns, the laymen, and the laywomen — and whatever are the merit-producing bases arising from their generosity, the merit-producing bases arising from their moral discipline, and the merit-producing bases arising from meditation; and whatever roots of virtue were generated in relation to those Blessed One buddhas teaching the Dharma by gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, great serpents, humans, non-humans, and whatever sentient beings have gone to birth in the animal realm; and whatever roots of virtue were generated by those who generated virtue in relation to those Blessed One buddhas as they passed into final nirvāṇa; and whatever roots of virtue were generated in relation to those Blessed One buddhas after they had passed into final nirvāṇa; and whatever roots of virtue were generated in dependence upon the buddhas, in dependence upon the Dharma, in dependence upon the Saṅgha, and in dependence upon persons who cultivated these mentally —

all of those roots of virtue, without exception and without remainder, are gathered together into one, summed up, and measured, and then one rejoices in them with the supreme rejoicing, rejoicing in them as excellent, foremost, superior, supremely superior, distinguished, highest, without superior, without anything above, without equal, and incomparable — and having so rejoiced, having come to possess that rejoicing, one then declares aloud, "I dedicate all of these merit-producing bases to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening," with the thought, "May I accomplish unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening."

Now in that regard: when persons of the bodhisattva vehicle dedicate and generate the awakening mind with certain things, certain referential objects, and certain aspects, do they apprehend those very things, those referential objects, and those aspects just as they have been taken as signs?

When this was said, the bodhisattva, the great being Maitreya spoke to the elder, the venerable Subhūti, as follows: "Venerable Subhūti, they do not apprehend those things, those referential objects, and those aspects just as they have been taken as signs."

When this was said, the elder, the venerable Subhūti spoke to the bodhisattva, the great being Maitreya as follows: "If one apprehends and takes as a sign what has no thing and no referential object, how would this not become a distorted perception, a distorted mind, and a distorted view? And why? It is because attachment, though without a thing, thinks of the impermanent as permanent, of what is suffering as bliss, of the selfless as self, and of the unattractive as attractive — constructing and elaborating in that way — and from this arise distorted perception, distorted mind, and distorted view. Moreover, just as the things are, just as the referential objects are, just as the aspects are, so too is awakening, and so too is the mind — and if that is so, then all phenomena and all elements are likewise so. But if just as the things are, just as the referential objects are, just as the aspects are, awakening is likewise so, and the mind is likewise so — then with what things, what referential objects, and what aspects does a mind dedicate to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening?"

— what meritorious deed accompanied by rejoicing does one dedicate to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening?

Then the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya said this to the Elder, the Venerable Subhūti: "Noble Subhūti, this teaching should not be expounded or demonstrated before a bodhisattva, a great being, who has newly entered the vehicle. Why is that? All those in whom there is only faith, only joy, only devotion, only reverence toward it will be ruined. Noble Subhūti, this should be expounded and closely taught before a non-regressing bodhisattva, a great being. A bodhisattva, a great being, who has been made firm by a virtuous spiritual friend — such a one will not retreat before this, will not be discouraged in any way, will not be embarrassed, will not be ashamed, will not turn their mind away from it, will not be broken and turn away, will not be terrified, will not be frightened in any way, will not come to be filled with any kind of fear. In this way, having rejoiced, the bodhisattva, the great being, should dedicate all meritorious deeds accompanied by rejoicing to omniscience itself."

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines. The seventh fascicle.

Then the Elder, the Venerable Subhūti, said this to the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya: "Maitreya, the very mind with which one rejoices and with which one dedicates — that mind has become exhausted and ceased, separated and transformed. Therefore, what is the mind with which one dedicates to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening? And what is the mind that is the meritorious deed accompanied by rejoicing that one dedicates to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening? And when the two minds do not meet, and that mind is by its very nature incapable of dedicating — how then could mind be capable of dedicating mind?"

Then Śakra, lord of the gods (Indra), said this to the Elder, the Venerable Subhūti: "Noble Subhūti, if those bodhisattvas, those great beings, who have newly entered the vehicle were to hear this teaching and become terrified, frightened in every way, and filled with fear — that would not be right. Noble Subhūti, how then should a bodhisattva, a great being, dedicate that meritorious deed accompanied by rejoicing to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening? And how, through holding fast to the meritorious deed accompanied by rejoicing and through dedicating the mind accompanied by rejoicing, does the mind accompanied by rejoicing come to be well held and well dedicated?"

Then the Elder, the Venerable Subhūti, addressed the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya, taking the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya as his basis, and spoke to the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya:

"Maitreya, in this regard a bodhisattva, a great being, should consider the following. In the ten directions of the world there are immeasurable and innumerable great trichiliocosms — world-systems of a thousand times a thousand times a thousand worlds. In each and every one of those directions, in each and every one of those great trichiliocosmic world-systems, there have been past buddhas, Blessed Ones — whose path is cut off, who have entered in, whose arising has ceased, who have gone beyond proliferation, who no longer lead beings into existence, whose tears are exhausted, whose thorns are broken, whose burden is thoroughly cast down, who have attained their own goal, whose binding to existence is entirely exhausted, who know all things rightly, whose minds are utterly and completely liberated, who have gone entirely beyond the dominion of all minds — immeasurable and innumerable buddhas, Blessed Ones, who have completely passed into nirvāṇa.

From the moment those buddhas, Blessed Ones, first aroused the mind of awakening, up until they manifestly and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and up until they completely passed into nirvāṇa in the realm of nirvāṇa without remainder of the aggregates, and up until the holy Dharma had not yet declined — throughout all of that span:

whatever wholesome roots accompanied by the perfections belong to those buddhas, Blessed Ones; whatever they accumulated of merit and whatever they accumulated of wholesome roots; whatever were their aggregates of moral discipline, of meditative absorption, of wisdom, of liberation, and of the gnosis and vision of liberation; whatever was their aspiration to benefit beings, whatever was their great loving-kindness, whatever was their great compassion, whatever were their immeasurable and innumerable buddha-qualities; whatever Dharma was taught by those buddhas, Blessed Ones, and whatever wholesome roots were produced by those who trained in, were devoted to, and abided in that Dharma; whatever wholesome roots accompanied by the six perfections belong to those bodhisattvas, those great beings, who were prophesied by those buddhas, Blessed Ones, to attain unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening; whatever wholesome roots belong to those individuals of the pratyekabuddha vehicle who were prophesied to attain their own awakening; whatever wholesome roots belong to those individuals of the śrāvaka vehicle arising from generosity, arising from moral discipline, arising from cultivation, whatever are the undefiled wholesome roots of those in training and whatever are the undefiled wholesome roots of those beyond training; whatever wholesome roots were generated in that Dharma by ordinary people; whatever wholesome roots were generated, upon hearing that Dharma, by gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, great serpents, humans, and non-humans; whatever wholesome roots were generated, upon hearing that Dharma, by sentient beings born in the animal realm; and whatever wholesome roots were generated by sentient beings toward those buddhas, Blessed Ones, as they were passing into nirvāṇa and after they had passed into nirvāṇa —

all of those wholesome roots, every single one, gathered together, summed up, measured, and made complete without remainder: one rejoices in all of that without remainder that is to be rejoiced in, and having rejoiced, if one dedicates that meritorious deed accompanied by rejoicing to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, how then does the bodhisattva, that great being who dedicates it, not fall into wrong perception, not fall into wrong mind, not fall into wrong view?"

When he had spoken these words —

The bodhisattva, that great being, Maitreya, spoke these words to the elder, the venerable Subhūti:

"Noble Subhūti, if the bodhisattva, that great being, does not come to perceive as mind the very mind by which he dedicates — if he does not take that mind to be mind — then in that way the bodhisattva, that great being, will have dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening the meritorious deed accompanied by rejoicing. For just as that mind does not rightly know that this mind is such-and-such, so in that way the bodhisattva, that great being's perception will not fall into wrong perception, his mind will not fall into wrong mind, and his view will not fall into wrong view.

But if he rightly knows and comes to perceive that the very mind by which he dedicates is this — that this mind is such-and-such — then in that way the bodhisattva, that great being's perception will fall into wrong perception, his mind will fall into wrong mind, and his view will fall into wrong view.

If, however, the bodhisattva, that great being, rightly knows and reflects on the mind by which he dedicates in the following way — knowing: 'When one attends to that mind, what has ceased has ceased, and it is thus known as ceased; it is free from arising, and it is transformed' — and knowing it in that way, he cannot dedicate what has ceased; and the nature of reality of the mind by which one dedicates is that very thing; the nature of reality of the phenomena by which one dedicates is that very thing; and the nature of reality of the phenomena to which one dedicates is also that very thing — if one dedicates in that way, then it is a true dedication and not a wrong dedication. That is how the bodhisattva, that great being, should dedicate.

Furthermore, noble Subhūti, the bodhisattva, that great being, just as with the past, so also with the future — those Blessed One buddhas whose stream of teaching has been cut off, whose entry into teaching has been cut off, whose conceptual elaboration has been cut off, and whose leading into continued existence has been cut off, immeasurable and innumerable — from the time those buddhas first aroused the awakening mind, up through the point at which they fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and up through the point at which they utterly pass into nirvāṇa in the realm of nirvāṇa without remainder of aggregates, and up through the point at which the true Dharma has not yet declined — all the wholesome roots of those Blessed One buddhas throughout that span that are accompanied by the perfections, and the aggregates of moral discipline, the aggregates of meditative absorption, the aggregates of wisdom, the aggregates of liberation, and the aggregates of the gnosis of the vision of liberation of those Blessed One buddhas, and whatever constitutes their aspiration to benefit, and whatever constitutes their great loving-kindness, and whatever constitutes their great compassion, and whatever constitutes the immeasurable and innumerable excellent qualities of those buddhas, and the Dharma that those Blessed One buddhas will teach, and those who will train in that Dharma, and those who will hold it with deep aspiration, and those who will abide in it — whatever constitutes the wholesome roots of all of these — and whatever constitutes the wholesome roots of those bodhisattvas endowed with the six perfections whom those Blessed One buddhas will prophesy to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — and whatever constitutes the wholesome roots of those individuals of the pratyekabuddha vehicle to whom those Blessed One buddhas will prophesy awakening as pratyekabuddhas —"

— and whatever constitutes the merit-producing basis that arises from generosity, and whatever constitutes the merit-producing basis that arises from moral discipline, and whatever constitutes the merit-producing basis that arises from meditation, on the part of those individuals belonging to the śrāvaka vehicle — and whatever constitutes the wholesome roots of the undefiled virtue belonging to those in training, and whatever constitutes the wholesome roots of the undefiled virtue belonging to those beyond training — and whatever wholesome roots ordinary persons will plant through that Dharma — and whatever gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, great serpents, humans, and non-humans will hear that Dharma and, having heard it, will plant wholesome roots — and whatever sentient beings reborn in the animal realm will hear that Dharma and, having heard it, will plant wholesome roots — and whatever wholesome roots sentient beings will plant in relation to the blessed one buddhas as they pass completely into nirvāṇa, and in relation to those who have completely passed into nirvāṇa —

all of these wholesome roots, every single one, are gathered together, summed up, measured, and made complete and without remainder; and then, rejoicing in all that is to be rejoiced in, without leaving anything out, having rejoiced — how is it that the bodhisattva, the great being, who dedicates to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening that merit-producing basis endowed with such rejoicing, does not fall into distorted perception, distorted mind, or distorted view?

If the bodhisattva, the great being, does not perceive as mind the very mind by which something is being dedicated, then in that way the bodhisattva, the great being, has genuinely dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening that merit-producing basis endowed with rejoicing. Because just as mind does not truly know that mind to be "this is that mind," in that very way the bodhisattva, the great being, does not fall into distorted perception, distorted mind, or distorted view.

However, if the bodhisattva, the great being, were to know and perceive as mind the very mind by which something is being dedicated, taking it to be "this is that mind," then in that case the bodhisattva, the great being, would fall into distorted perception, distorted mind, and distorted view.

If the bodhisattva, the great being, knows and thinks about the mind by which something is dedicated in this way — that when one thinks about that mind, one truly knows it as "exhausted in exhaustion," and understands it as having ceased, as freed from arising, as having undergone transformation — and if that which is exhausted also cannot itself be dedicated, and the nature of reality of the very mind by which one dedicates is also just that, and the nature of reality of the dharmas by means of which one dedicates is also just that, and the nature of reality of the dharmas to which one dedicates is also just that — if dedication proceeds in that way, it is genuine dedication and not false dedication. That is how the bodhisattva, the great being, should dedicate.

Moreover, noble Subhūti, the bodhisattva, the great being, should take up — from the first arousing of the mind right up through the manifest, perfect, complete awakening to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — those immeasurable and innumerable blessed one buddhas who are presently living, dwelling, and abiding throughout immeasurable and innumerable world-systems of great trichiliocosms, those whose stream of teaching has not been cut off, whose practice is uninterrupted, whose conceptual elaboration has been cut off, and whose leading into existence has been cut off —

until they pass into the nirvāṇa (Skt. nirvāṇa) that is the expanse free of any remainder of aggregates, and until the sublime Dharma has not yet declined — the wholesome roots accompanied by the perfections of those blessed one buddhas throughout that entire interval; the aggregates of moral discipline, the aggregates of meditative absorption, the aggregates of wisdom, the aggregates of liberation, and the aggregates of the wisdom-vision of liberation of those blessed one buddhas; whatever constitutes their intention to benefit beings; whatever constitutes their great loving-kindness and their great compassion; whatever constitutes the immeasurable and innumerable qualities of buddhahood; the wholesome roots of those who have trained in, are specially devoted to, and abide in the Dharma that those blessed one buddhas have taught; the wholesome roots accompanied by the six perfections of those bodhisattvas, those great beings, whom those blessed one buddhas have prophesied to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening; the wholesome roots of those individuals of the pratyekabuddha vehicle whom those blessed one buddhas have prophesied to the awakening of a pratyekabuddha; the bases of meritorious action arising from generosity, the bases of meritorious action arising from moral discipline, and the bases of meritorious action arising from cultivation of those individuals who belong to the śrāvaka vehicle; whatever constitutes the undefiled wholesome roots of those in training; whatever constitutes the undefiled wholesome roots of those beyond training; whatever wholesome roots ordinary persons have generated through that Dharma; whatever wholesome roots gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas (Skt. gandharva), asuras, garuḍas (Skt. garuḍa), kinnaras (Skt. kinnara), mahoragas (Skt. mahoraga), humans, and non-humans have generated upon hearing that Dharma and, having heard it, acting accordingly; whatever wholesome roots sentient beings who have taken birth in the animal realm have generated upon hearing that Dharma and, having heard it, acting accordingly; and whatever wholesome roots have been generated in relation to those blessed one buddhas as they pass into final nirvāṇa and after they have passed into final nirvāṇa —

all of those wholesome roots, without exception, gathered together, combined, measured, and reckoned in their entirety, one should rejoice in all of them, leaving none out. Then, having thus rejoiced, that bodhisattva, that great being, who dedicates to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening that basis of meritorious action accompanied by rejoicing — how is it that their perception does not become inverted, their mind does not become inverted, and their view does not become inverted?

If the bodhisattva, the great being, does not come to regard that very mind by which they dedicate as being a mind, then in that way the bodhisattva, the great being, dedicates that basis of meritorious action accompanied by rejoicing to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. Just as that mind does not truly know of itself "this mind is such-and-such," in that same way the bodhisattva, the great being, does not fall into inverted perception, does not fall into inverted mind, and does not fall into inverted view.

But if the bodhisattva, the great being, were to truly know and perceive that very mind by which they dedicate as being "this is a mind" — if they were to recognize it as a mind — then in that way the bodhisattva, the great being, would fall into inverted perception, inverted mind, and inverted view.

On the other hand, if the bodhisattva, the great being, knows and thinks in this way: when attending to that very mind by which one dedicates, one truly knows "what is exhausted is exhausted," and one knows it to be ceased, separated from arising, and transformed — then that which has been exhausted cannot be dedicated at all. The nature of reality of the very mind by which one dedicates is just that. The nature of reality of the phenomena by which one dedicates is also just that. The nature of reality of the phenomena to which one dedicates is also just that. If one dedicates in that way, it is a correct dedication and not a wrong dedication. That is how the bodhisattva, the great being, should dedicate.

Noble Subhūti, furthermore, the bodhisattva, the great being, beginning from the first arousal of the awakening mind by all buddhas, Blessed Ones of the past, future, and present — those who are immeasurable and innumerable, whose path is unbroken, whose entry is unbroken, whose conceptual elaboration is cut off, who have cut off what leads to continued existence — up to their unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, their complete and perfect awakening, and the interval of their complete and perfect awakening; and their complete and perfect passage into nirvāṇa in the realm of nirvāṇa without remainder of aggregates, the interval of their complete and perfect passage into nirvāṇa; and the sacred Dharma not declining, not coming to decline, and the interval before it has declined — the wholesome roots that are the perfections of those buddhas, Blessed Ones throughout that entire span; and the heaps of moral discipline, heaps of meditative absorption, heaps of wisdom, heaps of liberation, and heaps of the gnosis of the vision of liberation belonging to those buddhas, Blessed Ones; and whatever is their intention to benefit, whatever is their great loving-kindness, whatever is their great compassion, and whatever are the immeasurable and innumerable qualities of those buddhas; and whatever Dharma those buddhas, Blessed Ones have taught, will teach, and are teaching, and whoever has trained, will train, and is training in that Dharma, whoever has aspired to it, will aspire to it, and is aspiring to it, whoever has abided in it, will abide in it, and is abiding in it — all their wholesome roots; and whatever are the wholesome roots belonging to all those bodhisattvas, great beings, whom those buddhas, Blessed Ones have prophesied, will prophesy, and are prophesying to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening; and whatever are the wholesome roots belonging to all those individuals of the pratyekabuddha vehicle whom those buddhas, Blessed Ones have prophesied, will prophesy, and are prophesying to their own awakening; and whatever are the wholesome roots arising from generosity, arising from moral discipline, and arising from cultivation belonging to individuals of the śrāvaka vehicle, along with whatever are their wholesome roots that are the undefiled roots of those still in training, and whatever are the undefiled wholesome roots of those beyond training;

and whatever wholesome roots have been, will be, and are being cultivated in these teachings by ordinary beings; and whatever gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans have heard, will hear, and are hearing these teachings, and having heard them have generated, will generate, and are generating wholesome roots; and whatever sentient beings born in the realms of animals have heard, will hear, and are hearing this noble Dharma, and having heard it have cultivated, will cultivate, and are cultivating wholesome roots; and whatever wholesome roots have been, will be, and are being cultivated by sentient beings in relation to those buddhas, Blessed Ones who have passed, will pass, and are passing into complete nirvāṇa — all those wholesome roots of all of these, gathered and summed together, measured and made complete without remainder, are to be rejoiced in without exception. With the supreme rejoicing, with that highest and most excellent rejoicing, one rejoices in them as the most excellent, the chief, the supreme, the highest, the sublime, the great, the unsurpassed, the without-superior, the peerless, the incomparable, the equal of the equal. Having so rejoiced and possessing the merit arising from that rejoicing, one then dedicates the unsurpassed meritorious basis to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, thinking: "May this serve to perfectly accomplish unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening."

Now when one dedicates in this way by expressing it in words, thinking, "I dedicate so that these phenomena, being exhausted, ceased, departed, and transformed, will be dedicated to a place where they will not be exhausted," how is it that a bodhisattva, a great being, does not fall into perversion of perception, does not fall into perversion of mind, does not fall into perversion of view?

If the one who is dedicating thinks: "These phenomena are exhausted, ceased, departed, and transformed; therefore I shall dedicate to that into which they will not become exhausted" — thinking in that way, one has dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

And if again one reflects, thinking: "It is not that a phenomenon is dedicated by a phenomenon" — thinking in that way as well, one has dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Venerable Subhūti, a bodhisattva, a great being who dedicates in this manner does not fall into perversion of perception, does not fall into perversion of mind, does not fall into perversion of view. And why? Because that one has no clinging in relation to that dedication.

Furthermore, if one correctly understands, thinking: "Mind does not know mind, and a phenomenon does not know a phenomenon" — thinking in that way as well, one has dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. This is the unsurpassed dedication of a bodhisattva, a great being.

However, if a bodhisattva, a great being, apprehends the formation of merit, then that is not a dedication to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And why? Because that one has clinging in relation to that dedication.

But if one thinks: "That very formation of merit is empty and peaceful, and the meritorious basis that is endowed with rejoicing is likewise empty and peaceful" — thinking in that way, one has dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

But if one does not even know in this way — thinking "all formations are empty and peaceful" — then this too is the Perfection of Wisdom of that bodhisattva, that great being.

Whatever roots of virtue were dedicated by those blessed buddhas (bhagavat) who have passed completely into nirvāṇa, whatever that dedication was like, this root of virtue is exactly like that. And whoever has dedicated it in this way is one who belongs to that class, who has that characteristic mark, who is included in that category, who has that intrinsic nature. If one knows it in this way, then it has not been dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And why? Because the blessed buddhas do not make their dedication in the manner of apprehending signs. The past is spent, ceased, departed, and transformed. The future has not yet been reached. In what has arisen in the present, no abiding is perceived. And what is not perceived is not a sign and is not an object. If one makes it a sign and thinks of it in that way, then it has not been dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

If, freed from mindfulness, one does not make it a sign and does not think of it — or if, freed from mindfulness or lacking comprehension, one does not take it to mind — then in that case too it has not been dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. But if one takes that sign to mind while not apprehending it as a sign, then in that way the bodhisattva, the great being, has dedicated that root of virtue to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

This is how a bodhisattva, a great being, should train in this. This should be understood as the skillful means (upāya) of that bodhisattva, that great being. This dedication of roots of virtue through these skillful means is close to omniscience.

A bodhisattva, a great being, who wishes to train in these skillful means should, without interruption, listen to this very Perfection of Wisdom, hold it, retain it, read it, master it thoroughly, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it, transmit it as scripture, chant it, and inquire into it completely. And why? Because one who has not heard the Perfection of Wisdom, relying upon it, cannot enter into this activity of dedicating through the Perfection of Wisdom.

If someone were to say: "Even without relying on the Perfection of Wisdom, one is able to dedicate that meritorious basis to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening" — one should tell that person not to speak in such a way. And why? Because those very things, those material entities, have ceased, and those very formations have also ceased — they are peaceful, empty, and free from any object of apprehension. And yet that person, having apprehended and examined what is genuine reality as a sign, perceives what is not genuinely reality as if it were genuinely reality — and thus dedicates, apprehending what is without object as though it had an object. Such a dedication of that root of virtue to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening is not something the blessed buddhas would sanction. And why? Because whatever constitutes the apprehending of signs, conceptual proliferation, and focused objectification with regard to the complete nirvāṇa of the blessed buddhas — that itself is the great object of apprehension for that one.

The Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and completely awakened buddhas do not declare that a dedication accompanied by the perception of an object accomplishes great purpose. And why? Because such a dedication is poisonous and painful — like a fine food that is poisoned. Just as such food, though attractive in its colour, fragrance, taste, and texture, ought to be cast aside, yet because it is poisonous the wise must utterly abandon it and must not consume it. But one who by nature is childish and by nature deficient in wisdom thinks to enjoy it, and when he eats that food, through its colour, fragrance, taste, and texture it gives him pleasure in the tasting — yet when it dissolves inside him, its karmic maturation becomes one of suffering, and he suffers from that very basis unto death, or to a degree of suffering that is like death.

Noble Subhūti, in just the same way there are those here who, having grasped wrongly, cognized wrongly, and recited wrongly, neither know the meaning of what has been well spoken nor have rightly understood the genuine meaning as it truly is. These people say the following: "Come here, son of good family! Gather together in one sum all the roots of virtue of all those blessed buddhas of the past, future, and present who dwell in immeasurable, incalculable world-realms of all worlds — from the first arousing of their awakening mind right up through their manifesting complete and perfect awakening in unsurpassed, perfect, complete enlightenment, and onward through their passing fully into nirvāṇa in the realm of nirvāṇa that leaves no remainder of aggregates, and through the interval of their passing; all of this during whatever period the noble Dharma has not yet declined, is not yet declining, and has not declined — the roots of virtue of the blessed buddhas of the past, future, and present during that interval, together with those of their śrāvakas: their aggregate of moral discipline, their aggregate of meditative absorption, their aggregate of wisdom, their aggregate of liberation, and their aggregate of the gnosis that sees liberation; together with the roots of virtue created and to be created by those bodhisattvas, great bodhisattvas, who have been prophesied by those blessed buddhas to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, or who will be so prophesied, and together with whatever roots of virtue those who have been prophesied have established and will establish; together with the roots of virtue created and to be created by those individuals of the pratyekabuddha vehicle who have been prophesied, or who will be prophesied, to their own solitary awakening, and whatever roots of virtue those who have been prophesied have established and will establish; together with the roots of virtue created and to be created by those individuals of the śrāvaka vehicle who have been prophesied, or who will be prophesied, to the awakening of a hearer, and whatever roots of virtue those who have been prophesied have established and will establish; together with whatever roots of virtue ordinary beings have established, will establish, and are establishing there — gather all these roots of virtue together into one, sum them up, assess them in their entirety, leaving nothing out, and then, leaving nothing out, rejoice in all of them; and having so rejoiced, dedicate that meritorious act of supreme worthiness to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening." In this way they proclaim and teach this. Yet a dedication of that sort, being a dedication performed in the mode of signs, becomes poison — like that poisoned food. Therefore there is no dedication for one who holds the perception of an object. And why?

Object-fixation is poison. Therefore no person who is a follower of the bodhisattva vehicle should train in that way.

Then how should one train, and how should one take up the roots of virtue of all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones, of the past, the future, and the present? And how, having taken them up, does one take them up well? And how should one dedicate them, and how, having dedicated them, does one dedicate them well to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening?

On this point, a son of good family or daughter of good family who follows the bodhisattva vehicle and wishes to do no harm to the Tathāgatas should rejoice in all those roots of virtue in the following way and dedicate them in the following way. Whatever that root of virtue may be, one should rejoice in it just as the Tathāgatas — the arhats, the perfectly and completely awakened buddhas — know and see it with the wisdom of a buddha and the eye of a buddha; rejoicing in it just as it is with respect to what class it belongs to, what category it falls under, what kind it is, what its intrinsic nature is, what its defining characteristic is, and what its dharmic nature consists in. And with the thought, "Just as those Tathāgatas — the arhats, the perfectly and completely awakened buddhas — approved of the dedication of that root of virtue to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, so too do I rejoice," one should rejoice and dedicate accordingly.

A bodhisattva, a great being, who rejoices and dedicates in this way does no harm to the buddhas, the Blessed Ones; the rejoicing is genuine rejoicing, and that root of virtue is well and thoroughly dedicated to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening; and that person does no harm to the Tathāgatas — the arhats, the perfectly and completely awakened buddhas. Such a dedication, performed through aspiration and with elevated intention, is a dedication that is free of poison, a great dedication, a dedication within the dharmadhātu — complete and supremely complete.
