Whatever is intrinsic nature is the absence of intrinsic nature; and whatever is intrinsic nature is intrinsic nature — that is to say, because all phenomena lack a characteristic mark, they are of one characteristic mark. Subhūti, for that reason the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete buddha, has not perfectly and completely awakened to all phenomena. Why is that? Subhūti, the intrinsic nature of phenomena is non-dual; the intrinsic nature of all phenomena is nothing other than one."

"Subhūti, whatever is the intrinsic nature of all phenomena — that is the absence of intrinsic nature; and whatever is the absence of intrinsic nature — that is intrinsic nature. Subhūti, therefore all those extremes of attachment are entirely abandoned."

Subhūti then asked: "Blessed One, is the Perfection of Wisdom profound?"

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom is profound because space is profound."

Subhūti asked: "Blessed One, is the Perfection of Wisdom difficult to realize?"

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, in this way, no one whatsoever perfectly and completely awakens."

Subhūti asked: "Blessed One, is the Perfection of Wisdom inconceivable?"

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom is not something to be known by the mind, nor is it something to be realized by the mind."

Subhūti asked: "Blessed One, is the Perfection of Wisdom uncreated?"

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom is uncreated because no agent is to be found."

Subhūti asked: "Blessed One, in that case, how should a bodhisattva, a great being, engage in the Perfection of Wisdom?"

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, if a bodhisattva, a great being, engages in the Perfection of Wisdom by not engaging in form, then that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise, if one engages without engaging in feeling, without engaging in perception, without engaging in formations, and if one engages without engaging in consciousness — that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom.

If one engages without entertaining the thought 'form is impermanent,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise with feeling, perception, formations, and if one engages without entertaining the thought 'consciousness is impermanent,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom.

If one engages without entertaining the thought 'form is empty,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise without doing so with feeling, without doing so with perception, without doing so with formations, and if one engages without entertaining the thought 'consciousness is empty,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom.

If one engages without entertaining the thought 'form is incomplete or complete,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. For whatever is incompleteness or completeness of form — that is not form. And if one also does not engage in that way, that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Likewise with feeling, with perception, with formations, and if one engages without entertaining the thought 'consciousness is incomplete or complete,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. For whatever is incompleteness or completeness of consciousness — that is not consciousness. And if one also does not engage in that way, that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom."

When this had been declared, the venerable Subhūti said to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, it is wondrous that for so long as it is the case, the having of attachment and the being free of attachment among bodhisattvas, great beings, should be taught in just this way."

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, if a bodhisattva, a great being, engages without entertaining the thought 'form has attachment' or 'form is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise with feeling, perception, formations, and if one engages without entertaining the thought 'consciousness has attachment' or 'consciousness is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom."

"Subhūti, if a bodhisattva, a great being, engages without entertaining the thought 'the eye has attachment' or 'the eye is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise, up through the feelings arisen from mental contact, if one engages without entertaining the thought 'this has attachment' or 'this is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. If one engages without entertaining the thought 'the earth element has attachment' or 'the earth element is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise, up through the consciousness element, if one engages without entertaining the thought 'this has attachment' or 'this is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. If one engages without entertaining the thought 'the perfection of generosity has attachment' or 'the perfection of generosity is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise with the perfection of moral discipline, the perfection of patience, the perfection of diligence, the perfection of meditative concentration, and the perfection of wisdom — if one engages without entertaining the thought 'this has attachment' or 'this is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise with the thirty-seven factors of awakening, the powers, the fearlessnesses, the individual perfect knowledges, and the eighteen unshared qualities of a buddha — if one engages without entertaining the thought 'these have attachment' or 'these are free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. If one engages without entertaining the thought 'the fruit of stream-entry has attachment' or 'the fruit of stream-entry is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise with the fruit of once-returning, the fruit of non-returning, and arhatship — if one engages without entertaining the thought 'these have attachment' or 'these are free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. If one engages without entertaining the thought 'pratyekabuddhahood has attachment' or 'pratyekabuddhahood is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. If one engages without entertaining the thought 'buddhahood has attachment' or 'buddhahood is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom. Subhūti, if one engages without entertaining the thought 'omniscience itself has attachment' or 'omniscience itself is free of attachment,' that is engagement in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Subhūti, a bodhisattva, a great being, who engages in this way generates no attachment to form. Likewise, no attachment is generated to feeling, to perception, to formations, nor to consciousness. Subhūti, a bodhisattva, a great being, who engages in this way generates no attachment to the eye, and so on up through the feelings arisen from mental contact. No attachment is generated to the earth element, and so on up through the consciousness element. No attachment is generated to the perfection of generosity, nor to the perfection of moral discipline, nor to the perfection of patience, nor to the perfection of diligence, nor to the perfection of meditative concentration, nor to the perfection of wisdom. No attachment is generated to the factors of awakening, nor to the powers, nor to the fearlessnesses, nor to the individual perfect knowledges, nor to the eighteen unshared qualities of a buddha. No attachment is generated to the fruit of stream-entry, nor to the fruit of once-returning, nor to the fruit of non-returning, nor to arhatship. No attachment is generated to pratyekabuddhahood. No attachment is generated to buddhahood. No attachment whatsoever is generated even to omniscience.

Why is this?"

"Subhūti, omniscience itself is not attached, not bound, not liberated, not fully passed beyond. Subhūti, it is for this reason — in order to be completely free from all attachment — that a bodhisattva, a great being, should practice the Perfection of Wisdom."

Subhūti then said: "Blessed One, this phenomenon called the Perfection of Wisdom is profound. It is wonderful that whether it is taught or not taught it does not diminish, and whether it is taught or not taught it does not increase."

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One said to the venerable Subhūti: "Excellent, Subhūti! Excellent! Subhūti, it is exactly so, exactly so. Consider this example, Subhūti: for as long as a Tathāgata, an arhat, a fully and perfectly awakened buddha, remains alive, even if praises of space are uttered, space does not increase in the slightest; and even if no praises are uttered, space does not diminish in the slightest. Consider also, Subhūti, this example: if praises are uttered of an illusory man, no attachment arises and no affliction arises; and if no praises are uttered, no anger arises and no affliction arises. In the same way, Subhūti, the nature of reality of phenomena is such that whether it is taught or not taught, it is just that much — it is exhausted at just that."

The elder Subhūti then said: "Blessed One, any bodhisattva, any great being, who practices this profound Perfection of Wisdom and cultivates the Perfection of Wisdom without becoming discouraged, without becoming hesitant, yet who also brings it into practice — it is indeed difficult to be one who does not turn back. Blessed One, cultivating the Perfection of Wisdom in this way is like cultivating space. Blessed One, those great beings, those bodhisattvas, who have put on this armor are worthy of reverence. Why is this? Blessed One, one who aspires to put on armor for the sake of sentient beings aspires to put on armor together with space itself. One who aspires to put on armor for the sake of sentient beings who are equal to space, who are equal to the dharmadhātu — that one, Blessed One, is a bodhisattva, a great being who has put on the great armor; that one, Blessed One, is a bodhisattva, a great being who is a hero. One who aspires to attain manifest, complete, unsurpassed, and perfect awakening aspires, Blessed One, to complete liberation in space. That one aspires, Blessed One, to lift up space. Blessed One, a bodhisattva, a great being who aspires to armor for the sake of sentient beings equal to space and equal to the dharmadhātu — that one has attained the great armor of the perfection of diligence."

Then another monk, joining his palms together and bowing in the direction of the Blessed One, said to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, I pay homage to the Perfection of Wisdom. Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom neither produces any phenomenon nor suppresses any phenomenon."

Then Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, spoke to the venerable Subhūti in these words: "Noble Subhūti, one who brings this Perfection of Wisdom into practice in such a way — what would they be bringing into practice?" Subhūti replied: "Kauśika, one who brings this Perfection of Wisdom into practice would be bringing space into practice."

"Kauśika, one who intends to train in and practice this Perfection of Wisdom will come to practice what is without superior."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, what protection, guarding, and sheltering should be done for a son of good family or daughter of good family who comes to uphold this Perfection of Wisdom? Please declare this." Then the venerable Subhūti spoke to Śakra, lord of the gods, as follows: "Kauśika, do you truly perceive any phenomenon with respect to which protection, guarding, and sheltering are to be done?" Śakra replied: "Noble Subhūti, I do not." Subhūti said: "Kauśika, if a bodhisattva, a great being, abides in this Perfection of Wisdom just as it has been taught, that very abiding is itself the protection, guarding, and sheltering of it. But if one becomes separated from the Perfection of Wisdom, then those humans and non-humans who watch for an opportunity and seek an opportunity will find their opportunity. Kauśika, furthermore, one who thinks to protect, guard, and shelter a bodhisattva, a great being, who is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom is thinking to protect, guard, and shelter space itself. Kauśika, what do you think — can you protect, guard, and shelter an echo?" Śakra replied: "Noble Subhūti, that is not possible." Subhūti said: "Kauśika, in just the same way, a bodhisattva, a great being, who practices and abides in this Perfection of Wisdom thoroughly knows all phenomena to be like an echo. Therefore one does not become conceited about them, does not truly perceive them, does not truly know them, those phenomena do not exist and do not truly appear, they do not truly exist and are not apprehended — abiding in this way, one practices the Perfection of Wisdom."

Then, by the power of the buddha, all four great kings throughout the great trichiliocosm (the vast world-system of three thousandfold thousands of worlds), all the Śakras lords of the gods, all the Great Brahmas, and all the Great Brahmas who are lords of the Akaniṣṭha realm came to where the Blessed One was, and having arrived they bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, circumambulated the Blessed One three times, and stood to one side. Standing to one side, those great kings, all the Śakras lords of the gods, all the Great Brahmas, and the Great Brahma lord of Akaniṣṭha, by the power of the buddha and the blessing of the buddha, came to know that a thousand buddhas, using these very names, these very words, these very letters, and this very monk called Subhūti, had taught this very Perfection of Wisdom and this very chapter of the Perfection of Wisdom in this very place; and that the Śakras lords of the gods themselves had questioned and inquired about it in that same place, and that this very Perfection of Wisdom had been taught here; and that the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya (Loving-kindness), upon fully and perfectly awakening to unsurpassed complete awakening, would also teach this very Perfection of Wisdom in this very place.

This is the eighth chapter, entitled "Purification," from the noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines.

Then the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, what is called the Perfection of Wisdom is a mere name. That name too is not apprehended as something that exists as 'this.' What we call a name is simply the entity of a word. That Perfection of Wisdom itself also does not exist and is not apprehended. Just as a name is, so too is the Perfection of Wisdom; and just as the Perfection of Wisdom is, so too is a name. Since these two phenomena do not exist and are not apprehended, Blessed One, for what reason will the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya, after fully and perfectly awakening to unsurpassed complete awakening, teach this very Perfection of Wisdom in this very place, with these very names, these very words, and these very syllables?"

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One declared to the venerable Subhūti: "Subhūti, it is thus: the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya, will fully and perfectly awaken, having fully and perfectly realized that form is neither permanent nor impermanent, neither bound nor free, transcending all extremes and utterly pure. Likewise with feeling, perception, and formations — and with consciousness: neither permanent nor impermanent, neither bound nor free, transcending all extremes and utterly pure. It is for this reason, Subhūti, that the bodhisattva, the great being, Maitreya, after fully and perfectly awakening to unsurpassed complete awakening, will teach this very Perfection of Wisdom in this very place, with these very names, these very words, and these very syllables."

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, how wondrous! This Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure."

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, by the purity of form, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, likewise, by the purity of feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, by form's having no arising and no cessation, no defilement and no purification, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, likewise, by feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness having no arising and no cessation, no defilement and no purification, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, by the purity of space, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, because form is unstained and utterly without grasping, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, likewise, because feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are unstained and utterly without grasping, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, because space has no echo and is inexpressible and without object, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure. Subhūti, because all phenomena, whether staining or unstaining, have no stain, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly pure."

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words:

"Blessed One, those sons of good family or daughters of good family in whose ears this Perfection of Wisdom has come to resound have also obtained something well obtained. How much more so those who will hold it, carry it, read it, master it thoroughly, recite it aloud, teach it, expound it in detail, chant the scriptures, and repeat it by heart — for them there will arise no disease of the eyes, no disease of the ears, no disease of the nose, no disease of the tongue, no disease of the body will arise. They will not fall into confusion. Because they do not abandon their composure, they will not meet with untimely death. Many hundreds of thousands of divine sons will follow close behind them. On the eighth, the fourteenth, and the fifteenth of the month, wherever any son of good family or daughter of good family who teaches the Dharma comes to expound this Perfection of Wisdom, there and in each such place they will generate exceedingly great merit."

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One declared to the venerable Subhūti: "So it is, Subhūti, so it is. Subhūti, many hundreds of thousands of gods will follow close behind that son of good family or daughter of good family, step by step. Many hundreds of thousands of gods will come to that place. All of them, wishing to hear the Dharma from that teacher who expounds this Perfection of Wisdom, will be guarded, protected, and sheltered. And why is that? Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom is the unsurpassed jewel of the world together with its gods, humans, and asuras. Subhūti, for that reason as well, that son of good family or daughter of good family will generate great merit on that basis.

"Subhūti, nevertheless, for one who writes out this profound Perfection of Wisdom, holds it, carries it, reads it, masters it thoroughly, recites it aloud, teaches it, expounds it in detail, chants the scriptures, and repeats it by heart, many obstacles will arise. And why is that? Subhūti, a great jewel draws many enemies: the more precious it is held to be, the weightier the enmity it draws. Subhūti, this is how it is: this great jewel, the Perfection of Wisdom, is the unsurpassed jewel in the world. It has entered for the benefit and happiness of the world, and it abides indestructibly in order that all phenomena may be seen as unborn and unceasing, and in order that there may be no affliction whatsoever.

"Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom does not abide in any phenomenon, does not engage with any phenomenon, and does not grasp any phenomenon. And why is that? Subhūti, all those phenomena are nonexistent and cannot be apprehended. Subhūti, because it is unborn, the Perfection of Wisdom is unstained. Subhūti, this very word 'unstained' is the Perfection of Wisdom. Subhūti, because form is unstained, the Perfection of Wisdom is unstained. Likewise, because feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are unstained, Subhūti, this Perfection of Wisdom is unstained.

"Subhūti, even if a bodhisattva, a great being, does not know it to be so, he still practices the Perfection of Wisdom. Subhūti, moreover, this Perfection of Wisdom does not lead anyone into any phenomenon, does not lead anyone decisively into any phenomenon, does not reveal any phenomenon truly, does not reveal any phenomenon definitively, does not cause any phenomenon to be obtained, and does not cause any phenomenon to be definitively obtained."

Then many thousands of divine beings (devas) appeared in the sky, rejoicing and making expressions of wonder, scattering their garments and saying: "How marvelous! It appears that the wheel of Dharma has been turned a second time in Jambudvīpa!"

Then the Blessed One spoke these words to the venerable elder Subhūti: "Subhūti, this is not the wheel of Dharma being turned a second time. With respect to any phenomenon whatsoever, there is neither setting in motion nor reversal. Subhūti, it is precisely thus that this is the Perfection of Wisdom of bodhisattvas, great beings."

When the Blessed One had said this, the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, this is the great perfection of that bodhisattva, that great being, who has no attachment to any phenomenon whatsoever, who aspires to fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, complete, and perfect awakening yet does not fully and perfectly awaken to any phenomenon whatsoever, and who turns the wheel of Dharma yet does not truly reveal any phenomenon whatsoever. Why is this so? Because there is no phenomenon that could be apprehended and no phenomenon that could be revealed, and there is no one who could turn anything for anyone. Why is this? Because, Blessed One, all phenomena are utterly unestablished, and no phenomenon undergoes reversal or setting in motion. Why is this? Because, Blessed One, all phenomena are unestablished from the very beginning, since all phenomena are empty of inherent nature."

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke these words to the venerable Subhūti: "Subhūti, it is so, it is so. Subhūti, emptiness neither enters in nor reverses. Subhūti, the signless likewise neither enters in nor reverses. Subhūti, the wishless likewise neither enters in nor reverses. Subhūti, in teaching as has been taught here, all phenomena have been taught. Yet no one has taught, no one has heard, no one has understood, no one makes anything directly present, no one is making anything directly present, and no one will make anything directly present. Through this teaching of Dharma, no one has passed fully into nirvāṇa, will pass fully into nirvāṇa, or does not pass fully into nirvāṇa. And through this teaching of Dharma, no one has been established as a recipient of generosity."

When this was said, the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, because space does not exist, this is the Perfection of Non-existence. Blessed One, because all phenomena are equally without any referential object, this is the Perfection of Sameness with what is unequal. Blessed One, because of utter emptiness, this is the Perfection of Solitude. Blessed One, because all phenomena have no referential object, this is the Perfection of Non-deception. Blessed One, because there is neither name nor body, this is the Perfection of Non-abiding. Blessed One, because there is neither going nor coming, this is the Perfection of Non-entity. Blessed One, because all phenomena have no conceptual elaboration, this is the Perfection of Inexpressibility. Blessed One, because the five aggregates have no referential object, this is the Perfection of Namelessness. Blessed One, because all phenomena have no going, this is the Perfection of Non-going. Blessed One, because all phenomena have nothing to be grasped, this is the Perfection of Non-seizure."

Blessed One, because all phenomena are possessed of non-relinquishment, this is the Perfection of Non-relinquishment. Blessed One, because all phenomena are not manifestly established, this is the Perfection of Non-arising of All Phenomena. Blessed One, because no agent is to be apprehended, this is the Perfection of No Agent. Blessed One, because all phenomena have no self, this is the Perfection of No Knower. Blessed One, because death, transference, and birth do not arise, this is the Perfection of Non-transference. Blessed One, because the past extreme, the future extreme, and the present moment are not to be apprehended, this is the Perfection of No Discipline. Blessed One, because non-arising is made known as consciousness, this is the Perfection of Dream, Echo, Optical Illusion, Mirage, and Magical Apparition. Blessed One, because attachment, aversion, and delusion have no essential nature, this is the Perfection of No Affliction Whatsoever. Blessed One, because no support is to be apprehended, this is the Perfection of No Purification. Blessed One, because space is unstained, this is the Perfection of Unstainedness. Blessed One, because all conceit is transcended, this is the Perfection of No Conceptual Elaboration. Blessed One, because there is no agitation, this is the Perfection of No Conceit. Blessed One, because the dharmadhātu, the basic space of phenomena, simply abides, this is the Perfection of Immovability. Blessed One, because all phenomena are without inversion in their suchness, this is the Perfection of Freedom from Attachment. Blessed One, because all phenomena are free from conceptuality, this is the Perfection of No Uprising. Blessed One, because the signs of all phenomena are not to be apprehended, this is the Perfection of Peace. Blessed One, because of the perfection of qualities, this is the Perfection of No Fault. Blessed One, because there is no thorough conceptualization, this is the Perfection of No Affliction. Blessed One, because of the ultimate extreme of reality, this is the Perfection of No Sentient Being. Blessed One, because all phenomena have no uprising, this is the Perfection of No Measure. Blessed One, because all phenomena are without fixed adherence, this is the Perfection of Not Apprehending the Two Extremes. Blessed One, because all phenomena are without division, this is the Perfection of Non-differentiation. Blessed One, because śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas have no aspiration toward everything, this is the Perfection of Not Grasping at the Supreme. Blessed One, because conceptuality is inherently at peace, this is the Perfection of Non-conceptuality. Blessed One, because the nature of reality itself is beyond measure, this is the Perfection of the Immeasurable. Blessed One, because all phenomena are without attachment, this is the Perfection of Non-attachment. Blessed One, because all phenomena are unconditioned, this is the Perfection of Impermanence. Blessed One, because all phenomena are equal to space, this is the Perfection of Suffering. Blessed One, because all phenomena are without referential object, this is the Perfection of Emptiness.

Blessed One, because all phenomena are without manifest attachment, this is the Perfection of No-Self. Blessed One, because all phenomena are without manifest establishment, this is the Perfection of Signlessness. Blessed One, because there are no extremities or boundaries whatsoever, this is the Perfection of All Emptiness. Blessed One, because the factors of awakening are without referential object, this is the Perfection of the factors pertaining to awakening, beginning with the close placements of mindfulness. Blessed One, because the three doors of liberation are without referential object, this is the Perfection of Emptiness, Signlessness, and Wishlessness. Blessed One, because these are without referential object, this is the Perfection of the Eight Liberations. Blessed One, because the first dhyāna and the rest are without referential object, this is the Perfection of the Nine Sequential Meditative Absorptions. Blessed One, because suffering and so forth are without referential object, this is the Perfection of the Four Noble Truths. Blessed One, because generosity and so forth are without referential object, this is the Perfection of the Ten Grounds. Blessed One, because there is no forgetting, this is the Perfection of Powers. Blessed One, because there is utter absence of timidity, this is the Perfection of Fearlessness. Blessed One, because all gnosis is without attachment and without obstruction, this is the Perfection of the Individual Perfect Knowledges. Blessed One, because it utterly transcends all enumeration, this is the Perfection of all the unshared qualities of a buddha. Blessed One, because all phenomena abide in the suchness of non-deviation, this is the Perfection of the Tathāgata's Suchness. Blessed One, because all phenomena are without inherent nature, this is the Perfection of Self-Arising. Blessed One, because all aspects of the inherent nature of all phenomena are perfectly known, this very Perfection of Wisdom is the Perfection of the Gnosis of Omniscience.

This concludes the ninth chapter of the noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines, entitled "The Chapter of Praise."

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines. The Tenth Fascicle.

Then Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, reflected thus: those sons of good family and daughters of good family into whose ears this Perfection of Wisdom comes to resound will have previously performed service to the Victors of the past, will have planted roots of virtue in the presence of many buddhas, and will have been embraced by good spiritual friends. How much more is this true, then, of those who come to hold, retain, read, thoroughly master, proclaim aloud, teach, expound, recite from transmission, and chant this Perfection of Wisdom so profound as this — and who, having held, retained, read, thoroughly mastered, proclaimed aloud, taught, expounded, recited from transmission, and chanted it, then train in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness. Those persons will not have only meager roots of virtue. Those sons of good family and daughters of good family will have paid homage and made offerings to many buddhas.

And those sons of good family and daughters of good family who will now likewise hear this Perfection of Wisdom, and having heard it will hold it, retain it, read it, thoroughly master it, proclaim it aloud, teach it, expound it, recite it from transmission, and chant it, and who will train in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness — those sons of good family and daughters of good family will have heard this Perfection of Wisdom from those Tathāgatas, foe-destroyers, and perfectly complete buddhas of former times, and will have thoroughly inquired of and questioned the Blessed Buddhas concerning this Perfection of Wisdom. Know that those sons of good family and daughters of good family who will not be intimidated, not utterly disheartened, not embarrassed, not ashamed, not turn their minds away, not shrink back and turn away, not be frightened, not be wholly terrified, not fall into habitual terror when this Perfection of Wisdom so profound as this is taught, expounded, and explained — know that such persons have planted roots of virtue in the presence of many buddhas.

Then the venerable Śāriputra, having known with his own mind the following thought arising in the mind of Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, addressed the Blessed One in these words: "Blessed One, whatever son of good family or daughter of good family, having generated sincere faith, reflection, and aspiration, and having aroused the mind turned toward awakening regarding this very profound Perfection of Wisdom — its being taught, expounded, and explained — and who will hold, retain, read, thoroughly master, proclaim aloud, teach, expound, recite from transmission, chant, train in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness in relation to this Perfection of Wisdom: I will regard such a person as being, in every respect, a bodhisattva, a great being, a non-returning bodhisattva. And why? Blessed One, this Perfection of Wisdom is profound. Those who are of meager roots of virtue, who have not directly heard or thoroughly inquired of the Blessed Buddhas, who are of such a nature that they have not practiced thus in former times — such persons are incapable of generating aspiration toward this Perfection of Wisdom so profound as this. Blessed One, those sons of good family and daughters of good family who, without aspiring to it, intending to abandon it through non-comprehension — those persons likewise abandoned this very Perfection of Wisdom so profound even in former times when it was taught. And why? Blessed One, because of meager roots of virtue and because they did not practice it in former times, they are utterly incapable of generating any aspiration toward this profound Perfection of Wisdom. And those who will abandon it when this profound Perfection of Wisdom is taught — know them likewise: even in former times they abandoned this very profound Perfection of Wisdom when it was taught. For they had no faith in the teaching of this profound Perfection of Wisdom, no forbearance, no craving for it, no aspiration, no diligence, no clarity of mind, and no devotion toward it. Those persons did not thoroughly inquire of — did not put questions to — the former Blessed Buddhas and the śrāvakas of the buddhas."

Thereupon Śakra, lord of the gods, said to the venerable Śāriputra: "Noble Śāriputra, the Perfection of Wisdom is profound.

That former bodhisattvas, great beings, would fail to be devoted to the teaching of this profound Perfection of Wisdom — what wonder is there in that?

Thereupon Śakra, lord of the gods, said to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, I pay homage to the Perfection of Wisdom. Blessed One, whoever pays homage to the Perfection of Wisdom pays homage to the wisdom of omniscience."

The Blessed One declared: "Kauśika, it is so, it is exactly so. Kauśika, whoever pays homage to the Perfection of Wisdom pays homage to the wisdom of omniscience. And why is that? Kauśika, the omniscience of the blessed buddhas arises from this very source. And because it arises from the wisdom of omniscience, the Perfection of Wisdom is supremely distinguished. Therefore one should practise this Perfection of Wisdom. One should abide in this Perfection of Wisdom. One should train in yoga upon this Perfection of Wisdom."

Thereupon Śakra, lord of the gods, said again to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, how is it that a bodhisattva, a great being, while practising the Perfection of Wisdom, abides in the Perfection of Wisdom? And how, while practising the Perfection of Wisdom, does one train in yoga upon the Perfection of Wisdom?"

When this was asked, the Blessed One said to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Excellent, Kauśika, excellent! Kauśika, that you intend to inquire of the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly and completely awakened Buddha about this matter, and are inquiring — this too is well done by you. Kauśika, this boldness of yours has also arisen through the power of the Buddha.

Kauśika, when a bodhisattva, a great being, practises the Perfection of Wisdom, he does not dwell in form as though thinking 'this is form,' and he does not abide in it. Kauśika, it is precisely in this way — by not dwelling in form as though thinking 'this is form' — that a bodhisattva, a great being, trains in yoga with respect to form. Likewise, he does not dwell in feeling, perception, formations, or consciousness as though thinking 'this is consciousness,' and he does not abide in it. Kauśika, it is precisely in this way — by not dwelling in consciousness as though thinking 'this is consciousness' — that a bodhisattva, a great being, trains in yoga with respect to consciousness.

Kauśika, he does not apply the designation 'form.' Kauśika, by not applying the designation 'form' in this way, he does not abide in form. Kauśika, likewise he does not apply the designation 'feeling,' 'perception,' 'formations,' or 'consciousness.' Kauśika, by not applying the designation 'consciousness' in this way, he does not abide in consciousness. Abiding thus and training in yoga thus — that is how one abides in and trains in yoga upon the Perfection of Wisdom."

Thereupon the venerable Śāriputra said to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom is profound. Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom is unfathomable. Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom is difficult to grasp. Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom is immeasurable."

The Blessed One declared: "Śāriputra, it is so, it is exactly so. Śāriputra, one does not abide thinking 'form is profound.'

Śāriputra, it is because one does not abide thinking 'form is profound' that one trains in yoga upon form. Likewise with feeling, perception, and formations. Śāriputra, one does not abide thinking 'consciousness is profound,' and it is because one does not abide thinking 'consciousness is profound' that one trains in yoga upon consciousness.

Śāriputra, one does not train in yoga thinking 'form is profound.' Śāriputra, it is because one does not train in yoga thinking 'form is profound' that one does not abide thinking 'form is profound.' Likewise with feeling, perception, and formations. Śāriputra, one does not train in yoga thinking 'consciousness is profound,' and it is because one does not train in yoga thinking 'consciousness is profound' that one does not abide thinking 'consciousness is profound.'"

When the Blessed One had spoken these words, the venerable Śāriputra addressed him thus: "Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom is profound. It is fitting to expound it in the presence of those great bodhisattvas, bodhisattvas of great being, who have received a prophecy of irreversibility. Why so? Blessed One, such a one will not be despondent, will not become doubtful, will not become confused, and will not fall into dispute."

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, said to the venerable Śāriputra: "Noble Śāriputra, if this Perfection of Wisdom were expounded before a great bodhisattva, a bodhisattva of great being, who has not yet received a prophecy, what fault could arise?"

At that, the venerable Śāriputra said to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Kauśika, whatever great bodhisattva, bodhisattva of great being, who has not received a prophecy, comes to see, pay homage to, honor, and hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom, and upon hearing it neither shrinks back, nor is altogether frightened, nor comes to be possessed of fright — know, Kauśika, that such a great bodhisattva has come from far away. Know that he has long entered the vehicle, that his roots of virtue have fully ripened. Before long, without obstruction, he will receive a prophecy of unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. Know that he is drawing near to that prophecy.

That great bodhisattva will not pass beyond one, two, or three Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and completely awakened buddhas before receiving a prophecy of unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. And yet he will please those Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and completely awakened buddhas, and having pleased them, he will not cause them to withdraw their affection. The very sight of a Tathāgata, with the aid of a prophecy, will be fruitful for him, and from the sight of a Tathāgata he will receive a prophecy of unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. From the time he receives a prophecy of unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening until the very moment of his fully perfect awakening to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete buddhahood, every act of seeing, paying homage to, honoring, and serving Tathāgatas will be fruitful for him."

Then the venerable Śāriputra addressed the Blessed One: "Blessed One, that great bodhisattva, that bodhisattva of great being, who comes to see, pay homage to, honor, and hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom —

Blessed One, I would venture to say they have come from far away and have long since entered the vehicle with their roots of virtue fully ripened. As for those who, having heard this, will take it up, retain it, read it, master it, recite it, teach it, explain it in detail, transmit it by scriptural authority, and chant it — what need is there even to speak of them?"

Then the Blessed One spoke these words to the venerable Śāriputra: "Śāriputra, it is indeed so, it is indeed so. Śāriputra, that great bodhisattva, that bodhisattva of great being, who comes to see, pay homage to, honor, and hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom — know, Śāriputra, that such a bodhisattva of great being has come from far away and has long since entered the vehicle with their roots of virtue fully ripened. And as for those who, having heard this, will take it up, retain it, read it, master it, recite it, teach it, explain it in detail, transmit it by scriptural authority, and chant it — what need is there even to speak of them?"

Then the venerable Śāriputra addressed the Blessed One: "Blessed One, I am emboldened to offer an analogy. Sugata, I am emboldened. Blessed One, it is like this: suppose a son or daughter of good family, one who belongs to the bodhisattva vehicle, were to dream of sitting at the seat of awakening. Blessed One, that great bodhisattva, that bodhisattva of great being, is close to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening, on the verge of fully and perfectly awakening to it. Blessed One, in the same way, any son or daughter of good family who comes to see, pay homage to, honor, and hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom — Blessed One, that person who belongs to the bodhisattva vehicle has come from far away and has long since entered the vehicle with their roots of virtue fully ripened. And as for those who, having heard it, will take it up, retain it, read it, master it, recite it, teach it, explain it in detail, transmit it by scriptural authority, and chant it — what need is there even to speak of them? That person of the bodhisattva vehicle is prophesied and is close to awakening, and know that the Blessed One, the buddha, will give that great bodhisattva a prophecy of fully and perfectly awakening to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. And know that even the great bodhisattva who merely happens to hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom has their roots of virtue long since fully ripened through having entered the vehicle. Blessed One, as for those sons or daughters of good family who will take up, retain, read, master, recite, teach, explain in detail, transmit by scriptural authority, and chant this profound Perfection of Wisdom — what need is there even to speak of them? For this reason, Blessed One, sentient beings who are impoverished in Dharma, who have accumulated karma concordant with that impoverishment, and who have not for the most part been free of it — their minds will, for the most part, become averse to and turn away from this profound Perfection of Wisdom. Sentient beings who have not accumulated roots of virtue will, for the most part, not enter into and not develop faith in this ultimate truth (pariniṣpanna).

Those sons and daughters of noble family who will enter into and develop faith in this ultimate truth — they are ones who have accumulated roots of virtue. Blessed One, know that they have accumulated roots of virtue to a very high degree.

Blessed One, consider this analogy: a person comes from a wilderness, a desolate region a hundred leagues distant, or two hundred, or three hundred, or four hundred, or five hundred, or even a thousand leagues away. Having come from there, he perceives whatever signs indicate that a village, a town, or a settlement is at hand — signs he had known before: a cowherd or a herdsman, a boundary marker, a pleasant park, a beautiful grove, or other such signs. And upon seeing those earlier signs, he thinks: "Since these familiar signs appear before me in this way, a village, a town, or a settlement must be drawing near." And at that he is relieved, and from then on no thought of hardship arises in his mind.

Blessed One, in the same way, whatever great bodhisattva (bodhisattva) abides close to this profound Perfection of Wisdom — know, Blessed One, that such a great bodhisattva is drawing near to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening, and know that such a great bodhisattva will before long receive a prophetic declaration of unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. Moreover, he will not be frightened and will not be terrified at the prospect of falling to the ground of the śrāvakas or the pratyekabuddhas. And why is that? Because he has seen these earlier signs — namely, the opportunity to view, to pay homage to, to venerate, and to hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom.

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke these words to the venerable Śāriputra: "Śāriputra, it is so, it is exactly so. Just as all that has been and will be said here is spoken by the power of the buddhas, so too you should be confident in this matter."

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, the venerable Śāriputra addressed the Blessed One again: "Blessed One, consider this analogy: suppose a person here wishes to see the great ocean. As he goes to view the great ocean, if at some point along the way he sees a tree, or a sign indicating a tree, or a mountain, or a sign indicating a mountain, he thinks: 'The great ocean is still far from here.' But if later he no longer sees trees or signs of trees, or mountains or signs of mountains, he thinks: 'The great ocean is now close at hand.' And why is that? Because the great ocean gradually becomes lower and lower, so that within the great ocean there are no trees, no signs of trees, no mountains, no signs of mountains. And even though the great ocean is not yet directly visible to his eyes, he thinks: 'I have drawn near; from here the great ocean is not far.'

"Blessed One, in the same way, when a great bodhisattva hears this profound Perfection of Wisdom, he should understand: 'Even though the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and completely awakened buddhas, have not yet given me a direct prophetic declaration, still I am drawing near and will receive a prophetic declaration of unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening.' And why is that?"

And this is so in order that they may behold, pay homage to, revere, and hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, consider this further example. When the time of spring draws near, the old leaves fall from the branches of the various trees, and diverse new leaves appear. When the leaves have appeared, the people of Jambudvīpa look upon those forests, see those signs as before, and think: 'Now flowers and fruits will appear before long.' And so they become glad. And why is that? Because such signs as these had previously appeared from the trunks of those trees.

Blessed One, in the same way, when a great bodhisattva draws near in order to behold, pay homage to, revere, and hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom, one should understand that for this great bodhisattva the roots of virtue have fully ripened in relation to this profound Perfection of Wisdom — for it is those very prior roots of virtue that have drawn this profound Perfection of Wisdom close to him.

As for those gods who have previously seen buddhas, they think to themselves: 'Since the signs of a prophetic declaration to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening previously appeared for those earlier great bodhisattvas, this great bodhisattva will before long receive a prophetic declaration to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening.' And thinking thus they become greatly joyful, elated, and filled with delight.

Blessed One, consider yet another example. When a pregnant woman, heavy with child, moves her body she becomes greatly uncomfortable; she has little desire to walk about; she desires only small amounts of food; she becomes drowsy and sleeps little; she speaks little; her strength diminishes; her sufferings increase; she remains mostly sighing and groaning; she has little desire for company; she relies upon, firmly relies upon, cultivates, and habitually engages in the perception that runs counter to her former ways — and she thinks: 'I am experiencing such sensations in my body.' At that time, Blessed One, upon seeing such signs as these, one should understand that this woman will before long give birth to a child.

Blessed One, in the same way, when a great bodhisattva whose roots of virtue have fully ripened draws near in order to behold, pay homage to, revere, and hear this profound Perfection of Wisdom — and upon hearing it delights in it and arouses a mind that seeks the Perfection of Wisdom — at that time, Blessed One, one should understand that this great bodhisattva will before long receive a prophetic declaration to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening.

When these words were spoken, the Blessed One addressed the venerable Śāriputra and said: "Excellent! Excellent! Śāriputra, you have been emboldened here by the power of the buddhas."

Then the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, it is wonderful that the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and completely awakened buddhas, hold these great bodhisattvas well in their care, train them well, and entrust them well."

The Blessed One replied: "Subhūti, it is so. Those great bodhisattvas have entered upon this for the benefit of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the welfare, benefit, and happiness of the great multitude of beings.

out of compassion for gods and humans, wishing to fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and having fully and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, wishing to teach the supreme Dharma.

Then Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, when a great bodhisattva is engaged in this Perfection of Wisdom, how does the practice of the Perfection of Wisdom become perfectly complete?"

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, if a great bodhisattva engaged in the Perfection of Wisdom does not truly perceive the increase of form, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise, if one does not truly perceive what is not feeling, what is not perception, what is not formations, and the increase of consciousness, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. If one does not truly perceive the decrease of form, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise, if one does not truly perceive what is not feeling, what is not perception, what is not formations, and the decrease of consciousness, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. If one does not truly perceive phenomena, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. If one does not truly perceive what are not phenomena, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. In this way the practice of the Perfection of Wisdom becomes perfectly complete.

Subhūti said: "Blessed One, this teaching is inconceivable."

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, form is inconceivable. Likewise, feeling, perception, and formations are inconceivable, and Subhūti, consciousness is inconceivable. Subhūti, if one does not even know that form is said to be inconceivable, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. Likewise, if one does not even know that feeling, perception, and formations are inconceivable, and Subhūti, that consciousness is said to be inconceivable, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom."

Then the venerable Śāriputra addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, who would have faith in this Perfection of Wisdom, so profound as it is?"

The Blessed One declared: "Śāriputra, whatever great bodhisattva has engaged in the Perfection of Wisdom will have faith in this Perfection of Wisdom."

The venerable Śāriputra asked: "Blessed One, how does a great bodhisattva come to have engaged in it? How does one come to bear the name 'one who has engaged in it'?"

The Blessed One declared: "Śāriputra, in this regard a great bodhisattva does not conceptualize with respect to the powers, does not conceptualize with respect to the fearlessnesses, does not conceptualize with respect to the buddha qualities, and does not conceptualize even with respect to omniscience. And why is that? Śāriputra, the powers are inconceivable, the fearlessnesses are also inconceivable, the buddha qualities are also inconceivable, omniscience is also inconceivable, and all phenomena are also inconceivable. Śāriputra, when a great bodhisattva who has thus engaged in practice does not engage with anything whatsoever, that is practicing the Perfection of Wisdom. Such a one is called 'one who has engaged' and comes to bear the name 'one who has engaged.'"

Then the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words:

"Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom is profound. Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom is a mass of precious jewels. Blessed One, for the sake of the purity of space, the Perfection of Wisdom is a mass of purity. Blessed One, it is a wonder that many obstacles should arise for those sons and daughters of good family who hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom."

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One declared to the venerable Subhūti:

"So it is, Subhūti, so it is. Subhūti, for those sons or daughters of good family who hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom, many obstacles will indeed arise. And why is that? Subhūti, it is because the sinful māras strive to create obstacles for those sons and daughters of good family who hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom. Therefore one should copy it out quickly. Even if it takes a month, or two months, or three months to copy, it should still be copied. And if it takes a full year or longer to copy, then, Subhūti, those sons or daughters of good family should still copy this Perfection of Wisdom. And why is that? Subhūti, it is because wherever there is a great precious jewel, many obstacles arise."

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words:

"Blessed One, the sinful māras will rouse many kinds of eagerness and make effort to carry out obstructing deeds against those who hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom."

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, although the sinful māras will indeed strive to carry out obstructing deeds against those who hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom in this way, nevertheless they are not able to create obstacles for a bodhisattva, a great being, who has not broken his commitment."

Then the venerable Śāriputra addressed the Blessed One with these words:

"Blessed One, when māras delight in creating obstacles against those sons and daughters of good family who hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom, how is it, Blessed One, that sons and daughters of good family nowadays come to hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom?"

"Blessed One, through whose power will those sons and daughters of good family come to hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom?"

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke these words to the venerable Śāriputra:

"Śāriputra, those sons and daughters of good family will come to hold, retain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom, and likewise to learn in accordance with suchness, to practice in accordance with suchness, and to engage in yoga in accordance with suchness — all through the power of the blessed buddhas, the Tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly and fully awakened ones. And why is that? Śāriputra, it is the nature of reality of all phenomena that all those blessed buddhas who dwell, live, and abide in world-systems beyond measure and beyond counting will direct their attention toward and fully embrace this Perfection of Wisdom — toward holding it, retaining it, reading it, mastering it, reciting it aloud, teaching it, explaining it in detail, chanting it by heart, copying it out in writing. And those sons and daughters of good family who come to hold, explain, read, master, recite aloud, teach, explain in detail, chant by heart, copy out in writing, and study this Perfection of Wisdom, and who learn in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness — those blessed buddhas will likewise direct their attention toward them and fully embrace them. Śāriputra, no obstacle can be created against those sons and daughters of good family who are thus attended to and fully embraced by the buddhas."

When this was said, the venerable Śāriputra addressed the Blessed One with these words:

"Blessed One, all those bodhisattvas, great beings, who listen to this Perfection of Wisdom, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, chant it by heart, copy it out in writing, and study it, and who learn in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness — all of them, Blessed One, do so through the power of the buddhas, the blessing of the buddhas, and by being fully embraced by the buddhas. It is by that means that they will come to listen to this Perfection of Wisdom, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, chant it by heart, copy it out in writing, and study it, and to learn in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness."

When this was said, the Blessed One spoke these words to the venerable Śāriputra:

"Śāriputra, it is so, it is indeed so.

"Śāriputra, all those great bodhisattvas (bodhisattvas), through the power of the buddhas, the blessing of the buddhas, and by being fully embraced by the buddhas, will come to listen to this profound Perfection of Wisdom, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, chant it by heart, copy it out in writing, learn in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness.

Śāriputra, those great bodhisattvas who listen to this Perfection of Wisdom, hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, chant it by heart, copy it out in writing, and learn in accordance with suchness, practice in accordance with suchness, and engage in yoga in accordance with suchness — all of them are known by the Tathāgata. Śāriputra, all of them are blessed by the Tathāgata. Śāriputra, all of them are seen by the Tathāgata. Śāriputra, all of them are fully beheld by the Tathāgata with the eye of a buddha.

And having listened, those who have held it, retained it, read it, mastered it, recited it aloud, taught it, explained it in detail, chanted it by heart, copied it out in writing, and then learned in accordance with suchness, practiced in accordance with suchness, and engaged in yoga in accordance with suchness — they draw near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and they abide in suchness and in unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Śāriputra, as for those who copy out this Perfection of Wisdom, retain it, read it, master it, recite it aloud, teach it, explain it in detail, chant it by heart, and copy it out in writing, yet do not learn in accordance with suchness, do not practice in accordance with suchness, and do not engage in yoga in accordance with suchness — precisely because they do not learn in accordance with suchness, do not practice in accordance with suchness, and do not engage in yoga in accordance with suchness, they will not abide in suchness and in unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. Nevertheless, Śāriputra, even they are known by the Tathāgata; even they are blessed by the Tathāgata; even they are seen by the Tathāgata; and, Śāriputra, even they are fully beheld by the Tathāgata with the eye of a buddha.

Śāriputra, their effort and their diligent striving will be endowed with great purpose, great benefit, great fruit, and great karmic maturation. And why is this? Because the Perfection of Wisdom is endowed with the ultimate meaning of all phenomena, and it abides in order that all sentient beings may be made to comprehend things exactly as they are.

Moreover, Śāriputra, these sūtras endowed with the six perfections — even after the Tathāgata has passed away, at the time of the decline of the sacred Dharma, when the core of the Dharma and the Vinaya is obtained, they will arise in the south. From the south they will arise in the middle region. From the middle region they will arise in the north."

"Śāriputra, at that time, those sons of good family and daughters of good family who uphold this Perfection of Wisdom, who bear it, read it, master it thoroughly, proclaim it, teach it, explain it in detail, recite it by scriptural transmission, and chant it aloud — and who, even if only by having it put into a volume, carry it — upon these the Tathāgata looks with intention. These the Tathāgata knows. Śāriputra, these the Tathāgata has blessed. Śāriputra, these the Tathāgata watches over. Śāriputra, these the Tathāgata beholds with the eye of a buddha."

Śāriputra then asked: "Blessed One, will this profound Perfection of Wisdom spread and flourish in the northern lands and the northern regions in the future, in the last age, in the last period?"

The Blessed One declared: "Śāriputra, in the northern lands and the northern regions, those who listen to this profound Perfection of Wisdom and then engage in yoga upon this profound Perfection of Wisdom will become many and flourish.

"Śāriputra, those bodhisattvas, great beings, who listen to this profound Perfection of Wisdom, inquire into it, uphold it, bear it, read it, master it thoroughly, proclaim it, teach it, explain it in detail, recite it by scriptural transmission, chant it aloud, train in suchness, accomplish it in suchness, and engage in yoga upon it in suchness — these should be understood as having entered the vehicle genuinely and for a long time."

Śāriputra asked: "Blessed One, of those bodhisattvas, great beings, in the northern lands and the northern regions who listen to this profound Perfection of Wisdom, inquire into it, uphold it, bear it, read it, master it thoroughly, proclaim it, teach it, explain it in detail, recite it by scriptural transmission, chant it aloud, train in suchness, accomplish it in suchness, and engage in yoga upon it in suchness — how many will there be, few or many?"

The Blessed One declared: "Śāriputra, in the northern lands and the northern regions, bodhisattvas, great beings, will arise in great number, very great number indeed. Śāriputra, although they will be many, among all those many, the bodhisattvas, great beings, who listen to this Perfection of Wisdom, inquire into it, uphold it, bear it, read it, master it thoroughly, proclaim it, teach it, explain it in detail, recite it by scriptural transmission, chant it aloud, train in suchness, accomplish it in suchness, engage in yoga upon it in suchness, and who, when this Perfection of Wisdom is being taught, do not become dejected, do not shrink, do not feel shame, are not embarrassed, do not turn their minds away, are not struck with wonder, do not become afraid, and are not overcome with fear — these will be very few indeed. Such bodhisattvas, great beings, should be understood as having entered the vehicle genuinely and for a long time."

They follow in the wake of the former Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas, having fully questioned them, fully requested instruction from them, and fully inquired of them. Those sons and daughters of good family who belong to the bodhisattva vehicle thereby make offering to the former Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas. They will come to perfect their moral discipline completely. Starting from this very unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening, they will work for the benefit of the many.

Why is that? It is because I myself have spoken words connected with omniscience to those sons and daughters of good family. Even as they pass from life to life, they will engage rightly in this very omniscience and in this Perfection of Wisdom. Starting from unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening, they will speak of that, and they will rejoice in speaking of that. They will dwell well and enter into meditative equipoise upon this Perfection of Wisdom, and even Māra cannot separate them from it — how much less can other sentient beings do so through intent or through the power of mantra! Why is that? It is because they are firmly rooted in unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening. When sons and daughters of good family hear this, they will attain great joy, clarity, and profound gladness toward this Perfection of Wisdom. They will plant the roots of virtue of many people with respect to unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening.

Why is that? It is because those sons and daughters of good family have spoken these very words in my presence: "While engaged in the conduct of a bodhisattva, I shall correctly establish, correctly instruct, correctly set forth, correctly praise, correctly gladden, correctly differentiate for, cause to abide in perfect awakening, and cause to become irreversible — many hundreds, many thousands, many hundreds of thousands, many tens of millions, many billions, many hundreds of billions, many tens of billions of times hundreds of thousands of living beings."

Why is that? Regarding those sons and daughters of good family who, while practicing for the sake of awakening, speak these words — "I shall correctly establish, correctly instruct, correctly set forth, correctly praise, correctly gladden, correctly differentiate for, cause to abide in perfect awakening, and cause to become irreversible — many hundreds, many thousands, many hundreds of thousands, many tens of millions, many billions, many hundreds of billions, many tens of billions of times hundreds of thousands of living beings" — having looked upon the minds of those sons and daughters of good family who belong to the bodhisattva vehicle with my own mind, I have rejoiced on their behalf. In this way, those sons and daughters of good family are endowed with vast aspiration. When the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas who are present teach the Dharma,

those who will hear this Perfection of Wisdom in its full extent from the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas will form the intention to travel to other and yet other buddha-fields. In those buddha-fields as well, they will establish many hundreds of living beings, many thousands of living beings, many hundreds of thousands of living beings, many tens of millions of living beings, many billions of living beings, many hundreds of billions of living beings, many tens of billions of times hundreds of thousands of living beings in unsurpassable, perfect, and complete awakening — teaching them correctly, setting them on the path, praising them, generating joy in them, revealing the way, and causing them to be awakened and irreversible.

When this was declared, the venerable Śāriputra spoke thus to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, there is nothing whatsoever among the phenomena of past, future, or present that the Tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly and fully awakened buddhas have not seen, not heard, not directly known, or not fully understood. There is no phenomenon they do not fully understand, and no conduct of sentient beings they do not fully understand. Blessed One, it is therefore wonderful that at that very time the Tathāgatas know the conduct of those bodhisattvas, great beings who aspire to awakening, who are endowed with supreme intention, and who have applied diligent effort — those sons and daughters of good family who seek, thoroughly seek, and are on the verge of seeking this profound Perfection of Wisdom for the sake of all sentient beings and for the sake of these six perfections. Among those who seek, thoroughly seek, and are on the verge of seeking, some bodhisattvas will find this profound Perfection of Wisdom when they seek it; some will not find it even when they seek it; and some will find it without even seeking it. Blessed One, what is the cause of this?" So he asked, and the Blessed One spoke thus to the venerable Śāriputra:

"Śāriputra, it is exactly so, it is exactly so. There is nothing whatsoever among the phenomena of past, future, or present that a Tathāgata has not seen, not heard, not known, or not fully understood. Śāriputra, at that very time and in that very moment, some bodhisattvas search for this Perfection of Wisdom, seek it out, and search for it thoroughly, yet do not find it; while some bodhisattvas, though they have not searched, not sought it out, and not searched thoroughly, will find it. Why is this? It is because those bodhisattvas searched and sought out this Perfection of Wisdom in former times without ever abandoning their diligent effort, and thus through the very aspiration rooted in that former virtuous root, they will find this Perfection of Wisdom even without searching, seeking out, or searching for it thoroughly."

Moreover, those other sūtras that proclaim this very Perfection of Wisdom will themselves come close to those bodhisattvas, draw near to them, and approach them. Why is this? Śāriputra, it is as follows: a bodhisattva, a great being, who searches and thoroughly searches for this Perfection of Wisdom without ever abandoning diligent effort — that one, across transmigrations and through successive lives, will find this Perfection of Wisdom; and moreover, those other sūtras endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom will themselves come close to that person, draw near to them, and approach them.

When this had been declared, the venerable Śāriputra addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, is it only these sūtras endowed with the six perfections that will come close to, draw near to, and approach those sons and daughters of good family — or will others do so as well?"

The Blessed One declared: "Śāriputra, however many other profound and deep sūtras there may be, all of them will likewise come close to, draw near to, and approach those sons and daughters of good family. Why is this? Śāriputra, it is as follows: those bodhisattvas, those great beings, who establish sentient beings in unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, who teach it correctly, who set beings in it rightly, who praise them aright, who cause them to rejoice completely, who clearly expound it and cause beings to abide in perfect complete awakening, who make them irreversible — and who also train themselves in it — Śāriputra, even across subsequent rebirths, the profound and deep sūtras endowed with non-referential cognition (Skt. anupalambha), endowed with emptiness, and endowed with the six perfections will themselves come close to those bodhisattvas, draw near to them, and approach them."

This was the tenth chapter of the noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines, entitled "The Full Enumeration of the Qualities of Holding."

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines. The eleventh fascicle.

Then the venerable Subhūti addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, when the Blessed One fully enumerates these qualities of those sons and daughters of good family, how many obstructing acts of Māra will arise against them?"

When this was asked, the Blessed One declared to the venerable Subhūti: "Subhūti, many works of Māra that create obstacles will arise."

Subhūti asked: "Blessed One, what kinds of numerous works of Māra that create obstacles will arise?"

The Blessed One declared: "Subhūti, after a long time has passed, eloquence will arise in those bodhisattvas, those great beings, who are teaching the Perfection of Wisdom. Subhūti, this first arising of eloquence should be recognized as a work of Māra. And even when that eloquence has arisen, it will turn into distraction of mind — this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if during that time there is yawning, restlessness, fidgeting, and resorting to writing things down — this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if the mind is pervaded by agitation and scattering — this too should be recognized as a work of Māra."

Subhūti, if they write things down while their consciousnesses are at cross-purposes with one another, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if mindfulness is not attained, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if they laugh at one another and then write things down, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if they chatter with one another and then write things down, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if the eyes wander while they are writing things down, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if those who are writing things down fall into discord and disagreement with one another, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if someone rises from their seat and departs, thinking, "I find no depth in this, I taste no flavor in it," this too should be recognized as a work of Māra. Subhūti, if someone, with a faithless mind, rises from their seat and departs, thinking, "I have received no scriptural transmission from this Perfection of Wisdom," this too should be recognized as a work of Māra.

Subhūti, if someone thinks, "Let no mention be made here of the name of the village, city, or town where I was born; let no mention be made here of my own name and clan; let no mention be made of my father's and mother's names and clan; let no mention be made of the clan into which I was born" — and with that thought refuses to listen to the Perfection of Wisdom and intends to leave — then however many such beings have thus generated the awakening mind, in whatever ways they come to depart, they will grasp at cyclic existence for just that many eons, however long it takes them to turn back and take up the practice of yoga. Why is that? Subhūti, those bodhisattvas, those great beings, who do not listen to this Perfection of Wisdom will not attain definitive emergence from worldly and supramundane phenomena. Subhūti, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra for them.

Subhūti, furthermore, persons who belong to the bodhisattva vehicle, who abandon and discard this Perfection of Wisdom that accomplishes the gnosis of omniscience, and who intend instead to seek out sūtras that do not accomplish the gnosis of omniscience — this too should be recognized as a work of Māra for them.

Subhūti, just as someone who does not wish to train in worldly and supramundane phenomena and does not wish to attain definitive emergence from worldly and supramundane phenomena will not train in this Perfection of Wisdom — and not having trained in this Perfection of Wisdom will not attain definitive emergence from worldly and supramundane phenomena — so it is that those persons of small understanding, abandoning and discarding the Perfection of Wisdom, which is the very root of fully knowing all worldly and supramundane phenomena just as they are, intend instead to cling to the branches.

Subhūti, consider this analogy: a dog that, in terms of its very nature, has been given up and set aside from a meal that has been set out, and seeks morsels of food from a laboring man. In just the same way, Subhūti, in the future time to come, certain persons who belong to the bodhisattva vehicle, having abandoned this Perfection of Wisdom, which is the root of the gnosis of omniscience, will intend to seek the essence of buddhahood from what are merely branches, leaves, and petals — the vehicles of the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas. Subhūti, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra for those bodhisattvas. Why is that? Because those persons of small understanding do not know that this Perfection of Wisdom is what accomplishes the gnosis of omniscience.

Having abandoned and cast aside this Perfection of Wisdom, they will intend to devote themselves wholeheartedly to other sūtras — those that explicitly teach the ground of the śrāvakas and those that explicitly teach the ground of the pratyekabuddhas. Bodhisattvas of such a disposition should be recognized as ones who have entered upon what are merely branches, leaves, and petals. Why is that? Subhūti, a great bodhisattva (mahāsattva) should not train in the way that persons of the śrāvaka vehicle and persons of the pratyekabuddha vehicle train. Subhūti, how do persons of the śrāvaka vehicle and persons of the pratyekabuddha vehicle train? Subhūti, they think to themselves: "I alone shall be tamed; I alone shall be pacified; I alone shall be led to complete nirvāṇa." And for the sake of taming, pacifying, and leading themselves alone to complete nirvāṇa, they set out in the endeavor of accumulating all wholesome roots. Subhūti, a great bodhisattva should not train in that way. Rather, Subhūti, a great bodhisattva should train as follows: for the benefit of the entire world, one should abide in suchness oneself and establish all sentient beings in suchness as well, leading the immeasurable realm of sentient beings to complete nirvāṇa. A great bodhisattva should set out in the endeavor of accumulating all wholesome roots in that way, and yet should not be conceited about it.

Subhūti, consider this analogy: a certain person who has never seen an elephant searches for the color and shape of an elephant. He finds an elephant in a dark chamber and examines it there in that dim light. Having examined it there, he then thinks of searching for the elephant's tracks and, through those tracks, of grasping the elephant's color and shape. Subhūti, what do you think — is that person to be recognized as one of wise character? Subhūti replied: "Blessed One, he is not." The Blessed One declared: "In just the same way, Subhūti, a person belonging to the bodhisattva vehicle who, not knowing and not inquiring into this Perfection of Wisdom, sets it aside, yet desires to fully and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, complete, perfect awakening, and intends to search through sūtras that explicitly teach the ground of the śrāvakas and those that explicitly teach the ground of the pratyekabuddhas — such a one should be recognized as being of such a disposition. Subhūti, this too should be recognized as a work of Māra for those people."
