Furthermore, Subhūti, even within the dream of a bodhisattva great being, he does not become agitated, does not become frightened in any way, and is not overcome by terror: even if there occurs harm to a village, harm to a city, harm to a town, harm to a province, harm to a country, or the harm of a wildfire breaking loose, or he sees a predator, a wild beast, or other small species of wild creatures, or is confronted with beheading close at hand, or any other great, unbearable terror, suffering, and mental distress whatsoever arises, and even upon seeing the suffering, mental distress, and great unbearable terror of other sentient beings, no unbearable terror arises in him; he does not become agitated, does not become frightened in any way, and is not overcome by terror. Then, having awoken from that sleep, immediately upon rising he thinks thus: "All these three realms are just like a dream. When I have become fully and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, I shall teach the Dharma to all just as it is, teaching it truly." Subhūti, this too should be recognized as a defining characteristic of the irreversibility of an irreversible bodhisattva great being.

Furthermore, Subhūti, when, even within the dream of an irreversible bodhisattva great being, he sees the beings of the hells, he thinks thus: "Just as, in the buddha-field where I have become fully and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, all the lower realms of every kind will, in every way and from every direction, entirely fail to arise — so shall it be." Subhūti, this too should be recognized as a defining characteristic of this irreversible bodhisattva great being's purification of the lower realms.









Subhūti, in this regard, other bodhisattva mahāsattvas should recognize: "Alas, this bodhisattva mahāsattva has been empowered by Māra." Why is that? Because whatever aspects, signs, and marks belong to irreversible bodhisattva mahāsattvas are absent in him.

Furthermore, Subhūti, through such an empowerment that bodhisattva mahāsattva will generate manifest conceit. Having generated manifest conceit, overpowered by pride and puffed up with arrogance, overpowered by conceit, overpowered by Māra's empowerment, he will come to despise other bodhisattva mahāsattvas, will treat them scornfully, will deride them, will mock them, and will revile them. Subhūti, this too should be recognized as Māra's work, done through Māra's empowerment of that bodhisattva mahāsattva.

Subhūti, further still, one should recognize Māra's work through the showing of a name and the giving of a prophecy of a name to the bodhisattva mahāsattva. Subhūti, how should one recognize Māra's work through the showing of a name and the giving of a prophecy of a name to the bodhisattva mahāsattva? Subhūti, here the evil Māra comes in the guise of a monk and gives this prophecy to the bodhisattva mahāsattva: "Whatever mind you generated, thinking, 'Oh, may my name be thus-and-such when I have become fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening,' following after that name, examining and analyzing it with your mind—accordingly, when you become fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, your name will indeed be thus-and-such." In this way he gives him a prophecy of that very name.

Then the bodhisattva whose wisdom is corrupted and who is unskilled in means thinks to himself: "This monk has shown me exactly what I myself followed after with the name, thinking, 'Oh, may my name be thus-and-such when I have become fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening,' and examined and analyzed accordingly." He then considers: "The name that I myself contemplated and followed after, and the name spoken either by that evil Māra himself, or by those of Māra's faction who manifested through magical emanation, or by the monk empowered by Māra—comparing these two, however my generation of mind arose, and however this monk has shown my name, the two names match." Having reasoned thus, he comes to think: "I have already been prophesied by name to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening by those past tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas."

Subhūti, whatever aspects, signs, and marks of irreversible bodhisattva mahāsattvas I have taught are absent in him, and he has become separated from them. Yet through that showing of a name and giving of a prophecy of a name, he generates conceit...



Subhūti, I do not say that the various dwelling places such as the wilderness, the outskirts of villages, uninhabited regions, forest thickets, mountain caves, charnel grounds, heaps of straw, and so forth constitute the solitude of the bodhisattva great being.

Subhūti said: Blessed One, if that is so, then apart from dwelling in the various dwelling places such as the wilderness, the outskirts of villages, uninhabited regions, forest thickets, mountain caves, charnel grounds, and heaps of straw, what other solitude is there for the bodhisattva great being? Blessed One, what else is the solitude of the bodhisattva great being like?

Having said this, the Blessed One spoke thus to venerable Subhūti:

Subhūti, if the bodhisattva great being becomes secluded from mental engagements associated with the śrāvakas, and becomes secluded from mental engagements associated with the pratyekabuddhas, then in that way the bodhisattva great being dwells in solitude. Even while dwelling within a town, he dwells by means of the perfection of wisdom, skillful means, loving-kindness toward all sentient beings, and great compassion. Dwelling with this manner of dwelling is itself dwelling in solitude.

Subhūti, moreover I grant this solitude to the bodhisattva great being: seclusion from mental engagements associated with the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas. If the bodhisattva great being passes day and night dwelling with this solitude, then he dwells in solitude.

If the bodhisattva great being, while dwelling in dwelling places such as the wilderness, forest thickets, mountain caves, charnel grounds, and the outskirts of villages, dwells with this solitude just described, then the bodhisattva great being dwells in solitude.

Subhūti, as for the solitude that the evil Māra has taught—the solitude of dwelling in dwelling places such as the wilderness





Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom itself should be recognized as the spiritual friend of the bodhisattva, the great being. Subhūti, all six of the perfections should be recognized as the spiritual friend of the bodhisattva, the great being. The six perfections themselves are the Teacher. The six perfections are the path. The six perfections are illumination. The six perfections are the lamp. The six perfections are the light. The six perfections are the protector. The six perfections are the refuge. The six perfections are the resting place. The six perfections are the ally. The six perfections are the island. The six perfections are the mother. The six perfections are the father. The six perfections are that which brings about knowledge, realization, and abiding for the sake of unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Why is this so? Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom depends upon these, namely, upon the six perfections. Subhūti, whatever Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas there were in the past who, having manifestly and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, passed into complete nirvāṇa — the omniscience of all those Blessed Buddhas also arose from precisely these six perfections. Subhūti, whatever Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas will arise in future times, who will manifestly and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — the omniscience of all those Blessed Buddhas will also arise from precisely these six perfections. Subhūti, in immeasurable, innumerable, incalculable, inconceivable world systems, whatever Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas there are who, having thus manifestly and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, are dwelling, living, and teaching the Dharma — the omniscience of all those Blessed Buddhas also arose from precisely these six perfections. Subhūti, at present too, I myself, the Tathāgata, Arhat, perfectly complete Buddha, having manifestly and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening — Subhūti, my own omniscience likewise arose from precisely these six perfections.

Why is this so? Subhūti, within these six perfections are gathered the thirty-seven factors concordant with awakening, the four immeasurable abodes of Brahmā, the four means of gathering [disciples], and however many qualities of a buddha there are — the wisdom of a buddha, the wisdom of self-arising, the inconceivable wisdom, the immeasurable wisdom, the incalculable wisdom, the unequaled wisdom, the wisdom equal to the unequaled, and the wisdom of omniscience — all of these are gathered within the six perfections.

Subhūti, therefore these six perfections themselves are the spiritual friend of the bodhisattva, the great being. These six perfections themselves are the Teacher. The six perfections are the path. The six perfections are illumination. The six perfections are the lamp. The six perfections are the light. The six perfections are the protector. The six perfections are the refuge. The six perfections are the resting place. The six perfections are the ally.



Blessed One, if isolation or emptiness does not fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and if, apart from the Blessed One's isolation and emptiness, there is no other phenomenon whatsoever that fully and completely becomes a buddha, or fully and completely awakens, or by which one fully and completely awakens that can be observed, then, Blessed One, how am I to understand the meaning of what has been explained? Blessed One, please teach this. Sugata, please teach this.

When this had been said, the Blessed One spoke thus to venerable Subhūti: Subhūti, what do you think? Have sentient beings, from time without beginning, engaged in grasping at "I" and grasping at "mine"?

Subhūti said: Blessed One, it is so. Sugata, it is so. Sentient beings, from time without beginning, engage in grasping at "I" and grasping at "mine."

The Blessed One said: Subhūti, what do you think? Are the grasping at "I" and the grasping at "mine" empty?

Subhūti said: Blessed One, they are empty. Sugata, they are empty.

The Blessed One said: Subhūti, what do you think? Do sentient beings wander in saṃsāra through grasping at "I" and grasping at "mine"?

Subhūti said: Blessed One, it is so. Sugata, it is so. Sentient beings wander in saṃsāra through grasping at "I" and grasping at "mine."

The Blessed One said: Subhūti, in this way it is fully known that sentient beings become thoroughly afflicted. Just as sentient beings grasp and become attached, so they become thoroughly afflicted; yet in this there is no one whatsoever who becomes thoroughly afflicted. Subhūti, just as there is no grasping and no attachment, so grasping at "I" and grasping at "mine" are not fully known to exist. Subhūti, in this way it is fully known that sentient beings become purified. Just as there is no grasping at and no attachment to sentient beings, so they become purified; yet in this there is no one whatsoever who becomes purified.

Subhūti, when a bodhisattva, a great being, practices in this way, he practices the Perfection of Wisdom. Subhūti, in this way, within the isolation of all phenomena and the emptiness of all phenomena, it is fully known that sentient beings become thoroughly afflicted and become purified.

Subhūti said: Blessed One, it is truly wondrous that, within the isolation of all phenomena and the emptiness of all phenomena in this way, the affliction and purification of sentient beings are also fully known. Blessed One, when a bodhisattva, a great being, practices in this way, he practices the Perfection of Wisdom. Blessed One, when a bodhisattva, a great being, practices in this way, he does not practice with respect to form; he does not practice with respect to feeling, perception, formations, or consciousness. Blessed One, when a bodhisattva, a great being, practices in this way, he becomes unshakable by the world together with its gods, humans, and asuras. Blessed One, when a bodhisattva, a great being, practices in this way, he surpasses the conduct of all persons belonging to the vehicle of the śrāvakas and all persons belonging to the vehicle of the pratyekabuddhas, and he attains the state that cannot itself be surpassed. Why is this? Blessed One, because buddhahood itself, tathāgatahood itself, self-arisen wisdom itself, and omniscience itself cannot be surpassed.







Subhoti, if, when it is explained in this way, the bodhisattva great being is not dismayed, is not frightened at all, does not become terrified at all, and does not despair, then, Subhoti, that bodhisattva great being should be known to be practicing this Perfection of Wisdom.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but does the Perfection of Wisdom practice the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, it does not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but does that emptiness which is the emptiness of the Perfection of Wisdom practice the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, it does not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but apart from the emptiness of the Perfection of Wisdom, is there any phenomenon at all that is observed as practicing the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, there is not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but does emptiness practice the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, it does not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but is any phenomenon that practices the Perfection of Wisdom observed within this emptiness?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, it is not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but does emptiness practice emptiness?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, it does not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but does form practice the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, it does not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but do feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness practice the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, they do not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but apart from form, is there any phenomenon at all that is observed as practicing the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, there is not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, but apart from feeling, perception, and formations, and apart from consciousness, is there any phenomenon at all that is observed as practicing the Perfection of Wisdom?

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, there is not.

Subhoti said: Blessed One, then how does the bodhisattva great being come to practice the Perfection of Wisdom?

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One said this to the venerable Subhoti: Subhoti, do you yourself truly and directly see any phenomenon that practices the Perfection of Wisdom?

Subhoti said: Blessed One, I do not.

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, do you truly and directly see that Perfection of Wisdom in which the bodhisattva great being practices?

Subhoti said: Blessed One, I do not.

The Blessed One declared: Subhoti, what do you think? Do you truly and directly see any phenomenon whatsoever that cannot be observed? And is that very phenomenon born, or coming to be born, or arisen; or ceased, or coming to cease, or ceasing?

Subhoti said: Blessed One, I do not.

The Blessed One declared:



Kauśika, those sentient beings who will listen to this profound Perfection of Wisdom, and who, having heard it, will hold it, retain it, read it, master it, recite it fully, teach it, explain it, transm

Moreover, they will surpass and go beyond all those stream-enterers, once-returners, non-returners, arhats, and pratyekabuddhas. Those bodhisattva mahāsattvas do not merely surpass and go beyond stream-enterers, once-returners, non-returners, arhats, and pratyekabuddhas alone; they will also surpass and go beyond all those bodhisattva mahāsattvas who are great lords of generosity but who are devoid of the perfection of wisdom and skillful means.

Those bodhisattva mahāsattvas do not merely surpass and go beyond bodhisattva mahāsattvas who are great lords of generosity alone; they will also surpass and go beyond all those who are endowed with an aggregate of moral discipline that is unimpaired, faultless, unblemished, perfect, pure, and unadulterated, but who are devo



Furthermore, Ānanda, at the time when a bodhisattva great being is training in the perfection of wisdom, applying himself in yoga to the perfection of wisdom, and cultivating the perfection of wisdom, Ānanda, at that time all those evil Māras throughout the great trichiliocosm become filled with doubt, thinking thus: "Will this bodhisattva great being, without turning back midway, actualize the true limit on the level of a śrāvaka or a pratyekabuddha, or will he fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening?"

Ānanda, furthermore, at the time when a bodhisattva great being dwells, having taken his stand in the perfection of wisdom, at that time those evil Māras are pierced by the dart of sorrow.

Ānanda, furthermore, at the time when a bodhisattva great being practices the perfection of wisdom and applies himself in yoga to the perfection of wisdom, at that time those evil Māras cause harm to the bodhisattva great being. They think, "How can this bodhisattva great being be made to become discouraged and terrified, and how can even a single generation of his mind of unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening be exhausted?" And so they produce terrors, and they also cause and display things such as falling meteors and blazing fires in every direction.

Ānanda, regarding this, evil Māra does not intend to harm and come to every single bodhisattva great being; rather, he comes to some, and to others he does not come.

Ānanda asked: "Blessed One, to what kind of bodhisattva great being does evil Māra intend to come and cause harm?"

The Blessed One declared: "Ānanda, if a bodhisattva great being has failed, since the very beginning, to generate a mind of trust and belief when the perfection of wisdom is being taught, then, Ānanda, evil Māra intends to harm that bodhisattva great being and comes to him, and he will also find an opportunity.

"Ānanda, furthermore, if a bodhisattva, when this profound perfection of wisdom is being taught, becomes doubtful, wondering 'Is this the perfection of wisdom or is it not?' and a double mind arises, then, Ānanda, to that bodhisattva great being in whom such doubt has arisen, evil Māra also intends to cause harm and comes, and finds an opportunity there.

"Ānanda, furthermore, if in this regard a bodhisattva great being is separated from a spiritual friend and comes to be entirely seized by an evil companion, then he does not listen when this profound perfection of wisdom, this profound abode, is being taught; and because he does not listen, he does not understand; and because he does not understand, he does not ask, 'How, then, should the perfection of wisdom be cultivated?' Ānanda, to this bodhisattva great being also, evil Māra intends to cause harm and comes, and finds an opportunity there.

"Ānanda, furthermore, whatever bodhisattva great being comes to rely on engagement in upholding what is not the true Dharma, thinking, 'This is my companion, who in every matter will not abandon me. I have many other companions who are bodhisattva great beings, but they do not fulfill my intentions —"





"I will not speak harshly to another, saying 'you there,' nor will I answer and speak with rough words. In quarrels, disputes, and conflicts, I will act like one who is foolish, deaf, and mute. Even upon hearing another speak badly, say bad things, and utter bad words, I will not harbor ill will toward others. This coming to know the faults and defects of another is not good for me; it is not my proper concern. This thought, 'I will listen to the faults and defects of another,' is not my proper concern, I should think. Why is that? Because I should not disturb my superior intention. Having asked myself, 'After I have become fully and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, is it not my intention to bring happiness to all sentient beings through all the necessities of happiness and to bring them to complete liberation from suffering,' would it be that I have generated a mind of harm? And even when others and I are hostile toward one another, would it be a disturbance in me if I do not generate a mind of harm toward others? This is not something I should do. Rather, through firm fortitude, I will withstand whatever comes from others, and even toward one who creates an obstacle to my very life, I will not become disturbed, nor will a frown of anger arise on my face. This, Ānanda, I declare to be the definite emergence of that bodhisattva great being.

Ānanda, the bodhisattva great being should abide in this same way even in the presence of individuals belonging to the śrāvaka vehicle. Just as one does not become disturbed in the presence of any sentient being whatsoever, so should one abide in the presence of all sentient beings.

Ānanda, how should the bodhisattva great being abide in the presence of other individuals belonging to the bodhisattva vehicle? Ānanda, it is like this: just as one regards the Teacher, so should one abide, thinking, 'These bodhisattva great beings are my teachers.' 'These bodhisattva great beings have entered the same vehicle as I.' 'These bodhisattva great beings have entered the same path as I.' 'These bodhisattva great beings have an intention concordant with mine.' 'These bodhisattva great beings have genuinely entered a vehicle concordant with mine.' Whatever they should train in, I too should train in that. Just as they should train, so should I train in the same way. Even if some among them remain dwelling in a mixed manner, I should not dwell in a mixed manner. But if, through mental engagement endowed with omniscience, they dwell without mixing, then I too should train in that same way.

Ānanda, when the bodhisattva great being trains in omniscience in this way, there will be no obstacle to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening; he will swiftly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening.

This concludes, from the noble Eight Thousand Line Perfection of Wisdom, the chapter called 'Manifest Pride,' the twenty-fourth.

Then the venerable Subhūti said this to the Blessed One: 'Blessed One, in what should the bodhisattva great being train in order to train in omniscience?' The Blessed One declared: 'Subhūti, if the bodhisattva great being trains in exhaustion, he trains in omniscience.'







Subhūti, likewise, among the classes of sentient beings, those bodhisattva great beings who have entered this great path—namely, the path of the Perfection of Wisdom—thinking, "I shall become fully and perfectly awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening," are few. Among the classes of sentient beings, those who have entered the path of the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas are far more numerous.

Subhūti, further, it is like this: among the classes of sentient beings, those who have properly undertaken and abide in the karma that will cause them to become Śakra (Indra) himself are few. Among the classes of sentient beings, those who have properly undertaken and abide in the karma that will cause them to be born into the world of the gods are far more numerous.

Subhūti, likewise, among the classes of sentient beings, those bodhisattva great beings who train in the training of the Perfection of Wisdom are few. Subhūti, among the classes of sentient beings, those who train in the training of the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas are far more numerous.

Subhūti, further, it is like this: among the classes of sentient beings, those who have properly undertaken and abide in the karma that will cause them to become Brahmā himself are few. Among the classes of sentient beings, those who have properly undertaken and abide in the karma that will cause them to be born into Brahmā's retinue are far more numerous.

Subhūti, likewise, among the classes of sentient beings, those bodhisattva great beings who are irreversible from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening are few. Subhūti, among the classes of sentient beings, those bodhisattva great beings who are liable to turn back from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening are far more numerous.

Subhūti, therefore, among the classes of sentient beings, those sentient beings who have genuinely entered unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening are few. Subhūti, fewer still than those few are the sentient beings who accomplish suchness. Subhūti, fewer still than those very few who accomplish suchness are those who make the Perfection of Wisdom their yogic practice. Subhūti, fewer still than those few who make the Perfection of Wisdom their yogic practice are those bodhisattva great beings who are irreversible from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Subhūti, therefore, since one wishes to be counted among the number of those irreversible bodhisattva great beings—fewer even than the already few—one should train in and make a yogic practice of this very Perfection of Wisdom.

Subhūti, furthermore, when a bodhisattva great being trains thus in the Perfection of Wisdom, no mind of stinginess arises, no mind of doubt arises, no mind of envy arises, no mind of miserliness arises, no mind of degenerate moral discipline arises, no mind of malice arises, no mind of laziness arises, no mind of distraction arises, and no mind of corrupt wisdom arises.

Subhūti, in this way, when the bodhisattva great being trains in the Perfection of Wisdom, all the perfections come to be properly grasped, all the perfections come to be thoroughly grasped, all the perfections come to be supremely grasped, all the perfections come to be followed after, and all the perfections come to be gathered within. Subhūti, it is like this, for example—

Just as, within the view of the transitory collection, all sixty-two views come to be gathered, Subhūti, so too, when the bodhisattva great being trains in the Perfection of Wisdom, all the perfections come to be gathered within it. Subhūti, it is like this, for example: within the life faculty of a person, all the faculties come to be gathered. Subhūti, in just this way, when one trains in the Perfection of Wisdom, all the virtuous qualities of the bodhisattva great being come to be gathered within it. Subhūti, it is like this, for example: when a person's life faculty ceases, all the faculties cease. Subhūti, in just this way, when the bodhisattva great being trains in the Perfection of Wisdom, ignorance ceases and all non-virtuous factors cease, while all the perfections come to be gathered within and thoroughly grasped. Subhūti, therefore, the bodhisattva great being who wishes to thoroughly grasp all the perfections should train in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Subhūti, when the bodhisattva great being trains in the Perfection of Wisdom, he trains as the foremost of all sentient beings. Why is this? Because it is the very foremost of merit.

Subhūti, what do you think? However many sentient beings exist in the great trichiliocosm, however many are gathered by way of being reckoned as sentient beings—are all these many? Subhūti replied: Blessed One, even the sentient beings of Jambudvīpa alone are many—how much more so all the sentient beings of the great trichiliocosm! The Blessed One declared: Subhūti, if a single bodhisattva great being, for as long as he lived, remained and attended upon all those sentient beings with clothing, food, bedding, medicine for illness, requisites, and everything beneficial—Subhūti, what do you think? Would that bodhisattva great being generate much merit from that basis? Subhūti replied: Blessed One, much. Sugata, much. The Blessed One declared: Subhūti, if a bodhisattva great being were to meditate on this Perfection of Wisdom even for the briefest moment—even for the time it takes to snap one's fingers—he would generate far greater merit than that. Why is this? Subhūti, the meaning of the Perfection of Wisdom of the bodhisattva great being is so vast because the Perfection of Wisdom is that which brings the bodhisattva great being to the attainment of unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Subhūti, therefore, the bodhisattva great being who wishes to become fully and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, who wishes to become unsurpassed among all sentient beings, who wishes to become the protector of all beings without protector, who wishes to attain the domain of the buddhas, who wishes to attain the state of being foremost among the buddhas' herd, who wishes to delight in the buddhas' display, who wishes to roar the lion's roar of the buddhas, who wishes to attain the perfection of the buddhas, and who wishes to give discourse on the Dharma throughout the great trichiliocosm—such a bodhisattva great being should train in this Perfection of Wisdom.





Kauśika, moreover, it is possible to take the measure of a world system of four great continents, weighed out in units of measure, but Kauśika, it is not possible to take the measure of the merit of that generation of a mind endowed with rejoicing.

Kauśika, moreover, it is possible to take the measure of a small chiliocosm, weighed out in units of measure, but Kauśika, it is not possible to take the measure of the merit of that generation of a mind endowed with rejoicing.

Kauśika, moreover, it is possible to take the measure of a middling dichiliocosm, weighed out in units of measure, but Kauśika, it is not possible to take the measure of the merit of that generation of a mind endowed with rejoicing.

Kauśika, moreover, it is possible to take the measure of a great trichiliocosm, weighed out in units of measure, but Kauśika, it is not possible to take the measure of the merit of that son of noble family or daughter of noble family generating a mind endowed with rejoicing at the bodhisattva great being.

When this had been spoken, Śakra, lord of the gods, said this to the Blessed One: Blessed One, whatever sentient beings, upon hearing that the merit of generating a mind of rejoicing at bodhisattva great beings, from the very first generation of their mind up until their manifest and complete awakening into unsurpassed, perfect and complete buddhahood, is thus immeasurable, do not listen to it, do not wish for it, do not see it, and do not think to rejoice in it — those should be recognized as ones under the influence of māra.

Blessed One, whatever sentient beings do not rejoice in this generation of mind of the bodhisattva great beings have turned to the side of māra.

Blessed One, whatever sentient beings do not rejoice in this generation of mind of the bodhisattva great beings have died and transmigrated from the abode of māra.

Why is that? Blessed One, those who actually accomplish these generations of mind, and those who dedicate these generations of mind toward unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, or who rejoice in these generations of mind — they are the ones who destroy the abode of māra.

Blessed One, it is those bodhisattva great beings who have generated the mind set on unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening whose generations of mind should be rejoiced in.

Blessed One, I shall rejoice in this generation of mind of those sons of noble family or daughters of noble family who have not forsaken the Tathāgata, have not forsaken the Dharma, and have not forsaken the Saṅgha.

When this had been said, the Blessed One spoke thus to Śakra, lord of the gods: Kauśika, it is so, it is so. Kauśika, one should rejoice in these generations of mind of those sons of noble family or daughters of noble family who have not forsaken the Tathāgata, have not forsaken the Dharma, and have not forsaken the Saṅgha.

Kauśika, if those who follow the bodhisattva vehicle, or the pratyekabuddha vehicle, or the śrāvaka vehicle rejoice in this generation of mind, they will swiftly bring joy to the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas, and will not bring displeasure.

When this had been spoken, Śakra, lord of the gods, said this to the Blessed One:

The Blessed One said: It is so, it is so. Sugata, it is so. Son of noble family or daughter of noble family, whether followers of the bodhisattva vehicle, the pratyekabuddha vehicle, or the śrāvaka vehicle who rejoice in these generations of mind, they will most swiftly bring joy to the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas, and will not bring displeasure. And by means of those roots of virtue consisting in generations of mind accompanied by such rejoicing, wherever they come to be born, they will be honored there, they will be revered, esteemed, worshipped, adorned with offerings, and attended upon. They will never see forms that are unpleasant to the mind, never hear sounds that are unpleasant to the mind, never smell odors that are unpleasant to the mind, never taste flavors that are unpleasant to the mind, and never touch tangible objects that are unpleasant to the mind. It should be understood that they will not be born in the lower realms; it should be understood that they will be born in the higher realms. Why is this? Because those sons of noble family or daughters of noble family generate immeasurable and innumerable roots of virtue by rejoicing in that which brings happiness to all sentient beings.

Blessed One, whoever generates the aspiration and rejoices in the generation of mind of those individuals who follow the bodhisattva vehicle — for them too, that generation of mind will increase and cause them to attain unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. And having become fully awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, they will bring immeasurable and innumerable sentient beings to the complete transcendence of sorrow.

The Blessed One declared: Kauśika, by the power of the Tathāgata, what you have said is so, it is so. Kauśika, if a son of noble family or a daughter of noble family rejoices in the generation of mind of those individuals who follow the bodhisattva vehicle, then, Kauśika, by this method, that son or daughter of noble family, by rejoicing in the generation of mind of those who follow the bodhisattva vehicle, will come to rejoice in, generate, and accomplish the roots of virtue of immeasurable and innumerable sentient beings.

Subhūti said: Blessed One, how does a mind that is like an illusion come to fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening?

When this had been said, the Blessed One spoke thus to venerable Subhūti: Subhūti, what do you think — do you truly and correctly perceive mind as being like an illusion?

Subhūti said: Blessed One, that is not so.

The Blessed One said: Subhūti, what do you think — do you truly and correctly perceive illusion?

Subhūti said: Blessed One, that is not so. Blessed One, I do not truly and correctly perceive mind that is like an illusion, nor do I truly and correctly perceive illusion.

The Blessed One said: Subhūti, what do you think — since you do not truly and correctly perceive mind that is like an illusion, nor illusion itself, is there, apart from illusion or mind that is like an illusion, some other phenomenon that you truly and correctly perceive as the phenomenon by which one fully and completely awakens to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening?

Subhūti said:

Blessed One, that is not so. Blessed One, apart from illusion or mind that is like an illusion, I do not truly and correctly perceive any phenomenon by which one fully and completely awakens to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. Blessed One, since I do not truly and correctly perceive any such phenomenon apart from illusion or mind that is like an illusion, what phenomenon could I possibly declare to exist or not to exist? There is no way to apprehend that utterly isolated phenomenon as existing or as not existing, and it is not through any phenomenon that is utterly isolated that one fully and completely awakens to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. Why is that? Blessed One, it is not through a non-existent phenomenon that one fully and completely awakens to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. Blessed One, therefore the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly isolated, and that phenomenon which is utterly isolated is not something to be cultivated in meditation, since through it one neither attains nor abandons any phenomenon whatsoever. Blessed One, how then, in dependence on the Perfection of Wisdom, which is utterly isolated, could a bodhisattva great being fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening? Blessed One, unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening is likewise utterly isolated. Blessed One, since at the time when the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly isolated, unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening is also utterly isolated, how at that time could what is isolated fully and completely awaken to what is isolated?

When this had been said, the Blessed One spoke thus to venerable Subhūti: Excellent, Subhūti, excellent! Subhūti, it is just so, it is just so. Subhūti, the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly isolated, and unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening is likewise utterly isolated. Subhūti, it is precisely because the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly isolated that one fully and completely awakens to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, which is itself utterly isolated. Subhūti, if a bodhisattva great being correctly knows that the Perfection of Wisdom is utterly isolated, that is not the Perfection of Wisdom. Subhūti, it is in this way that a bodhisattva great being, in dependence on the Perfection of Wisdom, fully and completely awakens to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. Subhūti, yet there is also no such thing as fully and completely awakening to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening in dependence on the Perfection of Wisdom. It is not through what is isolated that one fully and completely awakens to what is isolated; nevertheless, a bodhisattva great being does fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and yet does not fully and completely awaken without depending on the Perfection of Wisdom.

Subhūti said: Blessed One, if I understand the meaning of what the Blessed One has taught, then, Blessed One, a bodhisattva great being practices in a profound domain of meaning.

The Blessed One said: Subhūti, it is just so, it is just so. Subhūti, a bodhisattva great being practices in a profound domain of meaning. Subhūti, when a bodhisattva great being practices even in that profound domain of meaning, and yet does not actualize that meaning as the level of a śrāvaka or the level of a pratyekabuddha, this is a difficult thing to accomplish.

Subhūti said: Blessed One, if I understand the meaning of what the Blessed One has taught, then a bodhisattva great being does not accomplish anything difficult at all. Why is that? Blessed One, it is thus: that very phenomenon which is being directly realized is itself not apprehended; that phenomenon which is to be directly realized is also not apprehended; and that by which one directly realizes that phenomenon is also not apprehended.

Blessed One, if, when this is taught in this way, a bodhisattva great being does not become disheartened, does not become discouraged, does not become disheartened at all, does not turn away in mind, does not become fearful, does not become terrified at all, and does not fall into complete terror, then he practices the Perfection of Wisdom. If he does not truly perceive, "I am practicing," then he practices the Perfection of Wisdom. If he does not, again and again, truly perceive, "I have drawn near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening," then he practices the Perfection of Wisdom. If he does not think, "I have left far behind the level of the śrāvaka and the level of the pratyekabuddha," then he practices the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, it is like this: space does not think, "I am near to some and far from others." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no conceptual thought in space. Blessed One, likewise, when a bodhisattva great being practices the Perfection of Wisdom, he does not think, "I have drawn near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and the level of the śrāvaka and the level of the pratyekabuddha are far from me." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no conceptual thought in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, it is like this: a magically created person does not think, "The magician is near to me, but this other assembled crowd of people is far from me." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no conceptual thought in a magically created person. Blessed One, likewise, when a bodhisattva great being practices the Perfection of Wisdom, he does not think, "I have drawn near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and the level of the śrāvaka and the level of the pratyekabuddha are far from me." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no conceptual thought in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, it is like this: a reflected image does not think, "That object in dependence upon which the reflection arises is near to me, but those who have not yet approached the mirror or the vessel filled with water are far from me." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no conceptual thought in a reflected image. Blessed One, likewise, when a bodhisattva great being practices the Perfection of Wisdom, he does not think, "I have drawn near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and the level of the śrāvaka and the level of the pratyekabuddha are far from me." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no conceptual thought in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, it is like this: for example, the Tathāgata has nothing that is pleasing or displeasing toward anyone. Why is that? Because the Tathāgata has abandoned all conception and discursive thought. Blessed One, likewise, when a bodhisattva great being practices the Perfection of Wisdom, he too has nothing that is pleasing or displeasing to anyone. Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no discursive thought in the Perfection of Wisdom. Blessed One, just as the Tathāgata has abandoned all conception and discursive thought, so too, Blessed One, the Perfection of Wisdom has abandoned all conception and discursive thought.

Blessed One, it is like this: for example, whatever emanation is emanated by a Tathāgata, arhat, perfect complete buddha does not think, "I have drawn near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and the level of the śrāvaka and the pratyekabuddha are far from me." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no discursive thought in an emanation. Blessed One, likewise, when a bodhisattva great being practices the Perfection of Wisdom, he too does not think, "I have drawn near to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, and the level of the śrāvaka and the level of the pratyekabuddha are far from me." Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no discursive thought in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, it is like this: for example, whatever action that emanation performs for the sake of performing some deed, that deed is indeed performed, yet that emanation has no discursive thought about it. Why is that? It is because there is no discursive thought in that emanation. Blessed One, likewise, the bodhisattva great being also, for the sake of performing whatever deed is to be performed, does perform the cultivation of this Perfection of Wisdom, and it is indeed performed, yet that Perfection of Wisdom has no discursive thought about it. Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no discursive thought in the Perfection of Wisdom.

Blessed One, it is like this: for example, when a skilled carpenter or a carpenter's apprentice constructs a wooden mechanical device fashioned in the form of a man or a woman and sets it in motion, then, although the deed for whose sake it was made is indeed performed, that assembly of wood has no discursive thought about it. Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no discursive thought in that assembly of wood. Blessed One, likewise, the bodhisattva great being also, for the sake of performing whatever deed is to be performed, does perform the cultivation of this Perfection of Wisdom, and it is indeed performed, yet that Perfection of Wisdom has no discursive thought about it. Why is that? Blessed One, it is because there is no discursive thought in the Perfection of Wisdom.

This concludes the twenty-sixth chapter, called "Like an Illusion," from the Noble Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines.

Then venerable Śāriputra said this to venerable Subhūti: "Venerable Subhūti, how wonderful it is that the bodhisattva great being who practices the Perfection of Wisdom practices what is truly essential." When this was said, venerable Subhūti said this to venerable Śāriputra:





Subhūti, such and such bodhisattva great beings should be recognized as irreversible from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. For them, no obstacle will arise from Māra or from others. Why is this? Subhūti, even if all the sentient beings whatsoever that exist in a great trichiliocosm of three thousand worlds were to become wicked Māras, and each and every one of those wicked Māras were to manifest that many hosts of Māra's forces, still, Subhūti, those wicked Māras would be unable to create an obstacle from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening for that bodhisattva great being who is heeded by the buddhas and who practices the perfection of wisdom.

Subhūti, moreover, not to speak of all the sentient beings of a great trichiliocosm of three thousand worlds becoming wicked Māras — Subhūti, even if all the sentient beings whatsoever that exist in as many great trichiliocosms of three thousand worlds as there are grains of sand in the Ganges River were to become wicked Māras, and each and every one of those wicked Māras were to manifest that many hosts of Māra's forces, still, Subhūti, those wicked Māras would be unable to create an obstacle from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening for that bodhisattva great being who is heeded by the buddhas and who practices the perfection of wisdom.

Subhūti, when a bodhisattva great being possesses two qualities, at that time the wicked Māras or the gods of Māra's retinue find it difficult to overpower him. What are the two? They are these: he has not forsaken any sentient being whatsoever, and he has realized that all phenomena are empty. Subhūti, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these two qualities is difficult for the wicked Māras or the gods of Māra's retinue to overpower.

Subhūti, when a bodhisattva great being possesses two other qualities, the wicked Māras or the gods of Māra's retinue find it difficult to overpower him. What are the two? They are these: he acts in accordance with what he has said, and he is heeded by the buddhas, the blessed ones. Subhūti, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these two qualities is difficult for the wicked Māras or the gods of Māra's retinue to overpower.

Subhūti, the gods too will think of approaching a bodhisattva great being who practices in this way. Having approached, they will think of inquiring after him fully, they will think of questioning him thoroughly, and they will think of paying him homage. They will also inspire in him joyful eagerness, saying: Son of noble family, you will swiftly and fully awaken to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening. Son of noble family, since this is so, you should abide by this very abiding, namely, the abiding of the perfection of wisdom. Why is this? Son of noble family, if you abide by this very abiding in which you now dwell, and practice accordingly, you will become a protector for sentient beings who have no protector; you will become a guardian for sentient beings who have no guardian; you will become a refuge for sentient beings who have no refuge; you will become a resting place for sentient beings who have no resting place; you will become a support for sentient beings who have no support; you will become an island for sentient beings who have no island; you will become a light for sentient beings who are blind; you will become a guide for sentient beings who have no guide; you will become a foundation for sentient beings who have no foundation.









Kauśika, therefore, sons of noble family or daughters of noble family who wish for the supreme excellence of all sentient beings, who wish for their excellent attainment, who wish for their attainment as the foremost, who wish for their perfect attainment, who wish for their supreme and perfect attainment, who wish for their marvelous attainment, who wish for their unexcelled attainment, who wish for their unsurpassed attainment, who wish for their attainment beyond which there is none higher, who wish for their unequaled attainment, and who wish for their attainment equal to the unequaled, should abide in this abiding place in which the bodhisattva great beings who practice and dwell in the Perfection of Wisdom abide.

This concludes the twenty-seventh chapter, called "The Chapter on the Essence," from the noble Eight Thousand Line Perfection of Wisdom.

The Perfection of Wisdom in Eight Thousand Lines, Bam po Twenty-Two.

Then at that time another god's son, together with the gods of the Heaven of the Thirty-Three, taking mandārava and great mandārava flowers, went to where the Blessed One was. At that time six hundred monks had gathered and were seated in that assembly. They too rose from their seats, arranged their upper robes over one shoulder, placed their right knee upon the ground, and, turning toward where the Blessed One was, joined their palms and paid homage. Through the power of the Buddha, the cupped hands of those monks became filled with mandārava and great mandārava flowers, and with those mandārava and great mandārava flowers they scattered, cast, and profusely strewed offerings upon the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, speaking these words: "Blessed One, we shall practice this Perfection of Wisdom. Blessed One, we shall abide in this unsurpassed abiding of the Perfection of Wisdom."

Then at that time the Blessed One smiled. This is the nature of things (dharmatā): whenever buddhas, blessed ones, smile, at that time rays of light of various colors and many hues—blue, yellow, red, white, madder, crystal, silver, and gold—issue forth from the mouth of the Blessed One. These illuminated the boundless, limitless, endless world systems everywhere, then rose upward as far as the world of Brahmā, and then returned, circled the Blessed One three times, and disappeared into the crown of the Blessed One's head.

Then the venerable Ānanda rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, placed his right knee upon the ground, and, turning toward where the Blessed One was, joined his palms, paid homage, and said this to the Blessed One: "Since the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, does not smile without cause or condition, Blessed One, what is the cause, what is the condition for this smile that the Blessed One has displayed?"

When he had said this, the Blessed One spoke thus to the venerable Ānanda: "Ānanda, in a future time, in an eon like a scattering of stars, these six hundred monks will fully and completely awaken to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, and having become perfectly awakened buddhas, they will teach the Dharma to sentient beings. They will all bear a single name, namely 'Flower-Strewn,' and as Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas and Teachers, they will appear in the world. Ānanda, moreover, the assemblies of śrāvaka Saṅgha of those Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas called Flower-Strewn will all be equal."



Ānanda, as for those who do not disparage this Perfection of Wisdom, who do not oppose it, who do not disturb it, who do not destroy it, who do not abandon it, who do not suppress it, and who do not intend to harm it, know that those bodhisattva great beings have performed service to the Victors (jinas) of the past. Ānanda, it is also thus: if the aspiration of a bodhisattva great being for unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening is never to be deceived, then it follows that a bodhisattva great being who has generated roots of virtue in the presence of the Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas, does not thereby bring about a maturation for the sake of śrāvakas or pratyekabuddhas. Rather, Ānanda, that bodhisattva great being is, for the most part, to be trained in the Perfection of Wisdom. Therefore, Ānanda, I entrust this Perfection of Wisdom to you, I hand it down to you, so that it may never disappear. Hold it, embrace it, recite it, master it completely, propagate it, and cause it to remain for a long time, so that not even a single syllable of it may be lost.

Ānanda, if you were to retain all the teachings that I have explained directly to you—apart from the Perfection of Wisdom—and later were to lose them, or set them aside, or forget them, Ānanda, you would not thereby be reckoned by me as having committed a fault. But Ānanda, it is thus: if you were to lose, set aside, or forget even a single phrase of speech endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom, Ānanda, you would thereby have committed a fault against me, and you would not have pleased my mind. Ānanda, furthermore, if, having taken up the Perfection of Wisdom, you were later to lose it, set it aside, and forget it, you would not have honored me, not have revered me, not have esteemed me, not have made offerings to me, not have paid me homage, and not have served me. Ānanda, you would likewise not have honored, revered, esteemed, made offerings to, paid homage to, or served the Buddhas, the Blessed Ones, of the past, future, and present.

Ānanda, if, having taken up the Perfection of Wisdom, you were later to lose it, set it aside, and forget it, Ānanda, you would thereby have committed a fault against me, and you would not have pleased my mind. Why is this? Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is the mother who gives birth to and brings forth the Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas of the past, future, and present; it is that which brings about the attainment of omniscience itself—so it has been declared by the Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfectly complete Buddhas.

Ānanda, therefore I entrust this Perfection of Wisdom to you, I hand it down to you, so that it may never disappear. Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is to be taken up. Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is to be held. Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is to be recited. Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is to be mastered completely. Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is to be proclaimed. Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is to be taught. Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is to be explained in detail.



By cultivating it, one comes to fully embrace, in their wake, the awakening of the buddhas, the blessed ones, of the past, the future, and the present. Ānanda, if anyone, at the time when this Perfection of Wisdom is on the verge of disappearing, embraces it in its wake, that person will thereby come to fully embrace, in their wake, the awakening of the buddhas, the blessed ones, of the past, the future, and the present.

Why is this so? Ānanda, the awakening of the buddhas, the blessed ones, is born from the Perfection of Wisdom. Ānanda, whatever supreme, perfect awakening belonged to those tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly awakened buddhas, the blessed ones, who arose in the past, that too, Ānanda, was born from the Perfection of Wisdom. Ānanda, whatever supreme, perfect awakening will belong to those tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly awakened buddhas, the blessed ones, who will arise in the future, that too, Ānanda, will be born from the Perfection of Wisdom. Ānanda, whatever supreme, perfect awakening belongs to those tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly awakened buddhas, the blessed ones, who now dwell, live, and remain, presently, in all the immeasurable and countless world systems, that too, Ānanda, is born from this very Perfection of Wisdom. Ānanda, my own supreme, perfect awakening was likewise born from this very Perfection of Wisdom.

Ānanda, therefore, bodhisattva great beings who wish to fully and completely awaken to supreme, perfect awakening, and who wish to train in the six perfections, should listen to this very Perfection of Wisdom, should take hold of it, bear it in mind, read it, master it fully, recite it aloud, teach it, explain it thoroughly, transmit it, chant it, write it down, and train in this very Perfection of Wisdom, making it their practice.

Why is this so? Ānanda, this Perfection of Wisdom is the mother of the bodhisattva great beings; it is what gives birth to them and nurtures them. Ānanda, whatever bodhisattva great beings, having trained in the six perfections, have definitely emerged into supreme, perfect awakening, or will definitely emerge into it, or are definitely emerging into it now, all of them, Ānanda, have trained in the six perfections in dependence upon the Perfection of Wisdom. All of them were born from the six perfections in dependence upon this very Perfection of Wisdom.

Why is this so? Ānanda, all the perfections are born from the Perfection of Wisdom, and it is this that brings about the attainment of supreme, perfect awakening. Ānanda, therefore, so that this Perfection of Wisdom may never disappear, I entrust it to you, I hand it over to you — this I say twice.

Ānanda, it is like this: this Perfection of Wisdom is the inexhaustible treasury of Dharma of the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly awakened buddhas. Why is this so? Ānanda, throughout the beginningless cycle of saṃsāra in past time, whatever Dharma has been taught to sentient beings by the buddhas, the blessed ones — all of that was taught from this very treasury of Dharma, the Perfection of Wisdom.

Ānanda, whatever buddhas, blessed ones, will appear in the immeasurable cycles of future time, who, having become fully and completely awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, will teach the Dharma to sentient beings — those buddhas, blessed ones, too, will teach it from this very treasury of Dharma, the Perfection of Wisdom. Ānanda, whatever buddhas, blessed ones, are now dwelling, living, and abiding in the immeasurable, innumerable world systems of the present, teaching the Dharma — those buddhas, blessed ones, too, Ānanda, will bring it forth from this very treasury of Dharma, the Perfection of Wisdom. Ānanda, therefore this treasury of the Perfection of Wisdom is the inexhaustible treasury of Dharma.

Ānanda, suppose you were to teach the Dharma of the level of the śrāvakas to persons of the śrāvaka vehicle, and that, upon your teaching that Dharma, all the sentient beings existing throughout the great trichiliocosm were to actually become arhats — even so, you, teaching the Dharma by turning and repeatedly turning the wheel of Dharma of my śrāvakas, would not thereby have accomplished the deed of a śrāvaka. Ānanda, but if you were to teach or fully explain to a bodhisattva, a great being, even a single Dharma teaching endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom, then, by teaching the Dharma through turning and repeatedly turning the wheel of Dharma of my śrāvakas, you would please me — but not by that former teaching of the Dharma.

If someone were to cause all the sentient beings existing, however many there are, throughout the great trichiliocosm to attain arhatship, Ānanda, what do you think — would the accumulated merit arising from the meritorious act of the generosity of those arhats, together with the meritorious act arising from moral discipline, and the meritorious act arising from meditation, be a great mass of merit?

Ānanda said: It would be great, Blessed One. It would be great, Sugata (Well-Gone One).

The Blessed One said: Ānanda, greater than the merit of all those persons of the śrāvaka vehicle is the merit generated by a bodhisattva, a great being, who teaches the Dharma endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom. Ānanda, greater still than that is the merit generated by a bodhisattva, a great being, who teaches the Dharma endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom to another bodhisattva, a great being, even if only for the small duration of a single day. Ānanda, let alone a single day — even if only for the small duration of a morning; Ānanda, let alone teaching the Dharma for a morning — even if only for the small duration of a single hour; Ānanda, let alone a single hour — even if only for the small duration of a mere instant; Ānanda, let alone a mere instant — even for the duration of a single breath; Ānanda, let alone a single breath — even for the small duration of a single moment: Ānanda, whatever bodhisattva, great being, teaches the Dharma endowed with the Perfection of Wisdom to another bodhisattva, great being, for even a single moment, a single breath, or a mere instant — this generates far greater merit than that. Ānanda, by this gift of Dharma from that bodhisattva, great being, the roots of virtue of all persons of the śrāvaka and pratyekabuddha vehicles are utterly outshone.







Subhūti, thus the bodhisattva great being views dependent origination as without extremes and without a middle. Subhūti, this way of viewing dependent origination is the unmixed quality present in the essence of awakening for the bodhisattva great being. Subhūti, whatever bodhisattva great being views dependent origination in this way will attain the wisdom of omniscience.

Subhūti, if any bodhisattva great being, through this actualization of the inexhaustible, practices the Perfection of Wisdom and views dependent origination, that one will not remain on the level of a śrāvaka or the level of a pratyekabuddha, but will instead abide in omniscience itself.

Subhūti, all those bodhisattva great beings who turn back from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening do so because they do not rely upon this mental engagement and this skillful means, and thus they do not understand.

How does the bodhisattva great being, by practicing the Perfection of Wisdom through the actualization of the inexhaustible, actualize the Perfection of Wisdom? And how, through actualizing the inexhaustible, does he view dependent origination in relation to the Perfection of Wisdom? Subhūti, all those bodhisattva great beings who have turned back, are turning back, and will turn back from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening do so, turned back, are turning back, and will turn back, because they do not rely upon this skillful means. Subhūti, all those bodhisattva great beings who have not turned back, do not turn back, and will not turn back from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening do so, have not turned back, do not turn back, and will not turn back, because they rely upon this very Perfection of Wisdom.

Thus should the bodhisattva great being, by practicing the Perfection of Wisdom, actualize the inexhaustible and thereby actualize the Perfection of Wisdom. Having thus actualized the inexhaustible, he views dependent origination in relation to the Perfection of Wisdom.

Subhūti, moreover, when the bodhisattva great being views dependent origination in this way, he does not perceive any phenomenon whatsoever as arising without a cause. He does not perceive any phenomenon whatsoever as a phenomenon possessing permanence, stability, eternality, or changelessness. He does not perceive any phenomenon whatsoever as an agent or an experiencer. Subhūti, this is how the bodhisattva great being, actualizing the inexhaustible with respect to the Perfection of Wisdom, actualizes it, practices the Perfection of Wisdom, and views dependent origination.

Subhūti, at the time when the bodhisattva great being actualizes the inexhaustible with respect to the Perfection of Wisdom and, in actualizing it, views dependent origination, at that time, Subhūti, the bodhisattva great being does not perceive form. Likewise he does not perceive feeling, does not perceive perception, does not perceive formations, does not perceive consciousness, and does not perceive ignorance.

Likewise he does not perceive formations, does not perceive consciousness, does not perceive name-and-form, does not perceive the six sense sources, does not perceive contact, does not perceive feeling, does not perceive craving, does not perceive grasping, does not perceive becoming, does not perceive birth, does not perceive aging and death, and does not truly perceive, again and again, sorrow, lamentation, suffering, unhappiness, and disturbance. He does not truly perceive, again and again, thinking "this is a buddha-field," nor does he truly perceive thinking "that is a different buddha-field," nor does he truly perceive any phenomenon by which one would truly perceive "this buddha-field or that other one." He does not truly perceive, again and again, that phenomenon either. Subhūti, this is the Perfection of Wisdom of the bodhisattva great beings.

Subhūti, at the time when the bodhisattva great being practices the Perfection of Wisdom, at that time the evil Māra becomes afflicted with intense sorrow's dart. Subhūti, it is like this: just as a person becomes afflicted with intense sorrow's dart when his parents have died, so too, Subhūti, at the time when the bodhisattva great being practices the Perfection of Wisdom, at that time the evil Māra likewise becomes afflicted with intense sorrow's dart.

Subhūti asked, "Blessed One, does a single evil Māra become afflicted with intense sorrow's dart, or do many evil Māras become afflicted with intense sorrow's dart, or do all the evil Māras that exist in the great trichiliocosm, the world system of three thousand [worlds], at that time become afflicted with intense sorrow's dart?"

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One said this to venerable Subhūti: "Subhūti, at the time when the bodhisattva great being dwells abiding in the Perfection of Wisdom, at that time all the evil Māras that exist in the great trichiliocosm, the world system of three thousand worlds, become afflicted with intense sorrow's dart, and each one becomes unhappy upon his own seat. Why is this? Because when the bodhisattva great being dwells abiding in the Perfection of Wisdom, the world together with its gods, humans, and asuras finds no opportunity, gains no foothold, for the thought 'let us seize hold of him and strike him down,' nor for the thought 'let us turn him back from unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.'

Subhūti, therefore, the bodhisattva great being who wishes to become fully awakened to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening should practice the Perfection of Wisdom. Why is this? Subhūti, because through the bodhisattva great being's practice of the Perfection of Wisdom, the cultivation of the perfection of generosity becomes complete. Likewise, the cultivation of the perfection of moral discipline, the perfection of patience, the perfection of diligence, and the perfection of meditative concentration becomes complete. Subhūti, through the bodhisattva great being's practice of the Perfection of Wisdom, the cultivation of all six perfections becomes complete, and the cultivation of all skillful means becomes complete."





Because it is without joy and without separation from joy, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly. Because it is without attachment and without separation from attachment, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly. Why is this? Subhāpūti, form, by its very nature endowed with reality, neither generates attachment nor generates separation from attachment. Subhūti, likewise feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness, by their very nature endowed with reality, neither generate attachment nor generate separation from attachment. Because they are utterly pure by nature, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly.

Because all phenomena are free from clinging to attachment and non-attachment, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly. Through fully comprehending the wisdom of the buddhas, one understands that all phenomena are awakening; thus the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly. Because all phenomena are empty, signless, and wishless, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly. Preceded by great loving-kindness, one understands that all phenomena are like medicine; thus the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly.

Because all phenomena abide in loving-kindness, abide in compassion, abide in joy, and abide in equanimity, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly. Because all phenomena, having become like Brahmā, do not generate aversion and do not take up any aversion at all, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly. Because all phenomena are without wishing and without anger, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood accordingly.

Because the ocean is without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Because space is without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Because Mount Sumeru is manifold, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as manifold.

Because form is without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Likewise, because feeling, perception, formations, and consciousness are without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Because the orb of the sun's rays shines without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Because all sounds are without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit.

Because the perfect accomplishment of all the qualities of a buddha is without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Because the accumulation of merit and wisdom for the entire realm of sentient beings is without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit.

Because the element of earth is without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Likewise, because the element of water, the element of fire, the element of wind, the element of space, and the element of consciousness are without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit.

Because the accumulation of virtuous and non-virtuous phenomena is measureless, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as measureless. Because the accumulation of all phenomena is measureless, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as measureless.

Because the attainment of meditative absorption in all phenomena is without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Because all the qualities of a buddha are without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit. Because all phenomena are without limit, the Perfection of Wisdom should be understood as without limit.































Chapter Twenty-Four.

Then the girl's parents said this to the girl: "Child of noble family, from the difficult trials you have described, it is certain that those teachings are truly inconceivable, surpassing all worlds; it is marvelous that they bring happiness to all sentient beings. Child of noble family, since you delight in such difficult trials for the sake of those beings, we grant you our permission. Daughter, we too will go together with you to see, pay homage to, attend upon, and make offerings to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata; open the way for us to go."

Then the merchant's daughter, in order to make offerings and pay homage to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata, made ready, and having informed her own parents, said: "Father and Mother, please do just as you have said, and I will not create any obstacle to any course of virtue whatsoever." Having said this, the merchant's daughter made ready to go and make offerings and pay homage to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata.

Then that merchant's daughter adorned five hundred chariots with ornaments, and likewise adorned those five hundred girls with ornaments. Having adorned them with ornaments, they took up various flowers of different colors and clothes of various colors; they took up incense, perfume, garlands, unguents, powders, monastic robes, parasols, banners, bells, and pennants; they took up various kinds of jewels of different sorts, and also flowers whose very substance consisted of various kinds of jewels of different sorts; and the parents likewise took up abundant food, drink, and delicacies. Then, entering together into a single chariot with the bodhisattva mahāsattva Sadāprarudita ("Ever-Weeping"), surrounded and led forth by those five hundred girls who had entered the five hundred chariots, together with the parents and a great retinue besides, they set out toward the east, proceeding gradually onward.

The bodhisattva mahāsattva Sadāprarudita, from far off, beheld the city of Gandhavatī ("Fragr

Then, having descended from that chariot, the merchant's daughter and those five hundred girls likewise descended from their chariots together with him. Then the bodhisattva mahāsattva Sadāprarudita, surrounded on all sides by the five hundred girls headed by the merchant's daughter, with them going before him, and with an immeasurable array of offerings, set out to where the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata was.

Now at that time the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata had caused to be built a multi-tiered pavilion for the Perfection of Wisdom, made of the nature of seven kinds of precious substances, adorned with red sandalwood, and draped with a net of pearls. At the four corners of that pavilion precious jewels had been placed, and these served the function of lamps. Four incense vessels made of silver were also hung in the four directions, and, in order to make offering to the Perfection of Wisdom, they were fumigated with black aloeswood alone. In the center of that pavilion four thrones made of the seven kinds of precious substances had been set up, and upon them four precious caskets had been placed; inside these had been set the Perfection of Wisdom, written in molten lapis lazuli upon leaves of gold. That pavilion was further adorned with hanging silk streamers of many different colors and kinds.

Then the bodhisattva mahāsattva Sadāprarudita, together with the five hundred girls headed by the merchant's daughter, beheld that pavilion adorned with an immeasurable array of offerings, and there he also saw many thousands of gods. He saw Śakra, lord of the gods, together with those many thousands of gods, scattering, casting forth, and thoroughly showering upon that pavilion divine māndārava flowers, divine sandalwood powder, divine gold powder, and divine silver powder; and he also heard divine cymbals sounding.

Having seen and heard this, the bodhisattva mahāsattva Sadāprarudita said to Śakra, lord of the gods: "Lord of the gods, why do you, together with these many thousands of gods, scatter, cast forth, and thoroughly shower upon this precious pavilion divine māndārava flowers, divine sandalwood powder, divine gold powder, and divine silver powder, and why do the gods strike these divine cymbals in the sky?"

When he had said this, Ś

Together with the five hundred maidens, those gods, carrying clusters of flowers and garlands, precious cloth, incense, perfume, garlands, ointment, powder, religious robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, banners, and flowers wrought of gold and silver, came in that great assembly and made offerings to the Perfection of Wisdom—that is to say, they set aside more and more of these offerings separately, in order to offer them also to the noble bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata.

Then the bodhisattva mahāsattva Sadāprarudita, together with the merchant's daughter and the other five hundred maidens, having first made offerings to the Perfection of Wisdom with flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointment, powder, religious robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, banners, sandalwood powder, flowers wrought of gold and silver, and divine music, went to where the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata was. Having arrived there, precisely in order to make offering to the Dharma, they scattered, cast forth, and thoroughly showered upon the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata flowers, incense, perfume, garlands, ointment, powder, religious robes, parasols, victory banners, bells, banners, sandalwood powder, and flowers wrought of gold and silver. They also struck up divine cymbals in proper accord.

Then those flowers formed themselves into a tiered pavilion of blossoms above the head of the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata. The flowers of gold and silver and the flowers of many colors hung in the sky like a celestial mansion. His religious robes and precious cloths likewise hung in the intermediate space like a maṇḍala of clouds of various jewels.

When the bodhisattva mahāsattva Sadāprarudita, together with the merchant's daughter and the other five hundred maidens, saw this miraculous display of the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata, they were filled with wonder and thought: "Since this bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata possesses such great miraculous power, such great might, and such great splendor—since even while still practicing the conduct of a bodhisattva he displays such marvelous transformations as these—what then will he be like when he fully and completely awakens to unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening?"

Then the merchant's daughter and the other five hundred maidens, their joy toward the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata thus aroused, together with the entire assembly and with a superior intention, generated the mind set upon unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening, saying: "By this root of virtue, may we in a future time become Tathāgatas, Arhats, perfect and complete Buddhas. May we too, while practicing the conduct of bodhisattvas, attain such qualities as those attained by the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata. Just as we honor and revere the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata, so may we come to honor and revere the Perfection of Wisdom. And just as the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata teaches the Dharma perfectly, so may we too come to teach it perfectly to many beings."









It trembled, trembled greatly, and trembled thoroughly on every side. It roared, roared greatly, and roared thoroughly on every side. All the abodes of Māra were shaken and overturned. In the world system of the great trichiliocosm, whatever grasses, shrubs, medicinal plants, and forests there were, all of them bowed down in the direction where the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata was. Flowers out of season scattered down from the sky above, and great showers of flowers fell as well. Śakra, lord of the gods, and the Four Great Kings scattered, cast down, and thoroughly strewed divine sandalwood powder and divine flowers toward the bodhisattva mahāsattva Dharmodgata and the bod







"Child of noble family, take these divine māndārava flowers and scatter them upon this ground.
